12351 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
Four verses were revealed concerning me.
I acquired a sword and brought it to the Prophet ﷺ, saying:
“O Messenger of Allah, grant it to me as an additional share.”
He replied:
“Put it down.”
Saʿd then stood to leave, but the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Put it back where you took it from.”
He returned and said:
“Grant it to me, O Messenger of Allah.”
He replied:
“Put it down.”
Saʿd again said:
“O Messenger of Allah, grant it to me. Am I to be treated like someone who rendered no service?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Put it back where you took it from.”
Saʿd said:
The following verse was then revealed:
“They ask you concerning the spoils of war. Say: The spoils belong to Allah and the Messenger.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1748:34).

12352 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
On the day of Badr, I brought a sword to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah has brought satisfaction to my heart against the enemy today, so grant me this sword.”
He replied:
“This sword belongs neither to me nor to you.”
I went away saying to myself:
“Today it will be given to someone who did not fight as I fought!”
While I was still there, a messenger came to me and said:
“Respond to the Messenger of Allah.”
I feared that something had been revealed concerning what I had said.
I came to him, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
“You asked me for this sword, but it belonged neither to me nor to you. Allah has now placed it under my authority, so it is yours.”
He then recited:
“They ask you concerning the spoils of war. Say: The spoils belong to Allah and the Messenger,”
until the end of the verse.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2740), whose wording is cited, al-Tirmidhī (3079), Aḥmad (1538), and al-Ḥākim (2/132).
Abū Dāwūd said after narrating the hadith (2740):
“The recitation of Ibn Masʿūd was: ‘They ask you concerning the additional spoil.’”
It has been said that the words of Allah:
“Say: The spoils belong to Allah and the Messenger”
were abrogated by His words:
“Know that whatever spoils you acquire, one-fifth belongs to Allah and the Messenger.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:41]
At first, the spoils were entirely placed under the authority of the Prophet ﷺ. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then replaced that ruling with the allocation of one-fifth.

12353 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The inhabitants of Paradise will see the people of the lofty chambers above them just as you see a brilliant star remaining on the eastern or western horizon, because of the difference in rank between them.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are those the dwellings of the prophets, which no one besides them can attain?”
He replied:
“No. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, they are men who believed in Allah and affirmed the truthfulness of the messengers.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3256) and Muslim (2831).

12354 — Abū Ayyūb said:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“When Allah promised you that one of the two groups would be yours.”
When He promised that one of the two groups would belong to us, our hearts became reassured.
The two groups were the caravan of Abū Sufyān or the army of Quraysh.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/47) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8817).
The words:
“The unarmed group”
refer to the caravan, which did not involve battle.
The word:
“Might”
means strength and severity; it may also refer to weapons.
The words:
“Allah intended to establish the truth by His words”
mean that events occurred exactly as Allah, Glorified and Exalted, intended.
This occurred at the Battle of Badr in the second year after the Hijrah, on Friday morning, the seventeenth of Ramaḍān.

12355 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
On the day of Badr, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at the polytheists, who numbered one thousand, while his Companions numbered three hundred and nineteen.
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ faced the direction of prayer, stretched out his hands, and began earnestly calling upon his Lord:
“O Allah, fulfill for me what You promised me. O Allah, grant me what You promised me. O Allah, if this small group of Muslims is destroyed, You will no longer be worshipped upon the earth.”
He continued calling upon his Lord with his hands extended while facing the direction of prayer until his cloak fell from his shoulders.
Abū Bakr came to him, picked up his cloak, placed it over his shoulders, embraced him from behind, and said:
“Prophet of Allah, your appeal to your Lord is sufficient. He will certainly fulfill what He promised you.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“When you sought help from your Lord, He answered you: ‘I will reinforce you with one thousand angels following one another.’”
Allah therefore reinforced him with the angels.
Abū Zumayl said:
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated to me:
While a Muslim man that day was pursuing a polytheist in front of him, he suddenly heard the sound of a whip striking above him and the voice of a horseman saying:
“Advance, Ḥayzūm!”
He looked at the polytheist in front of him and saw him fall flat on his back.
When he examined him, he found that his nose had been struck and his face split open as though by the blow of a whip, and the entire wound had turned greenish.
The Anṣārī came and informed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who said:
“You have spoken the truth. That was assistance from the third heaven.”
On that day, they killed seventy and captured seventy prisoners.
Abū Zumayl said that Ibn ʿAbbās continued:
When they took the prisoners captive, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked Abū Bakr and ʿUmar:
“What is your opinion concerning these prisoners?”
Abū Bakr said:
“Prophet of Allah, they are our cousins and members of our clans. I believe that you should accept ransom from them, so that it may strengthen us against the disbelievers. Perhaps Allah will guide them to Islam.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What is your opinion, Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb?”
I said:
“No, by Allah, O Messenger of Allah, I do not share Abū Bakr’s opinion. I believe that you should hand them over to us so that we may strike their necks. Hand ʿAqīl over to ʿAlī so that he may strike his neck, and hand a certain relative of mine over to me so that I may strike his neck. These are the leaders and champions of disbelief.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ preferred what Abū Bakr had said and did not prefer my opinion.
The following morning, I came and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr sitting and weeping.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell me why you and your companion are weeping. If I find reason to weep, I will weep; and if I do not, I will force myself to weep because of your weeping.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I am weeping because of what your companions proposed to me concerning accepting ransom. Their punishment was shown to me closer than this tree.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land…”
until His words:
“So eat from what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good, and fear Allah. Indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:67–69]
Allah then made the spoils lawful for them.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1763).

12356 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was inside a shelter on the day of Badr, he said:
“O Allah, I appeal to You by Your covenant and Your promise. O Allah, if You will, You will not be worshipped after this day.”
Abū Bakr took him by the hand and said:
“That is sufficient, O Messenger of Allah. You have pleaded persistently with your Lord.”
The Prophet ﷺ sprang up while wearing his armor, came out, and recited:
“The assembled forces will be defeated, and they will turn their backs in flight.”
 [Sūrat al-Qamar 54:45]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4875).

12357 — Rifāʿah ibn Rāfiʿ al-Zuraqī narrated:
Gabriel came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“How do you regard those among you who participated in Badr?”
He replied:
“Among the best Muslims,”
or words to that effect.
Gabriel said:
“The same is true of the angels who participated in Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3992).

12358 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said on the day of Badr:
“This is Gabriel holding the head of his horse, wearing the equipment of war.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3995).
The words:
“I will reinforce you with one thousand angels following one another”
mean that one angel followed behind another, as Ibn ʿAbbās explained.

12359 — ʿAlī narrated:
There was no horseman among us on the day of Badr except al-Miqdād.
I saw that everyone among us was asleep except the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who remained beneath a tree praying and weeping until morning.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1023), Abū Yaʿlā (280), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (899) and Ibn Ḥibbān (2257).

12360 — Abū Ṭalḥah narrated:
Drowsiness overcame us while we were standing in our battle lines on the day of Badr.
Abū Ṭalḥah said:
I was among those overcome by drowsiness that day. My sword repeatedly fell from my hand, and I would pick it up; then it would fall again, and I would pick it up.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (16357).
However, al-Bukhārī narrated it (4068, 4562) with the wording:
“On the day of Uḥud.”
The scholars concluded that drowsiness occurred at Badr, as Allah says in Sūrat al-Anfāl:
“When He caused drowsiness to overcome you as security from Him and sent down rain upon you from the sky to purify you with it, remove Satan’s defilement from you, strengthen your hearts, and make your feet firm.”
It also occurred at Uḥud, as Allah says in Sūrat Āl ʿImrān:
“Then, after distress, He sent down security upon you—a drowsiness that overcame a group among you.”
 [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:154]
The wisdom behind causing drowsiness to overcome the believers was to make their hearts feel safe and free from fear, because a terrified person is not normally overcome by sleep.
The words:
“He sent down rain upon you from the sky to purify you with it”
mean purification from both ritual impurity and physical filth, since some of them may have been in a state of minor ritual impurity and others in a state requiring the major ritual bath.
Allah intended to purify them outwardly and to cleanse them inwardly from Satan’s whisperings and defilement.

12361 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated, within the account of the Battle of Badr:
“During the night, a light shower of rain fell upon us. We went beneath the trees and shields to shelter from the rain…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (948).
The rain was sent down for two reasons. The first was that the ground was sandy, so Allah sent rain upon it to make the believers’ feet firm. The second was:
Satan had whispered to them that some among them had not purified themselves from minor or major ritual impurity. The Muslims therefore washed themselves and became purified both outwardly and inwardly. In this way, Allah removed Satan’s defilement from them.

12362 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Avoid the seven destructive sins.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what are they?”
He replied:
“Associating partners with Allah, sorcery, killing a soul whom Allah has forbidden to be killed except with legal right, consuming usury, consuming an orphan’s property, fleeing on the day the armies advance against one another, and falsely accusing chaste, believing, unsuspecting women of adultery.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2766) and Muslim (89).

12363 — Abū Saʿīd said:
The following verse was revealed concerning the day of Badr:
“Whoever turns his back to them on that day…”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2648), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11139, 11140), and al-Ḥākim (2/327).
The words:
“To join another group”
refer in this context to joining the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, because the verse was revealed concerning Badr and the believers had no other supporting group besides the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
After that, however, Muslims became supporting groups for one another.
Therefore, its ruling remains applicable to all believers. Allah has forbidden the believers, when they meet the enemy, from turning their backs in flight, except as a maneuver for battle or to join another body of believers wherever they may be. There is therefore no need to claim that the verse was abrogated.
The fact that the verse was revealed concerning the day of Badr does not mean that fleeing from battle is forbidden only for those who participated in Badr. It has been authentically established in the hadith of Abū Hurayrah that fleeing from battle is one of the destructive sins.

12364 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
On the day of Badr, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hand and said:
“My Lord, if this group is destroyed, You will never again be worshipped upon the earth.”
Gabriel, peace be upon him, said to him:
“Take a handful of dust.”
He took a handful of dust and threw it into their faces. There was not a single polytheist whose eyes, nostrils, and mouth were not struck by some of the dust from that handful, and they turned and fled.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/86) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1673).

12365 — Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām narrated:
On the day of Badr, we heard a sound descending from the sky to the earth, like the sound of a pebble falling into a metal basin. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ threw those pebbles, and the enemy was defeated.
That is the meaning of the words of Allah, the Exalted:
“You did not throw when you threw, but Allah threw, so that He might test the believers with a good trial from Himself.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (3/227) and al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/84).
The words:
“You did not throw when you threw”
mean that you did not cause the dust to reach them by your own power when you threw it. Rather, Allah caused it to reach them and strike the faces of the disbelieving army, until none of them remained without being struck by some of it.

12366 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Thaʿlabah ibn Ṣuʿayr narrated that when the two armies met, Abū Jahl said:
“O Allah, whichever of us has severed family ties and brought something unknown and unacceptable, destroy him this morning.”
Thus, Abū Jahl was the one who sought Allah’s judgment and victory.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23661), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11137), and al-Ḥākim (2/328).
The expression:
“Destroy him”
comes from the Arabic phrase meaning:
“May Allah bring about his destruction.”

12367 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf narrated:
While I was standing in the battle line on the day of Badr, I looked to my right and left and found myself between two young boys from the Anṣār. I wished that I had been standing between two men stronger than they appeared to be.
One of them nudged me and asked:
“Uncle, do you know Abū Jahl?”
I replied:
“Yes. What do you want with him, my nephew?”
He said:
“I have been informed that he insults the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if I see him, my body will not separate from his until the first of us to die has died.”
I was amazed by this. Then the other boy nudged me and said the same thing.
Before long, I saw Abū Jahl moving among the people and said:
“Do you not see him? That is the man you were asking about.”
They rushed toward him and struck him with their swords until they killed him. They then returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him.
He asked:
“Which of you killed him?”
Each of them replied:
“I killed him.”
He asked:
“Have you wiped your swords clean?”
They replied:
“No.”
He examined both swords and said:
“Both of you killed him.”
He awarded Abū Jahl’s personal battle possessions to Muʿādh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ.
The two men were Muʿādh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ and Muʿādh ibn ʿAfrāʾ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3141) and Muslim (1752:42).

12368 — Khabbāb ibn al-Aratt narrated:
We complained to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was reclining upon his cloak in the shade of the Kaʿbah. We said:
“Will you not seek victory for us? Will you not supplicate to Allah for us?”
He replied:
“Among those who came before you, a pit would be dug for a man and he would be placed within it. A saw would then be brought and placed upon his head, and he would be cut into two halves, yet that would not turn him away from his religion. His flesh would be combed with iron combs down to the bones and sinews, yet that would not turn him away from his religion.
By Allah, this matter will certainly be completed until a rider travels from Ṣanʿāʾ to Ḥaḍramawt fearing none except Allah, or fearing a wolf for his sheep. But you are being impatient.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3612).

12369 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“Indeed, the worst of creatures in the sight of Allah are the deaf and mute who do not understand”:
“They were a group from Banū ʿAbd al-Dār.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4646).

12370 — Abū Saʿīd ibn al-Muʿallā narrated:
I was praying in the mosque when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called me, but I did not answer him.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I was praying.”
He replied:
“Did Allah not say: ‘O you who believe, respond to Allah and the Messenger when he calls you to that which gives you life’?”
He then said to me:
“I will teach you a sūrah that is the greatest sūrah in the Qur’an before you leave the mosque.”
He took me by the hand. When he was about to leave, I said:
“Did you not say that you would teach me the greatest sūrah in the Qur’an?”
He replied:
“‘All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds.’ It is the seven oft-repeated verses and the magnificent Qur’an that I have been given.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4474).

12371 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“All the hearts of the children of Adam are between two fingers of the Most Merciful, like a single heart, which He directs however He wills.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“O Allah, Director of the hearts, direct our hearts toward obedience to You.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2654).

12372 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The example of those who uphold the limits established by Allah and those who violate them is like a group of people who drew lots for places aboard a ship. Some received the upper part and others the lower part.
Whenever those in the lower part needed water, they had to pass by those above them. They said, ‘Why do we not make a hole in our own section so that we do not inconvenience those above us?’
If those above leave them to do what they intend, they will all perish. But if they restrain them, they will save them and save themselves as well.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2493).

12373 — Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim narrated:
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq recited the verse:
“O you who believe, take care of yourselves. Those who go astray cannot harm you when you are rightly guided.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:105]
He then said:
“People are applying this verse in the wrong place. I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘When people see an oppressor and do not restrain him—or when they see wrongdoing and do not change it—Allah will encompass them all with His punishment.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4338), al-Tirmidhī (2168, 3057), Ibn Mājah (4005), and Aḥmad (1).

12374 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The leaders of Quraysh gathered in al-Ḥijr and pledged by al-Lāt, al-ʿUzzā, Manāt—the third of the three—Nāʾilah, and Isāf:
“If we see Muḥammad, we will all rise against him as one man and will not leave him until we kill him.”
His daughter Fāṭimah came weeping and entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. She said:
“The leaders of Quraysh have pledged against you that if they see you, they will all rise and kill you. Every one of them already knows the share he will take in shedding your blood.”
He said:
“My daughter, bring me water for ablution.”
He performed ablution and then entered the mosque where they were gathered.
When they saw him, they said:
“There he is.”
But they lowered their eyes, their chins dropped toward their chests, and they became motionless in their places. None of them raised his eyes toward him, and not one man stood against him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ approached until he stood over them. He took a handful of dust and said:
“May these faces be disgraced.”
He then threw the dust upon them. No man among them was struck by a pebble from that handful except that he was later killed at Badr as a disbeliever.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2672), Ibn Ḥibbān (6502), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (1/163).

12375 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A group of Quraysh, consisting of noblemen from every clan, gathered to enter Dār al-Nadwah. Iblīs met them in the form of a dignified old man.
When they saw him, they asked:
“Who are you?”
He replied:
“An old man from Najd. I heard that you were gathering, so I wished to attend. You will not be deprived of useful advice and counsel from me.”
They said:
“Very well, enter.”
He entered with them.
He said:
“Consider what you will do about this man. By Allah, he will soon overpower you in your affairs through his cause.”
One man said:
“Imprison him in chains and wait for death to overtake him, just as it overtook poets before him such as Zuhayr and al-Nābighah. He is only one of them.”
The enemy of Allah, the old man from Najd, cried out:
“By Allah, this is not a sound plan.”
“By Allah, his Lord will bring him out of prison to his companions. They will soon rise against you, take him from your hands, and protect him from you. I fear that they will then drive you from your own land.”
They said:
“Then consider another plan.”
Another man said:
“Expel him from your land and relieve yourselves of him. Once he has left, whatever he does and wherever he goes will not harm you. His trouble will be removed from you, and his affair will concern others.”
The old man from Najd replied:
“By Allah, this is not a sound plan. Have you not seen the sweetness of his words, his eloquence, and how what people hear from him captures their hearts?”
“By Allah, if you do that and he presents his message to the Arabs, they will gather against you. He will then come to you, drive you from your land, and kill your leaders.”
They said:
“By Allah, he has spoken the truth. Consider another plan.”
Abū Jahl said:
“By Allah, I will propose a plan that I do not think any of you has yet perceived. I see no other solution.”
They asked:
“What is it?”
He said:
“We will take from every clan a well-born, strong young man and give each of them a sharp sword. Then they will all strike him together with a single blow.”
“Once they have killed him, responsibility for his blood will be distributed among all the clans. I do not believe Banū Hāshim will be able to wage war against all of Quraysh.”
“When they see this, they will accept blood money. We will then be relieved of him and end his harm against us.”
The old man from Najd said:
“By Allah, this is the correct plan. The young man has spoken rightly. I see no other course.”
They dispersed having unanimously agreed upon that plan.
Gabriel came to the Prophet ﷺ and commanded him not to sleep that night in the bed in which he normally slept. Allah then granted him permission to depart.
After his arrival in Madinah, Allah revealed Sūrat al-Anfāl, reminding him of His blessing and protection:
“Remember when those who disbelieved plotted against you to imprison you, kill you, or expel you. They plotted, and Allah planned, and Allah is the best of planners.”
Concerning their statement:
“Wait for the uncertainty of death to overtake him, as it overtook the poets before him,”
Allah revealed:
“Or do they say, ‘He is a poet for whom we await the misfortune of time’?”
 [Sūrat al-Ṭūr 52:30]
That day was called “the Day of the Crowd” because of the large gathering that assembled to deliberate upon the plan.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/134–135).

12376 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Abū Jahl said:
“O Allah, if this is truly the truth from You, then rain stones upon us from the sky or bring us a painful punishment.”
The following verses were then revealed:
“Allah would not punish them while you were among them, nor would Allah punish them while they sought forgiveness. Yet why should Allah not punish them while they obstruct people from the Sacred Mosque…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4649) and Muslim (2796).
The words of Allah:
“Allah would not punish them while you were among them, nor would Allah punish them while they sought forgiveness”
mean that the presence of the Prophet ﷺ among them prevented punishment from descending upon them. This was an honor granted to the Prophet ﷺ.
When he left Makkah for Madinah, the remaining Muslims continued seeking Allah’s forgiveness, and through them the people were spared punishment. Their seeking forgiveness was the second cause preventing punishment from descending.
When those Muslims also left Makkah and permission was later given for its conquest, the punishment mentioned in the following verse came upon the disbelievers:
“Why should Allah not punish them while they obstruct people from the Sacred Mosque?”

12377 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The polytheists used to circumambulate the House, saying:
“At Your service, at Your service. You have no partner.”
The Prophet ﷺ would say:
“Enough, enough.”
They would then continue:
“Except a partner who belongs to You; You possess him and whatever he possesses.”
They would also say:
“Your forgiveness, Your forgiveness.”
Allah then revealed:
“Allah would not punish them while you were among them, nor would Allah punish them while they sought forgiveness.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“There were two sources of security among them: the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and seeking forgiveness.”
He said:
“The Prophet ﷺ departed, while seeking forgiveness remained.”
“Why should Allah not punish them while they obstruct people from the Sacred Mosque, though they are not its rightful guardians? Its rightful guardians are only those who fear Allah, but most of them do not know.”
He said:
“This refers to the punishment of the Hereafter,”
and:
“That refers to the punishment of this world.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/150–151) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1691).

12378 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The polytheists used to say:
“At Your service; You have no partner.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say:
“Woe to you! Enough, enough.”
They would then say:
“Except a partner who belongs to You; You possess him and whatever he possesses.”
They would say this while circumambulating the House.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1185).
The words:
“Enough, enough”
mean:
“This statement is sufficient for you. Stop there and do not add anything to it.”

12379 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The people of the pre-Islamic period used to circumambulate the House while naked. Allah therefore revealed:
“Their prayer at the House was nothing but whistling and clapping.”
He said:
“Mukāʾ means whistling, and taṣdiyah means clapping.”
Allah also revealed concerning them:
“Say: Who has forbidden the adornment of Allah which He has produced for His servants and the good things of provision? Say: They are for those who believe during the life of this world, exclusively theirs on the Day of Resurrection. In this way, We explain the signs in detail for people who know.”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:32]
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1696) and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (10/117–118).

12380 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will we be held accountable for what we did during the pre-Islamic period?”
He replied:
“Whoever does well after accepting Islam will not be held accountable for what he did during the pre-Islamic period. But whoever does evil after accepting Islam will be held accountable for both the earlier and later deeds.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6921) and Muslim (120). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12381 — Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what about the acts of devotion I used to perform during the pre-Islamic period, such as charity, freeing slaves, and maintaining family ties? Will I receive any reward for them?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“You accepted Islam while retaining the good you had previously done.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1436) and Muslim (123).

12382 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
When Allah placed Islam within my heart, I came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Extend your right hand so that I may pledge allegiance to you.”
He extended his hand, but I withdrew mine.
He asked:
“What is the matter, ʿAmr?”
I replied:
“I wish to set a condition.”
He asked:
“What condition do you wish to set?”
I replied:
“That I be forgiven.”
He said:
“Do you not know that Islam erases everything that came before it?”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (121).

12383 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“One man fights for spoils, another fights to gain fame, and another fights so that his position and bravery may be seen. Which of them is fighting in the path of Allah?”
He replied:
“Whoever fights so that the word of Allah may be supreme is fighting in the path of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2810) and Muslim (1904). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12384 — Usāmah ibn Zayd ibn Ḥārithah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us against al-Ḥuraqah, a clan of Juhaynah. We attacked them in the morning and defeated them.
A man from the Anṣār and I caught up with one of their men. When we overtook him, he said:
“There is no deity except Allah.”
The Anṣārī refrained from attacking him, but I stabbed him with my spear and killed him.
When we returned, news of that reached the Prophet ﷺ. He asked:
“Usāmah, did you kill him after he said, ‘There is no deity except Allah’?”
I replied:
“He only said it to protect himself.”
He repeated:
“Did you kill him after he said, ‘There is no deity except Allah’?”
He continued repeating this to me until I wished that I had not embraced Islam before that day.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4269) and Muslim (96:159). The wording is that of Muslim.

12385 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been commanded to fight the people until they testify that there is no deity except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah, establish prayer, and give zakāh. When they do that, they protect their lives and property from me, except where a right is due, and their reckoning is with Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (25) and Muslim (22).

12386 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that a man came to him and said:
“Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, do you not hear what Allah has mentioned in His Book:
‘If two groups of believers fight one another…’
 [Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:9]
until the end of the verse? What prevents you from fighting as Allah has mentioned in His Book?”
He replied:
“My nephew, it is preferable to me that I misunderstand that verse and refrain from fighting than that I misunderstand the verse in which Allah says:
‘Whoever deliberately kills a believer…’
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:93]
until its conclusion.”
The man said:
“But Allah says:
‘Fight them until there is no persecution and religion is for Allah. If they cease, there is to be no aggression except against the wrongdoers.’
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:193]”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
“We did that during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, when Islam was weak and few in number. A man would be persecuted because of his religion: either they would kill him or imprison him. This continued until Islam became widespread and there was no longer persecution.”
When the man realized that Ibn ʿUmar would not agree with what he wanted, he asked:
“What is your opinion concerning ʿAlī and ʿUthmān?”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
“What should I say concerning ʿAlī and ʿUthmān? As for ʿUthmān, Allah had already pardoned him, but you disliked that Allah should pardon him. As for ʿAlī, he was the cousin and son-in-law of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He pointed with his hand and said:
“His daughter—or daughters—are where you can see them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4650).

12387 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
Ibn ʿUmar came out to us—or came to us—and a man asked him:
“What is your view concerning fighting during civil strife?”
He replied:
“Do you know what persecution truly was? Muḥammad ﷺ fought the polytheists, and being forced back into disbelief by them was persecution. It was not like your fighting over political rule.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4651).

12388 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Any settlement you come to and stay in, your share is in it. Any settlement that disobeys Allah and His Messenger, one-fifth of it belongs to Allah and His Messenger, then it is yours.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1756).

12389 — ʿAmr ibn ʿAbasah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in prayer facing a camel taken from the spoils. When he concluded the prayer, he took a single hair from the camel’s side and said:
“Not even the equivalent of this hair from your spoils is lawful for me, except the one-fifth, and even the one-fifth is returned among you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2755), and through him by al-Bayhaqī (6/339).

12390 — ʿAlī narrated:
I had an aged she-camel from my share of the spoils on the day of Badr, and the Prophet ﷺ gave me another aged she-camel from the one-fifth.
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3091) and Muslim (1979:2).

12391 — Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim narrated:
ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān and I went to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“You gave Banū al-Muṭṭalib a share from the one-fifth of Khaybar but left us out, even though our relationship to you is of the same degree.”
He replied:
“Banū Hāshim and Banū al-Muṭṭalib are one and the same.”
Jubayr said:
The Prophet ﷺ did not give anything to Banū ʿAbd Shams or Banū Nawfal.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4229).
The spoils are apportioned among the fighters by giving one share to a foot soldier and three shares to a horseman: two shares for his horse and one share for himself.

12392 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ allocated two shares from the spoils for the horse and one share for the rider.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2863) and Muslim (1762).
Women who accompany the army are not assigned a fixed share, but they are given a discretionary gift from the spoils.

12393 — Yazīd ibn Hurmuz narrated:
Najdah wrote to Ibn ʿAbbās asking him about five matters. Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Were it not that I would otherwise conceal knowledge, I would not have written to him.”
Najdah wrote:
“To proceed: Inform me whether the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to take women on military expeditions; whether he assigned them a fixed share; whether he killed children; when an orphan’s state of orphanhood ends; and to whom the one-fifth belongs.”
Ibn ʿAbbās wrote in reply:
“You asked whether the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took women on military expeditions. He did take them, and they treated the wounded and were given discretionary gifts from the spoils. However, he did not assign them a fixed share.”
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not kill children, so do not kill children.”
“You asked when an orphan’s state of orphanhood ends. By my life, a man’s beard may grow while he still lacks the ability to manage his own interests and to give responsibly from his wealth. When he becomes capable of conducting his affairs properly, as other sound-minded people do, his state of orphanhood has ended.”
“You asked to whom the one-fifth belongs. We used to say that it belonged to us, but our people refused to recognize that claim.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1812).
The personal equipment of a slain enemy belongs to the one who killed him and is not included in the general division of the spoils.

12394 — Abū Qatādah ibn Ribʿī narrated:
We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the year of Ḥunayn. When the armies met, the Muslims initially retreated.
I saw a polytheist overpowering a Muslim. I circled around until I came behind him and struck him with my sword upon the upper part of his shoulder.
He turned toward me and seized me in an embrace so powerful that I felt death within it. Then death overtook him, and he released me.
I met ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and asked:
“What has happened to the people?”
He replied:
“It is the command of Allah.”
The Muslims then returned to the battle, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ announced:
“Whoever kills an enemy and has evidence proving it will receive the slain man’s personal equipment.”
I stood and asked:
“Who will testify for me?”
Then I sat down.
The Prophet ﷺ repeated:
“Whoever kills an enemy and has evidence proving it will receive his personal equipment.”
I stood again and asked:
“Who will testify for me?”
Then I sat down.
He repeated it a third time, so I stood.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What is the matter, Abū Qatādah?”
I related the entire incident to him.
A man from among the people said:
“He has spoken the truth, O Messenger of Allah. I have the slain man’s equipment, so compensate him for it from something else.”
Abū Bakr said:
“No, by Allah! He will not deprive one of the lions of Allah, who fights in defense of Allah and His Messenger, and give you his spoils.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“He has spoken the truth. Give it to him.”
The man then gave it to me.
I sold the armor and used its price to purchase an orchard among Banū Salamah. It was the first property I ever acquired in Islam.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in al-Jihād (990), al-Bukhārī (3142), and Muslim (1751).
Regarding the words:
“The Day of Criterion”:

12395 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“If you believe in Allah and what We sent down upon Our servant on the Day of Criterion”:
“The Day of Criterion means the day of Badr, the day upon which Allah distinguished truth from falsehood.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (11/200–201) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1706).

12396 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik narrated:
I never remained behind when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went on a military expedition except during the expedition of Tabūk. However, I had also remained behind from the Battle of Badr, and no one who stayed behind from it was reproached.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had set out only intending to intercept the caravan of Quraysh, but Allah brought the Muslims and their enemy together without any prior appointment.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3951) and Muslim (2769).

12397 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ received news that Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb was returning from Syria with forty mounted Qurayshī merchants, among whom were Makhramah ibn Nawfal and ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ called upon the Muslims to go out and said:
“Here is Abū Sufyān returning with the merchandise of Quraysh. Go out to intercept it; perhaps Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will grant it to you as spoils.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims set out. Some men responded quickly, while others delayed, because the call had only been to obtain property and they did not expect to encounter a battle.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out with slightly more than three hundred men. Most of his Companions were on foot, and they had eighty camels and one horse.
The hadith continues.
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/606–607).

12398 — Sālim Abū al-Naḍr, the freedman and secretary of ʿUmar ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, narrated:
ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā wrote a letter to him, which I read. It stated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not wish to meet the enemy. Ask Allah for safety and well-being. But when you do meet them, remain steadfast, and know that Paradise lies beneath the shadows of swords.”
He then said:
“O Allah, Revealer of the Book, Mover of the clouds, and Defeater of the confederate armies, defeat them and grant us victory over them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2965–2966) and Muslim (1742).

12399 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not wish to meet the enemy, but when you meet them, remain steadfast.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1741).

12400 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
On the day of Badr, Iblīs came with an army of devils, carrying his banner. He appeared in the form of a man from Banū Mudlij, specifically in the form of Surāqah ibn Mālik ibn Juʿshum.
Satan said to the polytheists:
“No one among the people can defeat you today, and I am your protector.”
When the people formed their battle lines, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took a handful of dust and threw it into the faces of the polytheists, whereupon they turned and fled.
Gabriel approached Iblīs. When Iblīs saw him, while his hand was being held by one of the polytheists, he pulled his hand away and fled together with his followers.
The man said:
“Surāqah, did you not claim that you were our protector?”
He replied:
“I see what you do not see. I fear Allah, and Allah is severe in punishment.”
That occurred when he saw the angels.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/221), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1715), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (3/78).

12401 — Abū Dharr narrated from the Prophet ﷺ, relating from Allah, Blessed and Exalted, that He said:
“My servants, I have forbidden injustice for Myself and made it forbidden among you, so do not wrong one another.
My servants, all of you are astray except those whom I guide, so seek guidance from Me and I will guide you.
My servants, all of you are hungry except those whom I feed, so ask Me for food and I will feed you.
My servants, all of you are unclothed except those whom I clothe, so ask Me for clothing and I will clothe you.
My servants, you commit sins by night and day, while I forgive all sins, so seek forgiveness from Me and I will forgive you.
My servants, you can never attain the ability to harm Me, nor can you attain the ability to benefit Me.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the humans and jinn among you, were all as righteous as the most righteous heart of any one man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the slightest.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the humans and jinn among you, were all as wicked as the most wicked heart of any one man among you, that would not diminish My dominion in the slightest.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the humans and jinn among you, all stood together upon one plain and asked Me, and I granted every person what he requested, that would diminish what I possess no more than a needle diminishes the sea when it is dipped into it.
My servants, they are only your deeds, which I record for you and then repay you for in full. Whoever finds good should praise Allah, and whoever finds otherwise should blame no one but himself.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2577).

12402 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Abū Bakr sent me among those who were to make the following public announcement in Minā on the Day of Sacrifice:
“After this year, no polytheist may perform pilgrimage, and no naked person may circumambulate the House.”
Abū Bakr therefore publicly declared the termination of the treaties to the people that year. Consequently, during the year of the Farewell Pilgrimage in which the Prophet ﷺ performed Ḥajj, no polytheist performed pilgrimage.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3177) and Muslim (1347). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12403 — Sulaym ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
There was a treaty between Muʿāwiyah and the Byzantines. Muʿāwiyah began advancing through their territory so that he could attack them immediately when the treaty expired.
Suddenly, a man appeared riding an animal or horse, saying:
“Allah is the Greatest! Fulfillment, not treachery!”
It was ʿAmr ibn ʿAbasah.
Muʿāwiyah asked him about this, and he replied:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever has a treaty with a people must neither loosen nor tighten any of its terms until its period expires, unless he openly terminates it with them on equal terms.”
Muʿāwiyah then returned with the people.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2759), al-Tirmidhī (1580), and Ibn Ḥibbān (4817).

12404 — ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say while standing upon the pulpit, after reciting:
“Prepare against them whatever strength you can”:
“Indeed, strength is archery. Indeed, strength is archery. Indeed, strength is archery.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1917).
Regarding the words:
“And war-horses kept ready”:

12405 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Horses may be a source of reward for one man, a protection for another, and a burden of sin upon another.
As for the one for whom they are a source of reward, it is a man who keeps them for the sake of Allah and ties them with a long rope in a meadow or garden. Whatever they graze within the reach of that rope is recorded as good deeds for him.
If they break their tether and run over one or two elevations, their hoofprints and dung are recorded as good deeds for him. If they pass by a river and drink from it without him intending to water them, that is also recorded as good deeds for him. Thus, they are a source of reward for him.
Another man keeps them in order to remain self-sufficient and preserve his dignity, while not neglecting Allah’s rights concerning their necks and backs. For him, they are a protection.
A third man keeps them out of pride, ostentation, and hostility toward the Muslims. For him, they are a burden of sin.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked about donkeys, and he replied:
“Nothing specific has been revealed to me concerning them except this comprehensive and unique verse:
‘Whoever does an atom’s weight of good will see it, and whoever does an atom’s weight of evil will see it.’”
 [Sūrat al-Zalzalah 99:7–8]
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in al-Jihād (975), al-Bukhārī (2371), and Muslim (987).

12406 — ʿUrwah al-Bāriqī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Goodness remains tied to the forelocks of horses until the Day of Resurrection: reward and spoils.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2852) and Muslim (1873).
The hadith clearly indicates that horses will never become completely unnecessary in warfare until the Day of Resurrection. Even if they are not needed during a particular period, the need for them will return before the Day of Resurrection.
This is further supported by what is established in the authentic hadith:

12407 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated, within a lengthy hadith concerning the emergence of the Antichrist:
The Antichrist will have reached their families behind them, so they will cast aside what is in their hands and turn back. They will send ten horsemen as scouts.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I know their names, the names of their fathers, and the colors of their horses. They will be the best horsemen upon the face of the earth that day—or among the best horsemen upon the face of the earth that day.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2899).

12408 — Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever keeps a horse for the sake of Allah, out of faith in Allah and belief in His promise, then the horse’s food, drink, dung, and urine will all be placed upon his scale on the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2853).

12409 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among the best ways of life for people is that of a man holding the reins of his horse in the path of Allah, flying upon its back whenever he hears an alarming cry or call to battle, seeking killing and death wherever they are likely to be found.
Or that of a man living with a small flock of sheep upon the summit of one of these mountains or in the depths of one of these valleys, establishing prayer, paying zakāh, and worshipping his Lord until death comes to him, dealing with people only in goodness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1889).

12410 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀṣim narrated:
When Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ the spoils on the day of Ḥunayn, he distributed them among those whose hearts were to be reconciled, but he gave nothing to the Anṣār. They appeared to feel some sadness because they had not received what others had received.
He addressed them, saying:
“Assembly of the Anṣār, did I not find you astray and Allah guided you through me? Were you not divided and Allah united you through me? Were you not poor and Allah enriched you through me?”
Whenever he mentioned something, they replied:
“Allah and His Messenger have shown us greater favor.”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4330) and Muslim (1061).

12411 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the verse:
“If there are twenty steadfast men among you, they will overcome two hundred”
was revealed, it was difficult for the Muslims because it had initially been made obligatory that one of them must not flee from ten.
Then the concession was revealed:
“Now Allah has lightened your burden, knowing that there is weakness among you. If there are one hundred steadfast men among you, they will overcome two hundred.”
He said:
“When Allah reduced the numerical burden upon them, the required degree of steadfastness was reduced in proportion to that concession.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4653).

12412 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
When the verse:
“If there are twenty steadfast men among you, they will overcome two hundred”
was revealed, it was prescribed that twenty must not flee from two hundred and that one must not flee from ten.
Allah then said:
“Now Allah has lightened your burden, knowing that there is weakness among you. If there are one hundred steadfast men among you, they will overcome two hundred.”
It was then prescribed that one must not flee from two and that one hundred must not flee from two hundred. If one person retreated from three, however, he was not regarded as having unlawfully fled.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/9138).

12413 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Allah initially obligated each one of them to fight ten enemy soldiers. This was burdensome and difficult for them, so Allah reduced it until each one was required to face only two.
Allah revealed concerning this:
“If there are twenty steadfast men among you…”
until the end of the verse.
He then said:
“Had it not been for a prior decree from Allah, a tremendous punishment would have touched you for what you took.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:68]
This refers to the spoils of Badr, meaning:
“Were it not that I do not punish one who disobeys Me until I first issue a clear warning and command to him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/263), al-Ṭabarānī (11/171), and Ibn Ḥibbān (4773), whose wording is cited.

12414 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
When they captured the prisoners, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked Abū Bakr and ʿUmar:
“What is your opinion concerning these prisoners?”
Abū Bakr said:
“Prophet of Allah, they are our cousins and clansmen. I believe that you should accept ransom from them so that it may strengthen us against the disbelievers. Perhaps Allah will guide them to Islam.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What is your opinion, Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb?”
I replied:
“No, by Allah, O Messenger of Allah. I do not share Abū Bakr’s opinion. I believe that you should hand them over to us so that we may strike their necks. Hand ʿAqīl over to ʿAlī so that he may strike his neck, and hand a certain relative of mine over to me so that I may strike his neck. These are the leaders and champions of disbelief.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ preferred Abū Bakr’s opinion and did not prefer mine.
The following morning, I came and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr sitting and weeping.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell me why you and your companion are weeping. If I find reason to weep, I will weep; and if I do not, I will force myself to weep because of your weeping.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I am weeping because of what your companions proposed to me regarding accepting ransom. Their punishment was shown to me closer than this tree.”
It was a tree near the Prophet of Allah ﷺ.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land…”
until His words:
“So eat from what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good.”
Allah then made the spoils lawful for them.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1763).

12415 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ consulted Abū Bakr concerning the prisoners. Abū Bakr said:
“They are your people and clansmen, so release them.”
He then consulted ʿUmar, who said:
“Kill them.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted ransom for them. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land…”
until His words:
“So eat from what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good.”
The Prophet ﷺ later met ʿUmar and said:
“A calamity nearly struck us because we opposed your opinion.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/329).
Ibn Jarīr narrated in his Tafsīr from Ibn ʿAbbās concerning the words of Allah:
“It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land”:
He said:
“That was on the day of Badr, when the Muslims were few.”
When their numbers increased and their authority became strong, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, later revealed concerning prisoners:
“Thereafter, either release them graciously or for ransom.”
 [Sūrat Muḥammad 47:4]
Allah therefore gave the Prophet ﷺ and the believers a choice concerning prisoners: they could kill them or enslave them.

12416 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Spoils of war were not lawful for any dark-haired people before you. Fire would descend from the sky and consume them.
When the day of Badr came, the people hastened to take the spoils. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
‘Had it not been for a prior decree from Allah, a tremendous punishment would have touched you for what you took. So eat from what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good, and fear Allah. Indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3085), Aḥmad (7433), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4806).
The words:
“Spoils were not lawful for any dark-haired people before you”
refer to the numerous nations that preceded this nation.

12417 — Khaythamah narrated:
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ was sitting among a group of people. They mentioned ʿAlī and insulted him.
Saʿd said:
“Refrain from insulting the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. We acquired worldly goods while accompanying the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
‘Had it not been for a prior decree from Allah, a tremendous punishment would have touched you for what you took.’”
“I hope that a mercy from Allah had already been decreed for us.”
Someone said:
“By Allah, al-Ukhaynis truly hated and insulted you.”
Saʿd laughed until laughter overcame him, then said:
“May a man not feel anger toward his brother because of a matter occurring between them, while still remaining trustworthy toward him?”
He also mentioned another statement.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1734) and al-Ḥākim (2/329–330), whose wording is cited.

12418 — Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ consulted the people concerning the prisoners on the day of Badr and said:
“Allah has placed them under your power.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb stood and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, strike their necks.”
The Prophet ﷺ turned away from him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then repeated:
“People, Allah has placed them under your power, although only yesterday they were your brothers and relatives.”
ʿUmar stood again and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, strike their necks.”
The Prophet ﷺ again turned away from him.
The Prophet ﷺ repeated the same words to the people. Abū Bakr then stood and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we believe that you should pardon them and accept ransom from them.”
The expression of distress disappeared from the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He pardoned them and accepted ransom from them.
Allah then revealed:
“Had it not been for a prior decree from Allah, a tremendous punishment would have touched you for what you took.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (13555).
Regarding the words of Allah:
“So eat from what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good”:

12419 — Abū Hurayrah narrated from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ a number of reports, among which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“One of the prophets went out for battle and said to his people: ‘No man should follow me who has contracted marriage with a woman and intends to consummate it but has not yet done so; nor one who has constructed a building but has not yet raised its roof; nor one who has purchased sheep or pregnant she-camels and is awaiting their offspring.’
He set out for battle and approached the town at the time of the afternoon prayer, or near that time. He said to the sun: ‘You are under command, and I am under command. O Allah, hold it back for me for a while.’
The sun was held back until Allah granted him victory.
They gathered the spoils, and fire came to consume them, but it refused to do so.
He said: ‘There has been misappropriation among you. Let one man from every tribe pledge allegiance to me.’
They pledged allegiance to him, and one man’s hand stuck to his hand. He said: ‘The misappropriation is among your tribe. Let your tribe pledge allegiance to me.’
They pledged allegiance, and the hands of two or three men stuck to his hand. He said: ‘The misappropriation is among you. You are the ones who stole from the spoils.’
They brought out an object of gold resembling the head of a cow.
They placed it with the spoils upon the open ground, and the fire came and consumed them.
Spoils of war were not lawful for anyone before us. Allah, Blessed and Exalted, saw our weakness and inability, so He made them lawful and good for us.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3124) and Muslim (1747). The wording is that of Muslim.

12420 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated concerning the verse:
“O Prophet, say to those prisoners in your hands…”
Al-ʿAbbās had been captured on the day of Badr and ransomed himself for forty ounces of gold.
When this verse was revealed, al-ʿAbbās said:
“Allah has granted me two things for which I would not exchange the entire world. I was captured on the day of Badr and ransomed myself for forty ounces of gold, but Allah later gave me forty slaves, and I hope to receive the forgiveness that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, promised us.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (11/285–286) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1737).

12421 — The Mother of the Believers ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Al-ʿAbbās said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I was already a Muslim.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Allah knows best about your Islam. If what you say is true, Allah will reward you. Nevertheless, ransom yourself, your two nephews—Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib—and your ally ʿUtbah ibn ʿAmr ibn Jaḥdam, the brother of Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr.”
He replied:
“I do not possess that amount, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Then where is the money that you and Umm al-Faḍl buried, when you said to her, ‘If I am killed, this money belongs to my sons al-Faḍl, ʿAbdullāh, and Qutham’?”
Al-ʿAbbās said:
“By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, I testify that you are truly the Messenger of Allah. No one knew about this except me and Umm al-Faḍl.”
He then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, count toward my ransom the twenty ounces of wealth that you took from me.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“I will not do so.”
Al-ʿAbbās then paid the ransom for himself, his two nephews, and his ally.
Allah revealed concerning him:
“O Prophet, say to those prisoners in your hands: If Allah knows of any goodness within your hearts, He will give you something better than what was taken from you and forgive you. Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
Al-ʿAbbās said:
“In Islam, Allah gave me in place of those twenty ounces twenty slaves, each of whom possessed wealth with which he traded, in addition to the forgiveness I hope to receive from Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/324), and through him by al-Bayhaqī (6/322).

12422 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Some men from the Anṣār sought permission from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, permit us to waive the ransom of al-ʿAbbās, the son of our sister.”
He replied:
“Do not waive even a single dirham of it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2537).

12423 — Jarīr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Emigrants and the Anṣār are allies of one another in this world and the Hereafter. The freed people of Quraysh and the emancipated people of Thaqīf are allies of one another until the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (19215), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (2/356–357), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7260).
Regarding the words:
“Those who believed but did not emigrate”:
This verse indicates that emigration was obligatory during the lifetime of the Prophet ﷺ before the conquest of Makkah.
After the conquest of Makkah, however, it is authentically established that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There is no emigration after the conquest.”
Likewise, emigration is not obligatory upon one who lives among polytheists and disbelievers while possessing freedom to worship and openly practice the rites of Islam. This is indicated by the statement of ʿĀʾishah, may Allah be pleased with her.

12424 — ʿAṭāʾ ibn Abī Rabāḥ narrated:
I visited ʿĀʾishah with ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr al-Laythī, and we asked her about emigration.
She replied:
“There is no emigration today. A believer formerly fled with his religion to Allah, the Exalted, and to His Messenger ﷺ out of fear that he would be persecuted because of it. Today Allah has made Islam manifest, and a person may worship his Lord wherever he wishes. What remains is striving and sincere intention.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3900).

12425 — Buraydah narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed a commander over an army or expedition, he would especially instruct him to fear Allah and to treat the Muslims accompanying him well. He would then say:
“Go forth in the name of Allah, in the path of Allah. Fight those who disbelieve in Allah. Go forth, but do not misappropriate the spoils, betray, mutilate, or kill a child.
When you meet your enemy from among the polytheists, invite them to one of three choices. Whichever of them they accept, accept it from them and refrain from fighting them.
First, invite them to Islam. If they accept, accept it from them and refrain from fighting them.
Then invite them to move from their land to the land of the Emigrants. Inform them that if they do so, they will possess the same rights and bear the same obligations as the Emigrants.
If they refuse to move, inform them that they will be like the Muslim Bedouins: Allah’s rulings that apply to the believers will apply to them, but they will have no share in the spoils or fayʾ unless they fight alongside the Muslims.
If they refuse Islam, ask them to pay the jizyah. If they accept, accept it from them and refrain from fighting them. If they refuse, seek Allah’s help and fight them.
When you besiege the people of a fortress and they ask you to grant them the protection of Allah and His Prophet, do not grant them the protection of Allah or His Prophet. Instead, grant them your own protection and that of your companions, for violating your protection and that of your companions is less serious than violating the protection of Allah and His Messenger.
When you besiege the people of a fortress and they ask you to submit them to Allah’s judgment, do not submit them to Allah’s judgment. Rather, submit them to your own judgment, for you do not know whether your judgment concerning them will truly coincide with Allah’s judgment or not.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1731).

12426 — Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām narrated:
The following verse was revealed concerning us:
“Blood relatives are more entitled to inherit from one another according to the Book of Allah. Indeed, Allah has knowledge of all things.”
He said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had established brotherhood between one man from the Emigrants and one from the Anṣār, and we had no doubt that they would inherit from one another.
If Kaʿb had died without another heir, I thought that I would inherit from him, and if I died in the same circumstances, he would inherit from me. This continued until the following verse was revealed:
“Blood relatives are more entitled to inherit from one another according to the Book of Allah. Indeed, Allah has knowledge of all things.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1742–1743) and al-Ḥākim (4/344–345), whose wording is cited.

12427 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“As for those with whom your oaths have established a bond, give them their due share. Indeed, Allah is Witness over all things.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:33]
A man would enter into a pact with another man despite there being no blood relationship between them, and each would inherit from the other. Sūrat al-Anfāl abrogated that ruling.
Allah, the Exalted, says:
“Blood relatives are more entitled to inherit from one another.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2921).
What was abrogated was inheritance based upon a pact. As for entering into covenants to obey Allah, support the oppressed, and establish brotherhood for Allah’s sake, these remain recommended matters and are encouraged in numerous hadiths.

12428 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās about Sūrat al-Tawbah. He replied:
“Al-Tawbah is the exposer. It continued revealing, ‘And among them… and among them…,’ until they thought that it would leave none of them unmentioned.”
I asked:
“What about Sūrat al-Anfāl?”
He replied:
“It was revealed concerning Badr.”
I asked:
“What about Sūrat al-Ḥashr?”
He replied:
“It was revealed concerning Banū al-Naḍīr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4882) and Muslim (3031:31).

12429 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
“The final verse revealed was: ‘They ask you for a ruling. Say: Allah gives you a ruling concerning one who dies leaving neither descendants nor ascendants.’
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:176]
The final sūrah revealed was Barāʾah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4654) and Muslim (1618:11).

12430 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Abū Bakr sent me during that pilgrimage among a group of callers who were to proclaim in Minā on the Day of Sacrifice:
“After this year, no polytheist may perform pilgrimage, and no naked person may circumambulate the House.”
Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then sent ʿAlī after them and commanded him to proclaim Barāʾah.
Abū Hurayrah said:
ʿAlī proclaimed Barāʾah together with us among the people of Minā on the Day of Sacrifice, announcing:
“After this year, no polytheist may perform pilgrimage, and no naked person may circumambulate the House.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4655) and Muslim (1347).
The words:
“The Day of the Greater Pilgrimage”
refer to the Day of Sacrifice.

12431 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
During the pilgrimage performed by the Prophet ﷺ, he stood between the stone pillars on the Day of Sacrifice and asked:
“What day is this?”
They replied:
“The Day of Sacrifice.”
He said:
“This is the Day of the Greater Pilgrimage.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1945), Ibn Mājah (3058), authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/331), and al-Bayhaqī (5/139), through Hishām ibn al-Ghāz, who said: I heard Nāfiʿ narrating from Ibn ʿUmar, and he mentioned it. The wording is that of Abū Dāwūd. Its chain of transmission is authentic.
Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān also stated the same, as recorded in the two Ṣaḥīḥ collections: al-Bukhārī (4657) and Muslim (1347).

12432 — Jābir narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ returned from the ʿumrah of al-Jiʿrānah, he appointed Abū Bakr to lead the pilgrimage. We traveled with him until we reached al-ʿArj. He called for the dawn prayer and was about to pronounce the opening takbīr when he heard the sound of a camel behind him. He paused and said:
“That is the sound of al-Jadʿāʾ, the she-camel of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Perhaps the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has decided to join the pilgrimage.”
It was ʿAlī riding upon it. Abū Bakr asked him:
“Have you come as commander or messenger?”
He replied:
“No, rather as a messenger. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent me with Barāʾah to recite it to the people at the stations of the pilgrimage.”
We arrived in Makkah. One day before the Day of Tarwiyah, Abū Bakr stood and addressed the people, teaching them their pilgrimage rites. When he finished, ʿAlī stood and recited Barāʾah to the people until he completed it.
We then departed with him. On the Day of ʿArafah, Abū Bakr stood and addressed the people, teaching them their pilgrimage rites. When he finished, ʿAlī stood and recited Barāʾah to the people until he completed it.
Then came the Day of Sacrifice. After we departed from Muzdalifah and Abū Bakr returned, he addressed the people, teaching them about their departure, sacrifice, and pilgrimage rites. When he finished, ʿAlī stood and recited Barāʾah to the people until he completed it.
On the first day of departure from Minā, Abū Bakr stood and addressed the people, explaining how they should depart and stone the pillars, thereby teaching them their pilgrimage rites. When he finished, ʿAlī stood and recited Barāʾah to the people until he completed it.
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (2993), Ibn Khuzaymah (2974), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6645).
The statement:
“When he returned from the ʿumrah of al-Jiʿrānah, he sent Abū Bakr,”
refers to the following year. The ʿumrah of the Prophet ﷺ from al-Jiʿrānah took place in the eighth year after the Hijrah during Dhū al-Qaʿdah, after which he returned to Madinah. It would therefore not have been possible for him to appoint Abū Bakr as pilgrimage leader during Dhū al-Ḥijjah of that same year.
Rather, Abū Bakr’s appointment occurred during the following year, the ninth year after the Hijrah. The mention of al-Jiʿrānah in this hadith is intended to show the chronological order of the events, not that the two events occurred immediately one after the other.

12433 — Zayd ibn Wahb narrated:
We were sitting with Ḥudhayfah when he said:
“Only three of those referred to in this verse remain, and only four of the hypocrites remain.”
A Bedouin said:
“You Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ tell us things that we do not understand. What, then, of these people who break into our houses and steal our precious belongings?”
He replied:
“Those are sinful people. Yes, only four of the hypocrites remain. One of them is an elderly man who, if he drank cold water, would not even feel its coolness.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4658).

12434 — ʿUbayd Allāh al-Khawlānī narrated that he heard ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān say, when people criticized him for rebuilding the Mosque of the Messenger ﷺ:
“You have spoken excessively about this matter. I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
‘Whoever builds a mosque seeking the pleasure of Allah, Allah will build for him its like in Paradise.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (450) and Muslim (533). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12435 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whenever a person goes to the mosque in the morning or evening, Allah prepares a place of hospitality for him in Paradise each time he goes in the morning or evening.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (662) and Muslim (669). The wording is that of Muslim.

12436 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated:
I was beside the pulpit of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when a man said:
“After accepting Islam, I would not mind performing no deed other than providing water to the pilgrims.”
Another said:
“After accepting Islam, I would not mind performing no deed other than maintaining the Sacred Mosque.”
Another said:
“Striving in the path of Allah is superior to what you have mentioned.”
ʿUmar rebuked them and said:
“Do not raise your voices beside the pulpit of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, especially on Friday. After I have prayed the Friday prayer, I will enter upon him and ask him for a ruling concerning that over which you have differed.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Do you regard providing water to the pilgrims and maintaining the Sacred Mosque as equal to the deeds of one who believes in Allah and the Last Day…”
until the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1879:111).

12437 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Do you regard providing water to the pilgrims and maintaining the Sacred Mosque as equal to the deeds of one who believes in Allah and the Last Day?”
Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib said when he was captured on the day of Badr:
“Even if you preceded us in Islam, emigration, and striving, we used to maintain the Sacred Mosque, provide water to the pilgrims, and free captives.”
Allah then revealed:
“Do you regard providing water to the pilgrims and maintaining the Sacred Mosque as equal to the deeds of one who believes in Allah and the Last Day and strives in the path of Allah? They are not equal in the sight of Allah, and Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.”
Meaning that those deeds had been performed while they were in a state of polytheism, and deeds performed in polytheism were not accepted.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/378) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1768).

12438 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to the place where water was provided to the pilgrims and asked for a drink.
Al-ʿAbbās said:
“Faḍl, go to your mother and bring the Messenger of Allah ﷺ a drink from her.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Give me some of this water.”
Al-ʿAbbās said:
“O Messenger of Allah, the people put their hands into it.”
He repeated:
“Give me some of it.”
He drank from it and then came to Zamzam while they were drawing water and working there. He said:
“Continue your work, for you are engaged in a righteous deed.”
He then said:
“Were it not that the people might overpower you and take this responsibility from you, I would descend and place the rope upon this.”
He meant his shoulder and pointed toward it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1635).

12439 — Bakr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Muzanī narrated:
I was sitting with Ibn ʿAbbās beside the Kaʿbah when a Bedouin came to him and asked:
“Why do I see your cousins serving honey and milk while you serve nabīdh? Is it because you are in need or because of miserliness?”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“All praise belongs to Allah. We are neither in need nor miserly.”
“The Prophet ﷺ arrived riding his mount with Usāmah behind him and asked for a drink. We brought him a vessel of nabīdh. He drank from it and gave the remainder to Usāmah. He then said:
‘You have done well and beautifully. Continue doing this.’”
“Therefore, we do not wish to change what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ instructed us to do.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1316).

12440 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“None of you truly believes until I am more beloved to him than his father, his child, and all people.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (15) and Muslim (44:70). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12441 — Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No servant truly believes until I am more beloved to him than his family, his wealth, and all people.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (15) and Muslim (44:69).

12442 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Hishām narrated:
We were with the Prophet ﷺ while he was holding the hand of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.
ʿUmar said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you are more beloved to me than everything except myself.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“No, by the One in Whose hand is my soul, not until I am more beloved to you than your own self.”
ʿUmar said:
“Now, by Allah, you are more beloved to me than my own self.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Now, ʿUmar.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6632).

12443 — Abū Isḥāq narrated:
I heard al-Barāʾ when a man asked him:
“Abū ʿUmārah, did you flee on the day of Ḥunayn?”
He replied:
“No, by Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not turn away. However, some young and lightly armed Companions went forward without protective armor. They encountered expert archers from the combined forces of Hawāzin and Banū Naṣr, whose arrows hardly ever missed.”
“They showered them with arrows that hardly missed, so the Muslims retreated toward the Prophet ﷺ. He was upon his white mule, while his cousin Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was leading it.”
“The Prophet ﷺ dismounted, sought Allah’s help, and said:
‘I am the Prophet—this is no lie. I am the son of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.’”
He then arranged his Companions in battle formation.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2930) and Muslim (1776). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Ḥunayn is a valley situated between Makkah and al-Ṭāʾif.

12444 — Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib narrated:
I participated with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of Ḥunayn. Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and I remained beside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and did not leave him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was riding a white mule given to him by Farwah ibn Nufāthah al-Judhāmī.
When the Muslims and disbelievers met, the Muslims turned and fled. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began urging his mule forward toward the disbelievers.
Al-ʿAbbās said:
I was holding the reins of the mule of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, restraining it so that it would not rush forward, while Abū Sufyān held the stirrup of the Prophet ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“ʿAbbās, call the Companions of the acacia tree.”
Al-ʿAbbās, who had a powerful voice, said:
I called at the top of my voice:
“Where are the Companions of the acacia tree?”
He said:
“By Allah, when they heard my voice, they turned back as cows turn toward their calves, saying, ‘Here we are at your service! Here we are at your service!’”
They fought the disbelievers while the call went out among the Anṣār:
“Assembly of the Anṣār! Assembly of the Anṣār!”
The call was then restricted to Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj:
“Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj! Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ, seated upon his mule and stretching himself upward to observe their fighting, said:
“Now the battle has grown fierce.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then took some pebbles, threw them into the faces of the disbelievers, and said:
“They are defeated, by the Lord of Muḥammad.”
Al-ʿAbbās said:
I went to look, and the battle appeared to remain as it had been.
“But by Allah, as soon as he threw those pebbles at them, I continued to see their strength weakening and their cause retreating.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1775).
The words:
“He sent down forces that you did not see”
refer to the angels.
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr said:
“Allah reinforced His Prophet with five thousand specially marked angels.”

12445 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, the freedman of Umm Burthun, narrated from a man who witnessed Ḥunayn while still a disbeliever:
“When we met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims, they could not withstand us for even the time required to milk a sheep.”
“We came brandishing our swords before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until we drew close to him. Suddenly, there were men of beautiful appearance standing between us and him.”
“They said, ‘May these faces be disgraced! Turn back!’ We were defeated by those words.”
Good: Narrated by Musaddad in his Musnad, as cited by al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/143), and by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/393).

12446 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ met me while I was in a state of major ritual impurity. He took me by the hand, and I walked with him until he sat down. I quietly slipped away, returned to my lodging, and performed the ritual bath. I then came back while he was still sitting.
He asked:
“Where were you, Abū Hurayrah?”
I told him, and he said:
