“Glory be to Allah, Abū Hurayrah! A believer does not become impure.”
Another narration states:
“A Muslim does not become impure.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (285) and Muslim (371). The second wording is also narrated by al-Bukhārī (283).

12447 — Ḥudhayfah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ met him while he was in a state of major ritual impurity. Ḥudhayfah moved away from him, performed the ritual bath, and then returned.
He said:
“I was in a state of major ritual impurity.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“A Muslim does not become impure.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (372).

12448 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed a commander over an army or expedition, he would personally instruct him to fear Allah and to treat the Muslims accompanying him well. He would then say:
“Set out in the name of Allah and in the path of Allah. Fight those who disbelieve in Allah. Set out, but do not misappropriate the spoils, betray agreements, mutilate the dead, or kill a child.
When you meet your polytheist enemy, invite them to three choices. Whichever one they accept, accept it from them and refrain from fighting them.
First, invite them to Islam. If they accept, accept it from them and refrain from fighting them.
Then invite them to move from their land to the land of the Emigrants. Inform them that, if they do so, they will have the same rights and obligations as the Emigrants.
If they refuse to move, inform them that they will be like the Muslim Bedouins: Allah’s rulings that apply to the believers will apply to them, but they will have no share in the spoils or fayʾ unless they fight alongside the Muslims.
If they refuse, ask them to pay the jizyah. If they agree, accept it from them and refrain from fighting them. If they refuse, seek Allah’s help and fight them.
When you besiege the people of a fortress and they ask you to grant them the covenant of Allah and His Prophet, do not grant them the covenant of Allah or His Prophet. Rather, grant them your covenant and that of your companions, for it is less serious for you to violate your own covenant and that of your companions than to violate the covenant of Allah and His Messenger.
When you besiege the people of a fortress and they ask you to submit them to Allah’s judgment, do not submit them to Allah’s judgment. Rather, submit them to your own judgment, for you do not know whether you will correctly arrive at Allah’s judgment concerning them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1731).

12449 — Jubayr ibn Ḥayyah narrated:
ʿUmar sent armies to the various regions to fight the polytheists. Al-Hurmuzān embraced Islam, and ʿUmar said to him:
“I wish to consult you about these military campaigns.”
He replied:
“Yes. Their situation and that of the Muslim enemy is like a bird with a head, two wings, and two legs. If one wing is broken, the two legs, the other wing, and the head remain. If the second wing is broken, the two legs and the head remain. But if the head is crushed, the two legs and both wings are lost.
“The head is Chosroes, one wing is Caesar, and the other wing is Persia. Command the Muslims to march against Chosroes.”
ʿUmar called us to mobilize and appointed al-Nuʿmān ibn Muqarrin as our commander. When we reached enemy territory, the governor of Chosroes came against us with forty thousand men. An interpreter stood and said:
“Let one of you speak to me.”
Al-Mughīrah said:
“Ask whatever you wish.”
He asked:
“Who are you?”
Al-Mughīrah replied:
“We are people from the Arabs. We used to live in severe misery and hardship. Because of hunger, we would suck animal hides and date stones. We wore wool and animal hair, and we worshipped trees and stones.
“While we were in that condition, the Lord of the heavens and the earth—exalted is His remembrance and majestic is His greatness—sent us a prophet from among ourselves whose father and mother we knew.
“Our Prophet, the Messenger of our Lord ﷺ, commanded us to fight you until you worship Allah alone or pay the jizyah.
“Our Prophet ﷺ also informed us from the message of our Lord that whoever among us is killed will enter Paradise and enjoy bliss unlike anything ever seen, while whoever remains alive among us will gain authority over you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3159).

12450 — ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim narrated:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ while a golden cross was hanging around my neck. He said:
“ʿAdī, remove this idol from yourself.”
I heard him reciting in Sūrat Barāʾah:
“They took their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah.”
He then said:
“They did not literally worship them. Rather, when their religious leaders declared something lawful for them, they treated it as lawful, and when they declared something forbidden, they treated it as forbidden.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3095).
This is also supported by what is narrated from Abū al-Bakhtarī, from Ḥudhayfah:
A man asked Ḥudhayfah:
“Abū ʿAbd Allāh, regarding the words, ‘They took their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah,’ did they worship them?”
He replied:
“No. When they declared something lawful for them, they treated it as lawful, and when they declared something forbidden for them, they treated it as forbidden.”
Narrated by Saʿīd ibn Manṣūr (1012, al-Tafsīr) and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (11/418–420). Abū al-Bakhtarī did not meet Ḥudhayfah.
This interpretation mentioned by ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim was also narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās and many of the Successors, and it is the correct interpretation. For this reason, the leading hadith scholars included the hadith of ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim in their works, and Shaykh al-Islām Ibn Taymiyyah graded it good in Kitāb al-Īmān (p. 64).

12451 — ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim narrated:
While I was with the Prophet ﷺ, a man came and complained to him of poverty. Another man then came and complained about highway robbery.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“ʿAdī, have you seen al-Ḥīrah?”
I replied:
“I have not seen it, but I have been told about it.”
He said:
“If you live long enough, you will certainly see a woman traveling from al-Ḥīrah until she circumambulates the Kaʿbah, fearing no one except Allah.”
I said to myself:
“Then where will the bandits of Ṭayyiʾ, who have spread fear throughout the land, be?”
He said:
“If you live long enough, the treasures of Chosroes will certainly be conquered.”
I asked:
“Chosroes son of Hormuz?”
He replied:
“Chosroes son of Hormuz.”
He continued:
“If you live long enough, you will certainly see a man take out a handful of gold or silver, seeking someone who will accept it from him, but he will find no one willing to accept it.
Every one of you will certainly meet Allah on the Day he meets Him with no interpreter between himself and Allah.
Allah will say, ‘Did I not send you a messenger who conveyed the message to you?’ He will reply, ‘Yes.’
He will say, ‘Did I not give you wealth and favor you?’ He will reply, ‘Yes.’
He will look to his right and see nothing but Hell, and look to his left and see nothing but Hell.”
ʿAdī said:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Protect yourselves from the Fire, even with half a date. Whoever cannot find even half a date should do so with a kind word.”
ʿAdī said:
“I saw a woman travel from al-Ḥīrah until she circumambulated the Kaʿbah, fearing no one except Allah. I was also among those who conquered the treasures of Chosroes son of Hormuz. If you live long enough, you will see the fulfillment of what the Prophet Abū al-Qāsim ﷺ said concerning a man bringing out a handful of wealth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3595).
Muslim also narrated it (1016) through ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maʿqil, from ʿAdī, limiting it to the wording:
“Whoever among you is able to shield himself from the Fire, even with half a date, should do so.”

12452 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Night and day will not cease until al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā are worshipped again.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I thought that when Allah revealed:
‘He is the One Who sent His Messenger with guidance and the religion of truth to make it prevail over every religion, even though the polytheists dislike it,’
this would remain complete and permanent.”
He replied:
“It will remain for as long as Allah wills. Then Allah will send a pleasant wind that will take the soul of everyone whose heart contains even the weight of a mustard seed of faith. Only those in whom there is no goodness will remain, and they will return to the religion of their forefathers.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2907).

12453 — Thawbān narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah drew the earth together for me, and I saw its eastern and western regions. The dominion of my nation will reach everything that was drawn together for me, and I was given the two treasures: the red and the white.
I asked my Lord not to destroy my nation through a universal famine and not to empower an external enemy over them who would eradicate their collective strength.
My Lord said: ‘Muḥammad, whenever I decree something, it cannot be reversed. I have granted you concerning your nation that I will not destroy them through a universal famine and will not empower an external enemy over them who would eradicate their collective strength, even if all the people from the surrounding regions—or from between the surrounding regions—were to unite against them, until some of them destroy and enslave others among themselves.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2889).

12454 — Tamīm al-Dārī narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“This religion will certainly reach every place reached by night and day. Allah will leave no house of stone or tent of animal hair without causing this religion to enter it—either through the honor of one whom Allah honors or through the humiliation of one whom He humiliates: an honor by which Allah strengthens Islam and a humiliation by which He lowers disbelief.”
Tamīm al-Dārī used to say:
“I witnessed this among my own household. Whoever among them embraced Islam received goodness, honor, and dignity, while whoever remained a disbeliever was afflicted with humiliation, subjection, and the jizyah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (16957), al-Ḥākim (4/430), and through him by al-Bayhaqī (9/181).

12455 — Al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No house of stone or tent of animal hair will remain upon the earth without Allah causing the word of Islam to enter it—either through the honor of one whom He honors or the humiliation of one whom He humiliates. Either Allah will honor them by making them among its followers, or He will humble them so that they submit to its authority.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23814), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6699), and narrated by al-Ḥākim (4/430).

12456 — Khālid ibn Aslam narrated:
We went out with ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar. A Bedouin asked him:
“Tell me about the words of Allah:
‘Those who hoard gold and silver and do not spend them in the path of Allah.’”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
“Whoever hoards them and does not pay their zakāh—woe to him. This warning applied before the legislation of zakāh was revealed. When zakāh was prescribed, Allah made it a purification for wealth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1404).
Ibn Mājah also narrated it through another chain with a good isnād (1787), in which Ibn ʿUmar said:
“I would not mind possessing gold equal to Mount Uḥud, provided that I knew its exact amount, paid its zakāh, and used it in obedience to Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”

12457 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No owner of hoarded wealth who fails to pay its zakāh will escape having it heated for him in the Fire of Hell and made into plates with which his sides and forehead will be branded. This will continue until Allah judges between His servants on a Day whose measure is fifty thousand years. He will then be shown his path, either to Paradise or to the Fire…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (987:26).

12458 — Al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays narrated:
I was among a group of Quraysh when Abū Dharr passed by, saying:
“Give those who hoard wealth the tidings of branding upon their backs that will emerge through their sides, and branding from the backs of their necks that will emerge through their foreheads.”
He then moved aside and sat down.
I asked:
“Who is this?”
They replied:
“This is Abū Dharr.”
I went to him and asked:
“What was the statement I heard you make a moment ago?”
He replied:
“I said only something that I heard from their Prophet ﷺ.”
I asked:
“What is your view concerning this state allowance?”
He replied:
“Take it, for today it is a source of assistance. But if it becomes the price paid for your religion, then leave it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (992:35).

12459 — Al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays narrated:
I sat with a gathering of Quraysh. A man with rough hair, clothing, and appearance came, stood before them, greeted them, and said:
“Give those who hoard wealth the tidings of stones heated in the Fire of Hell. A stone will be placed upon the nipple of one of them until it emerges from the upper part of his shoulder, and it will be placed upon the upper part of his shoulder until it emerges from his nipple, while he trembles.”
He then turned away and sat beside a pillar. I followed him and sat with him without knowing who he was.
I said:
“It appears that those people disliked what you said.”
He replied:
“They understand nothing…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1407–1408) and Muslim (992:34).

12460 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever is given wealth by Allah but does not pay its zakāh will have his wealth represented for him on the Day of Resurrection as a bald-headed, venomous male snake with two dark markings above its eyes. It will encircle his neck, seize him by the sides of his mouth, and say, ‘I am your wealth. I am your treasure…’”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4565).

12461 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“The hoarded treasure of one of you will become a bald-headed, venomous male snake on the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4659).

12462 — Zayd ibn Wahb narrated:
I passed through al-Rabadhah and found Abū Dharr there. I asked him:
“What caused you to settle in this place?”
He replied:
“I was in Syria, where Muʿāwiyah and I differed concerning the verse:
‘Those who hoard gold and silver and do not spend them in the path of Allah.’
Muʿāwiyah said, ‘It was revealed concerning the People of the Book.’
I said, ‘It was revealed concerning both us and them.’”
“A dispute occurred between us because of that, and he wrote to ʿUthmān complaining about me. ʿUthmān wrote ordering me to come to Madinah.
When I arrived, people crowded around me as though they had never seen me before. I mentioned this to ʿUthmān, and he said, ‘You may withdraw to a nearby place if you wish.’
That is what brought me to this place. Even if they appointed an Abyssinian over me, I would listen and obey.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1406).

12463 — Zayd ibn Wahb narrated that Abū Dharr said:
I was walking with the Prophet ﷺ upon the rocky plain of Madinah when Mount Uḥud came into view.
He said:
“Abū Dharr.”
I replied:
“At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“It would not please me to possess gold equal to this Mount Uḥud and have three nights pass while even one dinar of it remained with me—except an amount I kept aside to repay a debt—unless I distributed it among the servants of Allah in this direction, that direction, and that direction.”
He gestured to his right, his left, and behind him.
He then continued walking and said:
“Those who possess the most wealth will have the least reward on the Day of Resurrection, except those who distribute it in this direction, that direction, and that direction—to their right, left, and behind them—and how few they are…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6444) and Muslim (94). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12464 — Abū Dharr narrated:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was sitting in the shade of the Kaʿbah. When he saw me, he said:
“They are the greatest losers, by the Lord of the Kaʿbah.”
I came and sat down, but I could not remain still and stood up, asking:
“O Messenger of Allah, may my father and mother be sacrificed for you, who are they?”
He replied:
“They are those who possess the most wealth, except those who distribute it in this direction, that direction, and that direction—in front of them, behind them, to their right, and to their left—and how few they are.
No owner of camels, cattle, or sheep who fails to pay their zakāh will escape having them come on the Day of Resurrection larger and fatter than they ever were. They will gore him with their horns and trample him with their hooves. Whenever the last of them has passed over him, the first will return, until judgment between the people is completed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6638) and Muslim (990).

12465 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated that he was with Abū Dharr. A servant girl of his would carry out his needs, and seven coins remained with her.
He instructed her to use them to purchase small copper coins.
I said to him:
“Why not save them for a need that may arise or for a guest who may stay with you?”
He replied:
“The Prophet ﷺ, my close friend, gave me this solemn instruction:
‘Any gold or silver that is tied up and stored will be burning embers upon its owner until he spends it in the path of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (21384), al-Bazzār in his Musnad (3926), and al-Ṭabarānī (2/160).

12466 — Abū Bakrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Time has returned to its original form as it was on the day Allah created the heavens and the earth. The year consists of twelve months, four of which are sacred: three consecutive months—Dhū al-Qaʿdah, Dhū al-Ḥijjah, and Muḥarram—and Rajab of Muḍar, which lies between Jumādā and Shaʿbān.
He then asked, ‘What month is this?’
We replied, ‘Allah and His Messenger know best.’
He remained silent until we thought that he would give it another name.
He said, ‘Is it not Dhū al-Ḥijjah?’
We replied, ‘Yes.’
He asked, ‘What city is this?’
We replied, ‘Allah and His Messenger know best.’
He remained silent until we thought that he would give it another name.
He said, ‘Is it not the Sacred City?’
We replied, ‘Yes.’
He asked, ‘What day is this?’
We replied, ‘Allah and His Messenger know best.’
He remained silent until we thought that he would give it another name.
He said, ‘Is it not the Day of Sacrifice?’
We replied, ‘Yes, O Messenger of Allah.’
He said:
‘Your blood, your wealth, and your honor are sacred to one another, just as this day of yours is sacred, in this city of yours, during this month of yours.
You will meet your Lord, and He will question you concerning your deeds. Do not return after me to disbelief—or misguidance—striking one another’s necks.
Those who are present must convey this to those who are absent, for perhaps one to whom it is conveyed will understand it better than some who heard it directly.’”
He then asked:
“Have I conveyed the message?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4662) and Muslim (1679).

12467 — Qays narrated:
I heard al-Mustawrid, a member of Banū Fihr, say that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By Allah, this world in comparison with the Hereafter is only like one of you placing this finger”—Yaḥyā pointed with his index finger—“into the sea. Let him consider how much it brings back.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2858).

12468 — Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed through the marketplace while entering from one of the elevated districts of Madinah, with the people gathered around him. He passed by a dead, small-eared goat, took hold of it by the ear, and asked:
“Which of you would like to have this for one dirham?”
They replied:
“We would not like to have it for anything. What would we do with it?”
He asked:
“Would you like it to belong to you?”
They replied:
“By Allah, even if it were alive, its small ears would be considered a defect. What then, when it is dead?”
He said:
“By Allah, this world is more insignificant to Allah than this is to you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2957).

12469 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
The verse:
“Unless you set out, He will punish you with a painful punishment,”
and the verses beginning:
“It was not proper for the people of Madinah…”
until the words:
“…for what they used to do,”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:120–121]
were abrogated by the following verse:
“It is not for the believers to go forth all together. Rather, a group from every section of them should go forth to gain understanding of the religion and warn their people when they return to them, so that they may beware.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:122]
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2505), and through him by al-Bayhaqī (9/47).

12470 — Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq narrated:
I looked at the feet of the polytheists above our heads while we were inside the cave and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, if one of them were to look down at his feet, he would see us beneath them.”
He replied:
“Abū Bakr, what do you think of two people whose third is Allah?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4663) and Muslim (2381). The wording is that of Muslim.
Abū Bakr’s statement:
“If one of them looked down at his feet, he would see us,”
indicates that the story of the spider weaving a web over the entrance of the cave is not authentic.

12471 — Abū Mūsā narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“One man fights for spoils, another fights to gain a reputation, and another fights so that his position may be seen. Which one is fighting in the path of Allah?”
He replied:
“Whoever fights so that the word of Allah may be supreme is fighting in the path of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3126) and Muslim (1904).
The words:
“That is better for you”
mean that striving in the path of Allah is better for you in this world and the Hereafter. In this world, there may be spoils, while in the Hereafter there is reward with Allah, the Exalted.

12472 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has guaranteed for the one who strives in His path, leaving his home for no reason other than striving in His path and affirming His words, that He will either admit him into Paradise or return him to his home with whatever reward or spoils he has gained.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in al-Jihād (974), al-Bukhārī (7463), and Muslim (1876:104).

12473 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
The verse:
“Those who believe in Allah and the Last Day do not ask your permission…”
was abrogated by the verse in Sūrat al-Nūr beginning:
“The believers are only those who believe in Allah…”
until the words:
“All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
 [Sūrat al-Nūr 24:62]
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2771), and through him by al-Bayhaqī (9/173–174).

12474 — Abū Saʿīd narrated:
While the Prophet ﷺ was distributing wealth, ʿAbdullāh ibn Dhī al-Khuwayṣirah al-Tamīmī came and said:
“Be just, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Woe to you! Who would be just if I were not just?”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“Permit me to strike his neck.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Leave him. He will have companions whose prayer will make one of you consider his own prayer insignificant, and whose fasting will make one of you consider his own fasting insignificant. They will pass out of the religion as an arrow passes through its target.
The arrow’s feathers will be examined and nothing will be found upon them. Its point will be examined and nothing will be found upon it. Its shaft will then be examined and nothing will be found upon it, and finally its notch will be examined and nothing will be found upon it, because it passed so quickly through the dung and blood.
Their sign will be a man whose one hand—or one of whose breasts—resembles a woman’s breast, or a piece of flesh that shakes. They will emerge at a time when the people are divided.”
Abū Saʿīd said:
“I testify that I heard this from the Prophet ﷺ, and I testify that ʿAlī fought and killed them while I was with him. The man was brought forward exactly according to the description given by the Prophet ﷺ.”
He said:
The verse:
“Among them are those who criticize you concerning the charities”
was revealed concerning him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3610, 6933) and Muslim (1064:148).

12475 — ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Khiyār narrated that two men told him that they came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asking for charity.
He looked them over carefully and saw that they were strong, so he said:
“I will give you some if you wish, but neither a wealthy person nor a strong person capable of earning has any rightful share in it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1633), al-Nasāʾī (2598), and Aḥmad (17972).
Abū Dāwūd added:
“This occurred during the Farewell Pilgrimage while he was distributing charity.”
Its chain is authentic.

12476 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Charity is not lawful for a wealthy person or a strong, able-bodied person.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (2597), Ibn Mājah (1839), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (3290).
The words:
“And the needy”
refer to those who do not possess enough to meet their needs and make them self-sufficient.

12477 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The needy person is not the one who goes around asking people and is turned away after receiving one or two morsels or one or two dates.”
They asked:
“Who, then, is the needy person, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The one who does not possess enough to make him self-sufficient, whose condition is not noticed so that people give him charity, and who does not stand and ask people.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Ṣifat al-Nabī ﷺ (1713), al-Bukhārī (1479), and Muslim (1039).
The words:
“Those employed to administer it”
refer to the collectors, agents, and workers appointed over zakāh. They may receive payment from it in return for their work even if they are wealthy.

12478 — Ibn al-Sāʿidī al-Mālikī narrated:
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb appointed me to collect zakāh. When I completed the work and delivered it to him, he ordered that I be given wages.
I said:
“I only worked for Allah, and my reward is with Allah.”
He replied:
“Take what you have been given. I performed similar work during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he assigned me wages. I said what you have said, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told me:
‘When you are given something without asking for it, take it, eat from it, and give some in charity.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1045:112) and al-Bukhārī (7163).
As for the relatives of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for whom charity is forbidden, they may not serve as zakāh collectors and receive wages from it, as shown in the following hadith:

12479 — ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ibn Rabīʿah ibn al-Ḥārith narrated:
Rabīʿah ibn al-Ḥārith and al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib met and said:
“By Allah, why do we not send these two young men”—meaning me and al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās—“to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that they may speak to him? He could appoint them over the collection of charity, and they could deliver what other collectors deliver and receive what they receive.”
While they were discussing this, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib came and stood beside them. They mentioned the matter to him, and he said:
“Do not do it. By Allah, he will not appoint them.”
Rabīʿah ibn al-Ḥārith turned toward him and said:
“By Allah, you say this only because you are jealous of us. You obtained the honor of becoming the son-in-law of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, yet we were not jealous of you.”
ʿAlī said:
“Send them.”
They departed, while ʿAlī lay down.
After the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed Ẓuhr, we reached his room before him and stood beside it until he came. He took us by the ears and said:
“Bring out what you are concealing in your hearts.”
He entered, and we entered with him. He was staying that day with Zaynab bint Jaḥsh.
Each of us wanted the other to begin speaking. Finally, one of us said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you are the most dutiful and the best in maintaining family ties. We have reached marriageable age, so we came to ask you to appoint us over some of these charities. We would deliver to you what other workers deliver and receive what they receive.”
He remained silent for a long time until we were about to speak again. Zaynab gestured to us from behind the curtain not to speak to him.
He then said:
“Charity is not suitable for the family of Muḥammad. It is only the impurities removed from people’s wealth. Call Maḥmiyah for me”—he was responsible for the one-fifth—“and Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
They came, and he said to Maḥmiyah:
“Marry your daughter to this young man,”
meaning al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās, so he married her to him.
He said to Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith:
“Marry your daughter to this young man,”
meaning me, so he married her to me.
He said to Maḥmiyah:
“Give such-and-such as the bridal gifts for both of them from the one-fifth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1072).
This ruling applies when military campaigns are taking place and their rightful share of the one-fifth reaches them and sufficiently meets their needs. If this source of support ceases, and some of them become needy and find nothing except zakāh wealth, they may receive from it. This was the view of al-Qāḍī Yaʿqūb among the Ḥanbalīs, Abū Yūsuf among the Ḥanafīs, al-Iṣṭakhrī among the Shāfiʿīs, and it was the view chosen by Shaykh al-Islām Ibn Taymiyyah in al-Fatāwā al-Kubrā (5/374).
Some scholars even said that giving zakāh to them takes priority over giving it to others because of the instruction of the Prophet ﷺ to treat them well.
The words:
“Those whose hearts are to be reconciled”
refer to the fourth category entitled to receive zakāh.
They are divided into several categories:
Disbelievers whose acceptance of Islam is hoped for because they have shown indications of being inclined toward it.
Disbelievers whose harm is feared.
Those who have entered Islam but whose faith remains weak. They are given from zakāh to strengthen them in their religion.
Tribal leaders and distinguished people who have entered Islam. They are given from zakāh to attract their people and encourage others like them to enter Islam.
As for those who said that the share of those whose hearts are to be reconciled has ended and no longer applies, that may have been true during their own time, when Islam was dominant and the Muslims possessed strength and protection.
Today, however, there may be an even greater need for this share to reduce pressure upon Muslims due to the dominance of disbelievers and polytheists, especially in the case of those who newly enter Islam or whose acceptance of Islam is hoped for. Aḥmad said:
“They are given if the Muslims need to do so.”

12480 — Ibn Shihāb narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook the campaign of conquest—the conquest of Makkah. He then departed with the Muslims accompanying him, and they fought at Ḥunayn. Allah granted victory to His religion and the Muslims.
That day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah one hundred camels, then another hundred, and then another hundred.
Ibn Shihāb said:
Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib narrated to me that Ṣafwān said:
“By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave me what he gave me while he was the person I hated most. He continued giving to me until he became the person I loved most.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2313).

12481 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave gifts to a group of people while I was sitting among them. He left one man without giving him anything, though that man was the one I admired most among them.
I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and spoke privately to him, saying:
“O Messenger of Allah, why have you left out so-and-so? By Allah, I believe him to be a believer.”
He replied:
“Or merely a Muslim?”
I remained silent briefly, but what I knew concerning him overcame me, so I repeated:
“O Messenger of Allah, why have you left out so-and-so? By Allah, I believe him to be a believer.”
He replied:
“Or merely a Muslim?”
I remained silent again, but what I knew concerning him overcame me, so I repeated the question a third time.
He replied:
“Or merely a Muslim?”
He then said:
“I may give to one man even though another is more beloved to me, because I fear that the first may be thrown face-first into the Fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1478) and Muslim (150:131). The wording is that of Muslim.

12482 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
ʿAlī, while in Yemen, sent a piece of gold still mixed with earth to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ divided it among four men: al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis al-Ḥanẓalī, ʿUyaynah ibn Badr al-Fazārī, ʿAlqamah ibn ʿUlāthah al-ʿĀmirī of Banū Kilāb, and Zayd al-Khayr al-Ṭāʾī of Banū Nabhān.
Quraysh became angry and said:
“Do you give to the leaders of Najd and leave us?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“I only did this to reconcile their hearts.”
A man with a thick beard, prominent cheekbones, sunken eyes, a protruding forehead, and a shaved head came and said:
“Fear Allah, Muḥammad.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Who would obey Allah if I disobeyed Him? He entrusts me with the people of the earth, yet you do not trust me?”
The man then turned away. One of the people—thought to have been Khālid ibn al-Walīd—asked permission to kill him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“From the descendants and kind of this man will emerge a people who recite the Qur’an, but it will not pass beyond their throats. They will kill the people of Islam and leave the idol worshippers alone. They will pass out of Islam as an arrow passes through its target. If I live to encounter them, I will kill them as the people of ʿĀd were killed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4667) and Muslim (1064).
The words:
“For freeing slaves”
refer to giving zakāh to a slave under a contract of emancipation so that he may complete his payments, or using it directly to purchase and free a slave.

12483 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are three people whom Allah has undertaken to help: the one who strives in the path of Allah, the contracted slave who sincerely wishes to complete his payments, and the person who marries seeking chastity.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1655), al-Nasāʾī (3120, 3218), Ibn Mājah (2518), and Aḥmad (7410).
The words:
“Those burdened with debt”
refer to those who incurred debts for themselves in matters not involving disobedience, or those who assumed financial liabilities for others in acts of kindness, reconciliation, and similar matters. They may receive from zakāh, as established in authentic reports, even if they are otherwise wealthy.

12484 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
During the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, a man suffered a loss in fruit that he had purchased, and his debts became numerous.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Give charity to him.”
The people gave charity to him, but it was not enough to repay his debt in full.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to his creditors:
“Take whatever you find, and you are entitled to nothing beyond that.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1556).

12485 — Qabīṣah ibn Mukhāriq al-Hilālī narrated:
I assumed a financial liability to reconcile people, so I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asking for assistance with it.
He said:
“Remain here until charity comes to us, and we will order that you be given from it.”
He then said:
“Qabīṣah, asking people for financial help is not lawful except for one of three people: a man who has assumed a financial liability, for whom asking is lawful until he obtains enough to discharge it, after which he must stop asking;
a man whose wealth has been destroyed by a calamity, for whom asking is lawful until he obtains enough to support himself—or he said, enough to meet his needs;
and a man afflicted by severe poverty, concerning whom three sensible men from his people testify, ‘So-and-so has been afflicted by poverty.’ Asking is lawful for him until he obtains enough to support himself—or he said, enough to meet his needs.
Any asking beyond these cases, Qabīṣah, is unlawful wealth consumed by its recipient.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1044).

12486 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Charity is not lawful for a wealthy person except in five cases: one employed to administer it, one fighting in the path of Allah, a wealthy person who purchases it with his own money, a poor person who receives it as charity and then gives it as a gift to a wealthy person, or a debtor.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1636), Ibn Mājah (1841), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (2374) and al-Ḥākim (1/407).

12487 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
During the expedition of Tabūk, a man said in a gathering:
“We have never seen anyone more eager to fill their stomachs, more lying in speech, or more cowardly in battle than these reciters of ours.”
A man in the gathering replied:
“You have lied. Rather, you are a hypocrite. I will certainly inform the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
The statement reached the Prophet ﷺ, and Qur’an was revealed concerning it.
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar said:
I saw him clinging to the strap of the she-camel of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while the stones struck his feet, saying:
“O Messenger of Allah, we were only talking idly and joking.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued saying:
“Was it Allah, His verses, and His Messenger that you were mocking? Do not make excuses. You have disbelieved after your faith.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (11/543–544) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1829–1830).

12488 — Kaʿb narrated:
Makhshī ibn Ḥumayyir said:
“I wish that I could settle the matter by having every one of you struck one hundred times, provided that we escape having Qur’an revealed concerning us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir:
“Catch up with the people, for they have destroyed themselves. Ask them what they said. If they deny and conceal it, tell them, ‘No, you said such-and-such.’”
ʿAmmār caught up with them and said what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had instructed him to say. They then came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ offering excuses.
Makhshī ibn Ḥumayyir said:
“O Messenger of Allah, my name and my father’s name held me back.”
Allah, the Exalted, revealed concerning them:
“Do not make excuses. You have disbelieved after your faith. If We pardon a group among you, We will punish another group.”
The one whom Allah pardoned was Makhshī ibn Ḥumayyir. He changed his name to ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and asked Allah to allow him to be killed as a martyr in such a way that the place of his death would remain unknown. He was killed on the day of al-Yamāmah. Neither the place where he died nor the person who killed him was known, and no trace of him was found.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1831).

12489 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“You will certainly follow the ways of those before you, handspan by handspan and cubit by cubit, until even if they entered the hole of a lizard, you would follow them into it.”
We asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, do you mean the Jews and Christians?”
He replied:
“Who else?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7320) and Muslim (2669).

12490 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The Hour will not be established until my nation adopts the ways of the generations before it, handspan by handspan and cubit by cubit.”
It was asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, like the Persians and Byzantines?”
He replied:
“Who else among the people could they be?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7319).

12491 — Abū Mūsā narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The believer is to another believer like a building, each part strengthening the other.”
He then interlaced his fingers.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2446) and Muslim (2585).

12492 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You see the believers in their mutual mercy, love, and compassion as one body. When one limb suffers, the rest of the body responds with sleeplessness and fever.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6011) and Muslim (2586). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12493 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are two gardens of silver, whose vessels and everything within them are of silver, and two gardens of gold, whose vessels and everything within them are of gold. Nothing stands between the people and their looking upon their Lord except the veil of majesty over His Face in the Garden of Eden.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4878) and Muslim (180).
The words:
“The pleasure of Allah is greater”
mean that Allah’s pleasure with them is greater, more glorious, and more magnificent than all the bliss they enjoy, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12494 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah will say to the people of Paradise, ‘People of Paradise!’ They will reply, ‘Here we are at Your service, our Lord, and all goodness is in Your hands.’ He will ask, ‘Are you pleased?’ They will reply, ‘Why should we not be pleased when You have given us what You have not given to anyone else among Your creation?’ He will say, ‘I will give you something better than that.’ They will ask, ‘Our Lord, what could be better than that?’ He will say, ‘I bestow My pleasure upon you, and I will never be displeased with you afterward.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6549) and Muslim (2829).

12495 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the people of Paradise enter Paradise, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say, ‘Do you desire anything more so that I may increase you?’ They will reply, ‘Our Lord, what could be beyond what You have already given us?’ He will say, ‘My pleasure is greater.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7439) and al-Ḥākim (1/82).

12496 — Kaʿb narrated:
When Qur’anic revelation came mentioning the hypocrites and what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had said, al-Jallās remarked:
“By Allah, if this man is truthful, then we are worse than donkeys.”
ʿUmayr ibn Saʿd heard him and said:
“By Allah, Jallās, you are among the people most beloved to me, the one whose past conduct I regard most favorably—or the one whom I most dislike seeing harmed. Yet you have uttered a statement which, if I report it, will disgrace you, and if I conceal it, will destroy me. One of those two consequences is more grievous to me than the other.”
ʿUmayr went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and told him what al-Jallās had said. Al-Jallās swore by Allah that he had not said what ʿUmayr reported and claimed that ʿUmayr had lied about him.
Allah then revealed:
“They swear by Allah that they did not say it, but they certainly uttered the word of disbelief and disbelieved after professing Islam.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1843).

12497 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting in the shade of a tree when he said:
“A man will soon come to you who will look at you with the eyes of a devil. When he comes, do not speak to him.”
It was not long before a blue-eyed man appeared. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ called him and asked:
“Why do you and your companions insult me?”
The man went and brought his companions. They swore by Allah that they had neither said nor done anything, until the Prophet ﷺ left them alone.
Allah then revealed:
“They swear by Allah that they did not say it, but they certainly said…”
and He described them all until the end of the verse.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/571).

12498 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“Recognize the hypocrites by three qualities: when they speak, they lie; when they promise, they break their promise; and when they make a covenant, they betray it.”
Allah revealed confirmation of this in the verses:
“Among them are those who made a covenant with Allah: ‘If He gives us from His bounty…’”
until His words:
“…because they used to lie.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (11/583) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1846).
It is narrated from Abū Umāmah al-Bāhilī, from Thaʿlabah ibn Ḥāṭib, that he said to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Ask Allah to grant me wealth.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Woe to you, Thaʿlabah! A small amount for which you can give thanks is better than a great amount whose obligations you cannot bear.”
He later returned and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, ask Allah to grant me wealth.”
He replied:
“Woe to you, Thaʿlabah! Are you not content to be like the Messenger of Allah ﷺ? By Allah, if I asked Allah to cause the mountains to flow with gold and silver for me, they would do so.”
He returned again and said:
“Ask Allah to grant me wealth. By Allah, if Allah gives me wealth, I will certainly give everyone who possesses a right his due.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Allah, grant Thaʿlabah wealth. O Allah, grant Thaʿlabah wealth. O Allah, grant Thaʿlabah wealth.”
He acquired sheep, and they multiplied as rapidly as worms, until the streets of Madinah became too narrow for them, so he moved them outside the city.
He continued attending the prayers with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and then returning to his flock. They continued multiplying until the grazing lands around Madinah were insufficient, so he moved farther away. He would then attend only the Friday prayer with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ before returning to them.
The flock continued multiplying, so he moved still farther away and abandoned both the Friday prayer and the congregational prayers. He would meet passing travelers and ask:
“What news do you have? What has happened among the people?”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed to His Messenger ﷺ:
“Take charity from their wealth to purify and cleanse them through it, and pray for them. Indeed, your prayer is a source of reassurance for them. Allah is All-Hearing, All-Knowing.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:103]
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed two men to collect zakāh: one from the Anṣār and one from Banū Sulaym. He wrote for them the prescribed rates and ages of animals due as zakāh, commanded them to collect from the people, and instructed them to pass by Thaʿlabah and take the zakāh due upon his wealth.
They did so until they came to Thaʿlabah and read the letter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to him.
He said:
“Collect from the people first, and when you have finished, return to me.”
They did so, and when they returned he said:
“By Allah, this is nothing but the sister of the jizyah.”
They then departed and returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed to His Messenger ﷺ:
“Among them are those who made a covenant with Allah: ‘If He gives us from His bounty…’”
until His words:
“…because they used to lie.”
A man from the Anṣār who was related to Thaʿlabah mounted an animal and went to him, saying:
“Woe to you, Thaʿlabah! You are ruined. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has revealed such-and-such Qur’an concerning you.”
Thaʿlabah came, throwing dust upon his head, weeping and saying:
“O Messenger of Allah! O Messenger of Allah!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not accept his zakāh until Allah took the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in death.
He then came to Abū Bakr after the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“Abū Bakr, you know my standing among my people and my position with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so accept it from me.”
Abū Bakr refused to accept it.
He later came to ʿUmar, who also refused to accept it. Then he came to ʿUthmān, who likewise refused to accept it. Thaʿlabah died during the caliphate of ʿUthmān.
Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/578–580) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1847–1849).
This is a fabricated story. For that reason, the leading hadith scholars criticized it. Its chain revolves around ʿAlī ibn Yazīd al-Alhānī, who is extremely weak according to the majority of hadith scholars.
Al-Bukhārī said concerning him:
“His hadiths are objectionable, and he is weak.”
Al-Dāraquṭnī said:
“He is abandoned.”
I have mentioned this fabricated report only because it is widely circulated among preachers and commentators. The Book of Allah should have been kept free from association with such an objectionable report. I have included it solely to clarify its weakness and objectionable nature according to the scholars. Allah is the One Who grants success.

12499 — Abū Masʿūd narrated:
When we were commanded to give charity, we would carry loads in return for wages. Abū ʿAqīl came with half a ṣāʿ, while another person brought more than that.
The hypocrites said:
“Allah has no need of this man’s charity, and the other man only did what he did to show off.”
The following verse was then revealed:
“Those who criticize the believers who give charity voluntarily and those who can find nothing to give except the result of their own effort…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4668) and Muslim (1018). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
In another narration recorded by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4669), through another route from Sulaymān, the wording states:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would command us to give charity. One of us would labor until he could bring a mudd, although today one of them possesses one hundred thousand.”
It appears that he was referring indirectly to himself.

12500 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“Those who criticize the believers who give charity voluntarily”:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf brought forty ounces of gold to the Prophet ﷺ, while a man from the Anṣār brought a ṣāʿ of food.
Some of the hypocrites said:
“By Allah, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān only brought what he brought to show off.”
They also said:
“Allah and His Messenger have no need of this ṣāʿ.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/378) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1850).

12501 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
When ʿAbdullāh died, his son ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked him to give him his shirt so that he could use it as his father’s shroud. The Prophet ﷺ gave it to him.
He then asked the Prophet ﷺ to perform the funeral prayer over him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood to pray, but ʿUmar stood and took hold of the garment of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, saying:
“O Messenger of Allah, will you pray over him when your Lord has forbidden you to pray over him?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Allah has only given me a choice. He said, ‘Seek forgiveness for them or do not seek forgiveness for them. Even if you seek forgiveness for them seventy times, Allah will never forgive them.’ I will therefore exceed seventy times.”
ʿUmar said:
“He is a hypocrite.”
Nevertheless, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the funeral prayer over him. Allah then revealed:
“Never perform the funeral prayer over any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:84]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4670) and Muslim (2400).

12502 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
When ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl died, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was called to perform the funeral prayer over him.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood, I quickly went to him and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, will you pray over Ibn Ubayy when he said such-and-such on such-and-such a day?”
I began listing his statements. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiled and said:
“Move away from me, ʿUmar.”
When I continued pressing him, he said:
“I was given a choice, and I have chosen. If I knew that he would be forgiven were I to exceed seventy, I would exceed it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the funeral prayer over him and then departed. Only a short time passed before the two verses from Barāʾah were revealed:
“Never perform the funeral prayer over any of them who dies…”
until His words:
“…while they were defiantly disobedient.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:84]
ʿUmar said:
“Afterward, I was amazed at my boldness toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Allah and His Messenger knew best.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4671).

12503 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The father of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy said to him:
“My son, ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for one of his garments so that I may be shrouded in it, and ask him to perform the funeral prayer over me.”
ʿAbdullāh said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you know the standing of ʿAbdullāh. He has instructed me to ask you for a garment in which we may shroud him and to ask you to perform the funeral prayer over him.”
The Prophet ﷺ gave him one of his garments and intended to perform the funeral prayer over him.
ʿUmar said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you know ʿAbdullāh and his hypocrisy. Will you pray over him when Allah has forbidden you to do so?”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Where?”
ʿUmar replied:
“Seek forgiveness for them or do not seek forgiveness for them.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Then I will exceed it.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Never perform the funeral prayer over any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:84]
Allah also revealed:
“It is the same for them whether you seek forgiveness for them or do not seek forgiveness for them. Allah will never forgive them. Indeed, Allah does not guide the defiantly disobedient people.”
 [Sūrat al-Munāfiqūn 63:6]
A man entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and prolonged his sitting. The Prophet ﷺ went out three times hoping that the man would follow him, but he did not.
ʿUmar entered and saw the man. He recognized from the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that he disliked the man’s prolonged sitting and said:
“Perhaps you have inconvenienced the Prophet ﷺ.”
The man understood and stood up.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“I stood up three times so that you would follow me, but you did not.”
The man said:
“O Messenger of Allah, perhaps you should appoint a doorkeeper. Your wives are not like other women, and this would be purer for their hearts.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Believers, do not enter the houses of the Prophet unless you are given permission for a meal, without waiting for it to be prepared. But when you are invited, enter, and when you have eaten, disperse without remaining for conversation. Indeed, that inconveniences the Prophet, but he is too shy to ask you to leave, while Allah is not shy of the truth. When you ask his wives for something, ask them from behind a partition. That is purer for your hearts and their hearts. It is not permissible for you to inconvenience the Messenger of Allah, nor ever to marry his wives after him. Indeed, that would be an enormous matter in the sight of Allah.”
 [Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:53]
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for ʿUmar and informed him of this revelation.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ also consulted Abū Bakr and ʿUmar concerning the prisoners.
Abū Bakr said:
“O Messenger of Allah, preserve the lives of your people and accept ransom from them, using it as assistance.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“Kill them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Had both of you agreed upon one opinion, we would not have opposed you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ followed Abū Bakr’s opinion. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land. You desire the temporary gains of this world, while Allah desires the Hereafter. Allah is Almighty, All-Wise.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:67]
He said:
The verses were then revealed:
“We certainly created man from an extract of clay…”
until the end of the passage.
ʿUmar said:
“Blessed is Allah, the Best of creators.”
The verse was then revealed:
“Blessed is Allah, the Best of creators.”
 [Sūrat al-Muʾminūn 23:14]
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/438–439) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/288).

12504 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The fire kindled by the children of Adam is one part out of seventy parts of the Fire of Hell.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, this worldly fire would have been sufficient.”
He replied:
“The Fire of Hell has been made sixty-nine parts hotter than it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb Jahannam (1872), al-Bukhārī (3265), and Muslim (2834) through another route from Abū al-Zinād with similar wording. Muslim’s version adds:
“Every one of those parts is as hot as this fire.”

12505 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAmr al-Sulamī and Ḥujr ibn Ḥujr al-Kalāʿī narrated:
We came to al-ʿIrbāḍ ibn Sāriyah, who was among those concerning whom the following verse was revealed:
“Nor is there blame upon those who came to you so that you might provide them with mounts, but you said, ‘I cannot find anything upon which to carry you.’ They turned away while their eyes overflowed with tears out of grief that they could find nothing to spend.”
We greeted him and said:
“We have come to visit you and to acquire knowledge from you.”
Al-ʿIrbāḍ said:
One morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in the dawn prayer. He then turned toward us and delivered a deeply moving exhortation that caused the eyes to shed tears and the hearts to tremble.
Someone said:
“O Messenger of Allah, this sounds like the exhortation of one bidding farewell. What do you entrust us with?”
He replied:
“I instruct you to fear Allah and to hear and obey, even if an Abyssinian slave is appointed over you. Whoever among you lives after me will see much disagreement.
You must therefore adhere to my Sunnah and the Sunnah of the rightly guided caliphs. Hold firmly to it and bite onto it with your molar teeth.
Beware of newly introduced matters, for every newly introduced matter is an innovation, and every innovation is misguidance.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4607), Aḥmad (17145), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5) and al-Ḥākim (1/97).

12506 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik said concerning the time when he remained behind from the expedition of Tabūk:
“By Allah, after guiding me, Allah bestowed no blessing upon me greater than my truthfulness to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. I did not lie to him and thereby perish as those who lied perished when revelation came:
‘They will swear by Allah to you when you return to them…’
until His words:
‘…the defiantly disobedient people.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4673) and Muslim (2769).

12507 — Samurah ibn Jundub narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to us:
“Last night, two visitors came to me and took me away. We came to a city built with bricks of gold and bricks of silver. Men met us whose bodies were half as beautiful as anything you could ever see and half as ugly as anything you could ever see.
The two visitors said to them, ‘Go and immerse yourselves in that river.’ They entered it and then returned to us. The ugliness had disappeared from them, and they had become completely beautiful.
The two said to me, ‘This is the Garden of Eden, and that is your dwelling.’
They said, ‘As for the people who were half beautiful and half ugly, they mixed righteous deeds with evil ones, and Allah pardoned them.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4674).

12508 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“Others acknowledged their sins, having mixed a righteous deed with another that was evil”:
Ten men remained behind from the Prophet ﷺ during the expedition of Tabūk.
When the return of the Prophet ﷺ drew near, seven of them tied themselves to the pillars of the mosque.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would pass by them when returning from the mosque. When he saw them, he asked:
“Who are these people who have tied themselves to the pillars?”
They replied:
“This is Abū Lubābah and some of his companions who remained behind from you, O Messenger of Allah. They have tied themselves up until the Prophet ﷺ releases and excuses them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“By Allah, I will neither release nor excuse them until Allah Himself releases and excuses them. They turned away from me and remained behind from campaigning with the Muslims.”
When this reached them, they said:
“By Allah, we will not release ourselves until Allah Himself releases us.”
Allah then revealed:
“Others acknowledged their sins, having mixed a righteous deed with another that was evil. Perhaps Allah will turn to them in mercy.”
When “perhaps” is used by Allah, it indicates certainty. Indeed, He is the Accepter of repentance, the Most Merciful.
When this verse was revealed, the Prophet ﷺ sent for them, released them, and accepted their excuse.
They brought their wealth and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, this is our wealth. Give it in charity on our behalf and seek forgiveness for us.”
He replied:
“I have not been commanded to take your wealth.”
Allah then revealed:
“Take charity from their wealth to purify and cleanse them through it, and pray for them,”
meaning: seek forgiveness for them,
“Indeed, your prayer is a source of reassurance for them.”
The Prophet ﷺ then accepted from them and sought forgiveness for them.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (11/651–652) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1872, 1875).
It has also been said that Abū Lubābah tied himself up during the campaign against Banū Qurayẓah, as Mujāhid mentioned and as previously discussed in the commentary on the words of Allah:
“Believers, do not betray Allah and the Messenger, nor knowingly betray your trusts.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:27]
Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr preferred the first view.

12509 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
Six men were among those who remained behind from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during Tabūk: Abū Lubābah, Aws ibn Khidhām, Thaʿlabah ibn Wadīʿah, Kaʿb ibn Mālik, Murārah ibn al-Rabīʿ, and Hilāl ibn Umayyah.
Abū Lubābah, Aws, and Thaʿlabah came and tied themselves to the pillars. They brought their wealth and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, take it. This is what held us back from you.”
He said:
“I will not release them until a decision comes.”
The Qur’an was then revealed:
“They mixed a righteous deed with another that was evil…”
until His words:
“Indeed, your prayer is a source of reassurance for them.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Nuʿaym in Maʿrifat al-Ṣaḥābah (1/312–313).
The words:
“Pray for them. Indeed, your prayer is a source of reassurance for them”
apply generally to everyone who brought his charity to the Prophet ﷺ. He would supplicate and invoke blessings for them, as established in the authentic hadith:

12510 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā narrated:
Whenever people brought their charity to the Prophet ﷺ, he would say:
“O Allah, bestow Your blessings upon the family of so-and-so.”
My father brought his charity to him, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
“O Allah, bestow Your blessings upon the family of Abū Awfā.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1497) and Muslim (1078).

12511 — Abū Mūsā narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, extends His hand during the night so that the sinner of the day may repent, and He extends His hand during the day so that the sinner of the night may repent. This will continue until the sun rises from the west.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2759).
Regarding the words:
“And He receives charities”:

12512 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A man gives charity from lawful earnings—and Allah accepts only what is pure—so the Most Merciful, Blessed and Exalted, receives it with His hand and nurtures it just as one of you raises his young horse, servant, or weaned camel.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (931) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ, as cited in Majmaʿ al-Baḥrayn (1412).

12513 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Those who established a mosque to cause harm”:
They were men from the Anṣār who constructed a mosque. Abū ʿĀmir said to them:
“Build your mosque and prepare whatever strength and weapons you can. I am going to Caesar, the emperor of the Byzantines, and I will bring a Byzantine army to expel Muḥammad and his Companions.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1878).
This Abū ʿĀmir was the monk who adopted Christianity during the pre-Islamic period before the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Madinah. He had studied the knowledge of the People of the Book.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Madinah, the Muslims gathered around him, and Islam gained prominence, Abū ʿĀmir openly displayed hostility and hatred. He encouraged his followers from Madinah to build this mosque beside the Mosque of Qubāʾ so that it could serve as a center where they could secretly consult one another.
He himself traveled to Syria seeking assistance against the Prophet ﷺ. The Byzantine ruler promised him an army and equipment.
When they finished building their mosque, they came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“We have completed the construction of our mosque and would like you to pray in it and invoke blessings for us.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“We are preparing to travel, but when we return, Allah willing.”
That is, from Tabūk.
When he returned from Tabūk toward Madinah and only one day or part of a day remained before reaching it, revelation came to him:
“Never stand in it. A mosque founded upon piety from the first day is more worthy for you to stand in. Within it are men who love to purify themselves, and Allah loves those who purify themselves.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then sent men to demolish it.
Regarding the words:
“A mosque founded upon piety from the first day”:
It has been said that this refers to the Mosque of Qubāʾ, as stated in the authentic report:

12514 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated, within the account of the migration of the Prophet ﷺ:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained among Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf for more than ten nights. He founded the mosque that was established upon piety, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed within it.
He then mounted his riding animal and continued on while the people walked beside him until it knelt at the site of the Mosque of the Messenger ﷺ in Madinah.
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3906).
It has also been said that the verse refers to the Prophet’s Mosque in Madinah, as stated in another authentic report:

12515 — Abū Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Saʿīd al-Khudrī passed by me, and I asked him:
“How did you hear your father describe the mosque that was founded upon piety?”
He replied:
“My father said: I entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the home of one of his wives and asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, which of the two mosques is the one founded upon piety?’
He took a handful of pebbles, struck the ground with them, and said:
‘It is this mosque of yours.’
He meant the mosque of Madinah.
I said:
“I testify that I heard your father narrate it in this manner.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1398).

12516 — Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
Two men disagreed during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning the mosque founded upon piety.
One said:
“It is the Mosque of the Messenger.”
The other said:
“It is the Mosque of Qubāʾ.”
They came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked him, and he replied:
“It is this mosque of mine.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22805), Ibn Ḥibbān (1604), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (6/254).
The two reports may be reconciled by saying that when the mosque founded upon piety is specifically described as having been founded “from the first day,” it refers to the Mosque of Qubāʾ, because it was built more than ten days before the mosque of Madinah. When the expression “the mosque founded upon piety” is used without that qualification, it refers to the mosque of Madinah.
Many scholars reconciled the reports in this or a similar manner.
Regarding the words:
“Within it are men who love to purify themselves, and Allah loves those who purify themselves”:
The words of Allah:
“Within it are men who love to purify themselves”
may refer to purification with water or with stones after relieving oneself. This is unlike the hypocrites, who did not purify themselves but entered the mosques while carrying both outward and inward impurity. Allah, the Exalted, thus distinguished the believers from the hypocrites through purity.

12517 — Abū Umāmah narrated:
A man said:
“O Messenger of Allah, permit me to wander through the land in religious seclusion.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“The religious journeying of my nation is striving in the path of Allah.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2486), al-Ḥākim (2/73), and al-Bayhaqī (9/161).

12518 — Al-Musayyib ibn Ḥazn narrated:
When death approached Abū Ṭālib, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to him and found Abū Jahl and ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah with him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Uncle, say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ a statement by which I may testify on your behalf before Allah.”
Abū Jahl and ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah said:
“Abū Ṭālib, will you abandon the religion of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued presenting the testimony of faith to him, while they continued repeating their statement, until the final words Abū Ṭālib spoke to them were:
“He remains upon the religion of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
He refused to say:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By Allah, I will continue seeking forgiveness for you unless I am forbidden from doing so.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“It is not for the Prophet and those who believe to seek forgiveness for the polytheists, even if they are close relatives, after it has become clear to them that they are companions of the Blazing Fire.”
Allah, the Exalted, also revealed concerning Abū Ṭālib, addressing the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“You cannot guide whomever you love, but Allah guides whomever He wills, and He knows best those who are guided.”
 [Sūrat al-Qaṣaṣ 28:56]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4675) and Muslim (24).

12519 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
At the time of his uncle’s death, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him:
“Say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ and I will testify on your behalf by it on the Day of Resurrection.”
He replied:
“Were it not that Quraysh would reproach me and say that fear of death caused me to say it, I would comfort your eyes by uttering it.”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“You cannot guide whomever you love, but Allah guides whomever He wills, and He knows best those who are guided.”
 [Sūrat al-Qaṣaṣ 28:56]
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (25:42).

12520 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I asked my Lord for permission to seek forgiveness for my mother, but He did not grant me permission. I then asked Him for permission to visit her grave, and He granted me permission.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (976).

12521 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
They continued seeking forgiveness for their polytheist relatives until this verse was revealed. When it was revealed, they stopped seeking forgiveness for those among them who had died. They were not forbidden from seeking forgiveness for those still alive until they died.
Allah then revealed:
“Abraham’s seeking of forgiveness for his father was only because of a promise he had made to him…”
This means that Abraham sought forgiveness for him while he was alive. When he died, Abraham stopped seeking forgiveness for him.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (12/23–24) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/1893).

12522 — ʿAlī narrated:
I heard a man seeking forgiveness for his parents even though they were polytheists. I asked him:
“Do you seek forgiveness for them while they are polytheists?”
He replied:
“Did Abraham not seek forgiveness for his father?”
I came to the Prophet ﷺ and mentioned that to him. The following verse was then revealed:
“Abraham’s seeking of forgiveness for his father was only because of a promise he had made to him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3101), al-Nasāʾī (2036), Aḥmad (1085), and al-Ḥākim (2/335).
The words:
“A promise he had made to him”
mean a promise made to his father. Abraham, peace be upon him, promised his father that he would seek forgiveness for him in the hope that he might embrace Islam. He sought forgiveness for him while his father was still alive in polytheism.
The words:
“But when it became clear to him that he was an enemy of Allah, he disassociated himself from him. Indeed, Abraham was tenderhearted and forbearing”
refer to what occurred after his father died upon disbelief, as established in the authentic hadith:

12523 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Abraham will meet his father Āzar on the Day of Resurrection, and Āzar’s face will be covered with darkness and dust.
Abraham will say to him, ‘Did I not tell you not to disobey me?’
His father will reply, ‘Today I will not disobey you.’
Abraham will say, ‘My Lord, You promised me that You would not disgrace me on the Day they are resurrected. What disgrace could be greater than the fate of my distant father?’
Allah, the Exalted, will say, ‘I have forbidden Paradise to the disbelievers.’
It will then be said, ‘Abraham, what is beneath your feet?’
He will look and see a bloodstained male hyena. It will be seized by its legs and cast into the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3350).

12524 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was asked:
“Tell us about the Expedition of Hardship.”
He replied:
“We departed for Tabūk during intense heat. We stopped at a place where extreme thirst afflicted us until we thought our necks would be severed from it.
“A man would go searching for water and would not return until we feared that his neck had been severed by thirst. A man would even slaughter his camel, squeeze the liquid from its stomach contents and drink it, and place the remainder upon his abdomen.”
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah has accustomed you to receiving goodness when you supplicate, so supplicate for us.”
He asked:
“Would you like that?”
Abū Bakr replied:
“Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ raised his hands and did not lower them until a cloud shaded them and poured down rain. They filled all the vessels they possessed. We then looked around and found that the rain had not passed beyond the army.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (1383), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1841), al-Ḥākim (1/159), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/231), all through Ibn Wahb, who said: ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith informed us, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Hilāl, from ʿUtbah ibn Abī ʿUtbah, from Nāfiʿ ibn Jubayr, from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās, who narrated it. Its chain of transmission is authentic.
Regarding the words:
“Then He turned to them in mercy,”
the mention of repentance is repeated after Allah stated at the beginning of the verse:
“Allah has certainly turned in mercy to the Prophet, the Emigrants, and the Anṣār.”
The first mention of repentance applies to the Prophet ﷺ and all his Companions who departed for the Expedition of Hardship. The second applies specifically to those who considered remaining behind or turning back because of its severity but then continued and did not remain behind.
The words:
“Indeed, He is Most Kind and Merciful to them”
conclude the verse with glad tidings for all those who departed on the Expedition of Hardship that Allah accepted their repentance. Whoever receives Allah’s acceptance of repentance will never be punished by Him.
The words:
“And also the three whose matter was deferred”
refer to Kaʿb ibn Mālik, Murārah ibn al-Rabīʿ al-ʿAmrī, and Hilāl ibn Umayyah al-Wāqifī. Their story is given in detail in the following hadith:

12525 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik narrated:
I never remained behind from any campaign undertaken by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ except the expedition of Tabūk. I had also remained behind from the Battle of Badr, but no one who remained behind from Badr was reproached. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had set out only intending to intercept the caravan of Quraysh, until Allah brought the Muslims and their enemy together unexpectedly.
I was present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Night of al-ʿAqabah when we pledged ourselves firmly to Islam. I would not exchange my participation in that night for participation at Badr, even though Badr is more widely remembered among the people.
As for my situation, I had never been stronger or more financially able than I was when I remained behind from that expedition.
By Allah, I had never possessed two riding animals at the same time before that expedition, but I possessed two for it.
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended a campaign, he would conceal its destination by appearing to intend another, except for this expedition. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook it during intense heat, facing a distant journey, a barren wilderness, and a numerous enemy. He therefore made the matter clear to the Muslims so that they could prepare adequately, and he informed them of the destination he intended.
The Muslims accompanying the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were very numerous, and no comprehensive register contained all their names—meaning no official military record existed.
Kaʿb said:
Any man who wished to remain absent thought that his absence would go unnoticed unless revelation from Allah came concerning him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook this expedition at a time when the fruits had ripened and the shade had become pleasant, and I was strongly inclined toward remaining among them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims began preparing. Each morning I would go out intending to prepare with them, but I would return without accomplishing anything, saying to myself:
“I am fully capable of preparing whenever I wish.”
I continued delaying until the people became fully committed to departure. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims departed in the morning, while I had still made none of my preparations.
I said:
“I will prepare a day or two after him and then catch up with them.”
The morning after they departed, I went out to prepare, but I returned without accomplishing anything. The next morning I again went out and returned without completing anything.
This continued until the army had traveled quickly and gone far ahead. I intended to set out and catch them—how I wish I had done so—but that was not decreed for me.
Whenever I went out among the people after the departure of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and looked around, it grieved me that I saw only men suspected of hypocrisy or those weak persons whom Allah had excused.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not mention me until he reached Tabūk. While sitting among the people there, he asked:
“What has happened to Kaʿb?”
A man from Banū Salimah said:
“O Messenger of Allah, his two fine garments and his admiration of his own appearance held him back.”
Muʿādh ibn Jabal said:
“What an evil statement you have made! By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, we have known nothing but good concerning him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained silent.
Kaʿb ibn Mālik continued:
When I learned that the Prophet ﷺ had begun his return journey, anxiety overwhelmed me. I began thinking of lies and asking myself:
“How can I escape his anger tomorrow?”
I sought the assistance of every wise member of my family in devising an excuse.
When it was announced that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was about to arrive, all false ideas left me. I realized that I could never escape the matter through any lie, so I resolved to tell him the truth.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in the morning. Whenever he returned from a journey, he would begin by going to the mosque, pray two units there, and then sit to receive the people.
When he had done so, those who had remained behind came to him, offering excuses and swearing oaths. They numbered more than eighty men.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted their outward claims, renewed their pledges, sought forgiveness for them, and entrusted their hidden intentions to Allah.
I came to him. When I greeted him, he smiled the smile of one who was angry and said:
“Come forward.”
I walked forward and sat before him. He asked:
“What caused you to remain behind? Had you not purchased your riding animal?”
I replied:
