“Yes. By Allah, had I been sitting before anyone else among the people of this world, I believe I could have escaped his anger by presenting an excuse, for I have been given skill in argument.
“But, by Allah, I know that if I tell you a lie today that causes you to be pleased with me, Allah may soon cause you to become angry with me. Yet if I tell you the truth and you become angry with me because of it, I hope through it for Allah’s pardon.
“No, by Allah, I had no excuse. By Allah, I had never been stronger or more financially able than I was when I remained behind from you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“As for this man, he has spoken the truth. Rise and wait until Allah judges concerning you.”
I rose, and some men from Banū Salimah followed me, saying:
“By Allah, we have never known you to commit a sin before this. You were incapable even of presenting an excuse to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ like those who remained behind did.
“The seeking of forgiveness for you by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would have been sufficient to erase your sin.”
By Allah, they continued reproaching me until I almost returned and declared my truthful statement to have been false.
I then asked them:
“Did anyone else receive the same treatment as I did?”
They replied:
“Yes, two men said the same thing you said, and the same response was given to them.”
I asked:
“Who are they?”
They replied:
“Murārah ibn al-Rabīʿ al-ʿAmrī and Hilāl ibn Umayyah al-Wāqifī.”
They named two righteous men who had participated at Badr and whose example gave me comfort.
I continued on once they had mentioned them to me.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prohibited the Muslims from speaking to the three of us, out of all those who had remained behind.
The people avoided us and changed their behavior toward us until the very earth seemed unfamiliar to me and was no longer the land I knew.
We remained in that condition for fifty nights.
As for my two companions, they submitted quietly, remained in their homes, and wept.
I was the youngest and strongest of the three, so I continued going out, attending prayer with the Muslims, and walking through the marketplaces, but no one spoke to me.
I would come to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and greet him while he sat after prayer. I would ask myself:
“Did he move his lips in returning my greeting or not?”
I would then pray near him and secretly glance at him. When I turned toward my prayer, he would look at me, but when I turned toward him, he would turn away.
When the people’s estrangement became prolonged and unbearable, I walked to the garden of Abū Qatādah, my cousin and the person most beloved to me, and climbed over its wall.
I greeted him, but by Allah, he did not return my greeting.
I said:
“Abū Qatādah, I adjure you by Allah: do you know that I love Allah and His Messenger?”
He remained silent.
I repeated the question and adjured him again, but he remained silent.
I asked a third time, and he replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
My eyes overflowed with tears, and I turned away and climbed back over the wall.
While I was walking through the marketplace of Madinah, a Nabatean from Syria who had brought food to sell in Madinah was asking:
“Who will direct me to Kaʿb ibn Mālik?”
The people began pointing me out to him. When he reached me, he handed me a letter from the king of Ghassān.
It read:
“To proceed: I have been informed that your companion has treated you harshly. Allah has not placed you in a land of humiliation or neglect. Join us, and we will comfort and support you.”
When I read it, I said:
“This too is part of the trial.”
I went to an oven and burned the letter in it.
When forty of the fifty nights had passed, a messenger from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to me and said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commands you to separate from your wife.”
I asked:
“Should I divorce her, or what should I do?”
He replied:
“No. Simply separate from her and do not approach her.”
The same command was sent to my two companions.
I said to my wife:
“Go to your family and remain with them until Allah judges concerning this matter.”
Kaʿb continued:
The wife of Hilāl ibn Umayyah came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Hilāl ibn Umayyah is an elderly, helpless man with no servant. Would you object if I continued serving him?”
He replied:
“No, but he must not approach you.”
She said:
“By Allah, he has no desire for anything. By Allah, he has continued weeping from the day this matter began until today.”
Some members of my family said to me:
“Why do you not seek the permission of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for your wife to serve you, just as he permitted the wife of Hilāl ibn Umayyah to serve him?”
I replied:
“By Allah, I will not ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for permission concerning her. How do I know what he would say if I asked him, when I am a young man?”
I remained in that condition for another ten nights, completing fifty nights from the time the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prohibited the people from speaking to us.
On the morning of the fiftieth night, after praying the dawn prayer, I was sitting on the roof of one of our houses in the state Allah described: my own soul had become constricted for me, and the earth, despite its vastness, had become constricted.
I heard the voice of a man who had climbed Mount Salʿ shouting at the top of his voice:
“Kaʿb ibn Mālik, rejoice!”
I immediately fell down in prostration and knew that relief had come.
After praying the dawn prayer, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ announced that Allah had accepted our repentance. The people went out to give us the glad tidings, and messengers also went to my two companions.
One man galloped toward me on horseback, while another man from the tribe of Aslam ran and climbed the mountain. The voice reached me faster than the horse.
When the man whose voice I had heard came to me with the glad tidings, I removed both of my garments and gave them to him as a reward for the news.
By Allah, I owned no other garments that day.
I borrowed two garments, put them on, and set out toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
People met me group after group, congratulating me on the acceptance of my repentance and saying:
“May Allah’s acceptance of your repentance bring you joy.”
Kaʿb said:
I entered the mosque and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sitting with the people around him.
Ṭalḥah ibn ʿUbayd Allāh stood and hurried toward me, shook my hand, and congratulated me.
By Allah, no other man among the Emigrants stood up for me, and I never forgot that act of Ṭalḥah.
Kaʿb continued:
When I greeted the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, his face was shining with happiness, and he said:
“Rejoice in the best day you have experienced since the day your mother gave birth to you.”
I asked:
“Is this from you, O Messenger of Allah, or from Allah?”
He replied:
“No, it is from Allah.”
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was pleased, his face would shine as though it were a piece of the moon, and we recognized this sign in him.
When I sat before him, I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, as part of my repentance, I wish to give up all my wealth as charity to Allah and His Messenger.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Retain some of your wealth, for that is better for you.”
I said:
“Then I will retain my share in Khaybar.”
I also said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah saved me only because of truthfulness. As part of my repentance, I will speak nothing but the truth for as long as I live.”
By Allah, I know of no Muslim whom Allah tested more favorably in truthfulness from the time I made that statement to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ than He tested me.
From the day I said that to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until this day, I have never deliberately told a lie. I hope that Allah will continue protecting me for the rest of my life.
Allah revealed to His Messenger ﷺ:
“Allah has certainly turned in mercy to the Prophet and the Emigrants…”
until His words:
“And be with the truthful.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:117–119]
By Allah, after guiding me to Islam, Allah has never bestowed upon me a blessing greater in my estimation than my truthfulness to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. I did not lie to him and thereby perish as those who lied perished.
When revelation came concerning those who lied, Allah said the worst thing He said concerning anyone:
“They will swear by Allah to you when you return to them so that you may leave them alone. So leave them alone. They are impure, and their refuge is Hell as repayment for what they used to earn. They swear to you so that you may be pleased with them. But even if you are pleased with them, Allah is not pleased with the defiantly disobedient people.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:95–96]
Kaʿb said:
The matter of the three of us was deferred, unlike those whose excuses the Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted when they swore to him. He renewed their pledges, sought forgiveness for them, and postponed our case until Allah judged it.
That is the meaning of Allah’s words:
“And also the three whose matter was deferred.”
What Allah described was not merely our remaining behind from the expedition. Rather, it referred to His postponing our case and deferring judgment concerning us, in contrast to those who swore and offered excuses that the Prophet ﷺ accepted.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4418) and Muslim (2769:53).

12526 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Truthfulness leads to righteousness, and righteousness leads to Paradise. A man continues speaking the truth until he becomes a thoroughly truthful person.
Lying leads to wickedness, and wickedness leads to the Fire. A man continues lying until he is recorded with Allah as a habitual liar.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6094) and Muslim (2607).
The words:
“Be with the truthful”
first refer to the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions who departed for the expedition of Tabūk. They then apply generally to all truthful people who accept the truth and remain steadfast upon it. A liar is not suitable for companionship.

12527 — Jābir narrated:
We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the expedition of Baṭn Buwāṭ while he was pursuing al-Majdī ibn ʿAmr al-Juhanī. Five, six, or seven men among us would take turns riding a single camel used for carrying water.
When the turn of a man from the Anṣār came to ride his camel, he made it kneel, mounted it, and urged it forward. The animal hesitated somewhat, so he said to it:
“Move! May Allah curse you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Who is this person cursing his camel?”
He replied:
“It is I, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Dismount from it, for a cursed animal must not accompany us. Do not supplicate against yourselves, do not supplicate against your children, and do not supplicate against your wealth, lest your supplication coincide with a time when Allah grants whatever is requested and He answers you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3009).

12528 — Ṣuhayb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How remarkable is the affair of the believer! All of his affairs are good, and this applies to no one except the believer. If prosperity reaches him, he gives thanks, and that is good for him. If hardship reaches him, he remains patient, and that is good for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2999).

12529 — Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no day upon which the sun rises except that two angels are appointed at its sides, calling out with a proclamation heard by all of Allah’s creation except humankind and the jinn: ‘People, come to your Lord. A small amount that is sufficient is better than a great amount that distracts.’
The sun never sets except that two angels are appointed at its sides, proclaiming with a call heard by all of Allah’s creation except humankind and the jinn: ‘O Allah, give replacement to the one who spends and give destruction to the one who withholds.’”
Allah, the Exalted, revealed Qur’an corresponding to the angels’ words:
“People, come to your Lord.”
He said in Sūrat Yūnus:
“Allah calls to the Home of Peace and guides whomever He wills to a straight path.”
He also revealed concerning their words:
“O Allah, give replacement to the one who spends and give destruction to the one who withholds”:
“By the night when it covers, and by the day when it appears, and by the One Who created the male and the female: indeed, your efforts are diverse. As for the one who gives, is mindful of Allah, and affirms the finest reward, We will ease him toward ease. But as for the one who withholds, considers himself self-sufficient, and denies the finest reward, We will ease him toward hardship.” [Sūrat al-Layl 92:1–10]

Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21721), Ibn Ḥibbān (686, 3329), and al-Ḥākim (2/444–445).

12530 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
Angels came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was sleeping. Some of them said:
“He is asleep.”
Others said:
“His eyes are asleep, but his heart is awake.”
They said:
“This companion of yours has a parable, so present his parable.”
Some said:
“He is asleep.”
Others said:
“His eyes are asleep, but his heart is awake.”
They said:
“His example is that of a man who built a house, prepared a banquet within it, and sent someone to invite the people. Whoever answered the caller entered the house and ate from the banquet. Whoever did not answer the caller neither entered the house nor ate from the banquet.”
They said:
“Explain it to him so that he may understand it.”
Some said:
“He is asleep.”
Others said:
“His eyes are asleep, but his heart is awake.”
They said:
“The house is Paradise, and the caller is Muḥammad ﷺ. Whoever obeys Muḥammad ﷺ has obeyed Allah, and whoever disobeys Muḥammad ﷺ has disobeyed Allah. Muḥammad ﷺ distinguishes between the people.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7281).

12531 — Ṣuhayb narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When the people of Paradise enter Paradise, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, will say: ‘Do you desire anything more that I may increase you?’
They will reply: ‘Did You not brighten our faces? Did You not admit us into Paradise and save us from the Fire?’
He will then remove the veil, and they will not have been given anything more beloved to them than looking upon their Lord, Mighty and Majestic.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (181).
It was also narrated with the addition:
“He then recited the verse:
‘For those who do good is the finest reward and an increase.’”
It was narrated by Aḥmad (18935).

12532 — Abū Dharr narrated from the Prophet ﷺ, concerning what he narrated from Allah, Blessed and Exalted, that He said:
“My servants, I have forbidden oppression for Myself and made it forbidden among you, so do not oppress one another.
My servants, all of you are astray except those whom I guide, so ask Me for guidance and I will guide you.
My servants, all of you are hungry except those whom I feed, so ask Me for food and I will feed you.
My servants, all of you are unclothed except those whom I clothe, so ask Me for clothing and I will clothe you.
My servants, you commit sins by night and day, while I forgive all sins, so seek My forgiveness and I will forgive you.
My servants, you can never attain the ability to harm Me, so as to harm Me, nor can you attain the ability to benefit Me, so as to benefit Me.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the human and jinn among you, were all as pious as the most pious heart of any one man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the least.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the human and jinn among you, were all as wicked as the most wicked heart of any one man, that would not diminish My dominion in the least.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the human and jinn among you, were all to stand together upon one plain and ask Me, and I were to give every person what he requested, that would diminish what I possess no more than a needle diminishes the sea when dipped into it.
My servants, these are only your deeds. I record them for you and then repay you for them in full. Whoever finds good should praise Allah, and whoever finds otherwise should blame no one but himself.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2577).
The words:
“Rather, people wrong themselves”
mean that they wrong themselves through their disbelief and commission of sins.

12533 — Mālik ibn Naḍlah narrated:
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while appearing poorly dressed and unkempt.
He asked:
“Do you possess wealth?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“What kind of wealth?”
I replied:
“Every kind: camels, slaves, horses, and sheep.”
He said:
“When Allah grants you wealth, let its effects be seen upon you.”
He then asked:
“Are the camels of your people born with healthy ears, after which you deliberately take a knife and cut their ears, saying, ‘These are baḥīrah,’ or cut their skins and say, ‘These are ṣurm,’ and then declare them forbidden for yourself and your family?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“What Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has given you belongs to you. Allah’s arm is stronger, and Allah’s blade is sharper.”
He may also have said:
“Allah’s arm is stronger than your arm, and Allah’s blade is sharper than your blade.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what do you say about a man with whom I stayed, but he neither honored nor hosted me, and then he later came to stay with me? Should I repay him as he treated me, or should I host him?”
He replied:
“Host him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (15888), whose wording is given here, Abū Dāwūd (4063), al-Tirmidhī (2006), and Ibn Ḥibbān (5416).
The words:
“Or do you fabricate lies against Allah?”
refer to their claim:
“Allah commanded us to do it.”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:28]

12534 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among the servants of Allah are people whom the prophets and martyrs will admire, though they are neither prophets nor martyrs.”
We said:
“O Messenger of Allah, describe them to us, for we love them.”
He said:
“They are those who love one another for Allah’s sake, without any close family relationship between them and without wealth they exchange among themselves. They will not be frightened when the people are frightened, nor will they grieve when the people grieve.”
He then recited:
“Indeed, the close allies of Allah will have no fear, nor will they grieve.”
Good: Narrated by Hannād in al-Zuhd (475), Abū Dāwūd (3527), and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (12/211).
The statement:
“The prophets will admire them”
emphasizes the lofty rank granted to them. It does not mean that they are better or higher in rank than the prophets.

12535 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among Allah’s servants are people whom the prophets and martyrs will admire.”
It was asked:
“Who are they, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“They are people who love one another through the spirit granted by Allah, without wealth or lineage binding them together. Their faces will be light, and they will be upon pulpits of light. They will not fear when the people fear, nor grieve when the people grieve.”
He then recited:
“Indeed, the close allies of Allah will have no fear, nor will they grieve.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11172), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (12/211), and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (8584).

12536 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has servants who are neither prophets nor martyrs, yet the martyrs and prophets will admire them on the Day of Resurrection because of their nearness to Allah, the Exalted, and the rank of their gathering with Him.”
A Bedouin knelt upon his knees and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, describe them and make their qualities clear to us.”
He replied:
“They are people from the ordinary masses and from scattered tribes who became sincere friends for Allah’s sake and loved one another for His sake. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will place pulpits of light for them on the Day of Resurrection. The people will be afraid, but they will not be afraid. They are the close allies of Allah, Mighty and Majestic: ‘They will have no fear, nor will they grieve.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (4/170–171).

12537 — Abū Mālik al-Ashʿarī gathered his people and said:
“People of al-Ashʿar, gather together and bring your women and children so that I may teach you the prayer of the Prophet ﷺ as he performed it for us in Madinah.”
They gathered and brought their women and children. He performed ablution and demonstrated to them how to perform it, washing every required place completely.
When the shadow had shifted and the heat had lessened, he stood and called the adhān. The men formed the first rows, the children stood behind them, and the women stood behind the children.
He then called the iqāmah, stepped forward, raised his hands, and pronounced the opening takbīr. He recited Sūrat al-Fātiḥah and another sūrah quietly.
He then pronounced the takbīr and bowed, saying three times:
“Glory and praise belong to Allah.”
He then said:
“Allah hears the one who praises Him,”
and stood upright.
He pronounced the takbīr and fell into prostration, pronounced the takbīr and raised his head, pronounced the takbīr and prostrated again, then pronounced the takbīr and rose to stand.
Thus, he pronounced six takbīrs during the first unit of prayer and another takbīr when rising for the second unit.
After completing the prayer, he turned toward his people and said:
“Remember my takbīrs and learn the manner of my bowing and prostration. This is the prayer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ as he used to perform it for us at this time of day.”
He then said that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had completed his prayer, he turned toward the people and said:
“People, listen and understand. Know that Allah has servants who are neither prophets nor martyrs, yet the prophets and martyrs will admire them because of their gatherings and nearness to Allah.”
A Bedouin from the far side of the gathering knelt down and gestured toward the Prophet of Allah ﷺ, saying:
“O Prophet of Allah, people who are neither prophets nor martyrs, yet the prophets and martyrs will admire them because of their gatherings and nearness to Allah—describe them and make their qualities clear to us.”
That is, explain their characteristics and appearance.
The face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ brightened with happiness at the Bedouin’s question, and he said:
“They are people from the ordinary masses and scattered tribes. They are not connected by close family ties, but they love one another for Allah’s sake and are sincere toward one another.
On the Day of Resurrection, Allah will place pulpits of light for them and seat them upon them. He will make their faces light and their garments light.
The people will be terrified on the Day of Resurrection, but they will not be terrified. They are the close allies of Allah, upon whom there will be no fear, nor will they grieve.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22906), whose wording is given here, ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Mubārak in al-Zuhd (714), and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (12/212).
The words:
“They will have no fear”
refer to what lies ahead of them, while:
“Nor will they grieve”
refer to what has passed.
Rather, they place their trust in their Lord.
Allah says in the revelation:
“Indeed, those who say, ‘Our Lord is Allah,’ and then remain steadfast, the angels descend upon them, saying, ‘Do not fear and do not grieve, but rejoice in the Paradise you were promised.’”
 [Sūrat Fuṣṣilat 41:30]

12538 — Abū Dharr narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“What do you say about a man who performs a good deed and is praised by the people for it?”
He replied:
“That is the immediate glad tiding of the believer.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2642).
Among these glad tidings is also a righteous dream that the believer sees or that is seen concerning him.

12539 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated:
I asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about the words:
“For them are glad tidings in the worldly life.”
He replied:
“It is the righteous dream that the believer sees or that is seen concerning him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2275), Ibn Mājah (3898), Aḥmad (22687), and al-Ḥākim (2/340, 4/391).

12540 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Nothing remains of prophethood except the glad tidings.”
They asked:
“What are the glad tidings?”
He replied:
“Righteous dreams.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6990).

12541 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah accepts the repentance of His servant as long as the soul has not reached the throat.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3537) and Aḥmad (6160), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (628) and al-Ḥākim (4/257).

12542 — Muḥammad ibn ʿAbbād ibn Jaʿfar narrated that he heard Ibn ʿAbbās recite:
“Indeed, they fold their chests.”
He said:
I asked him about it, and he replied:
“There were people who felt ashamed when relieving themselves beneath the open sky and when having intercourse with their wives beneath the open sky, so this verse was revealed concerning them.”
In another narration:
“A man would have intercourse with his wife and feel ashamed, or relieve himself and feel ashamed, so the verse was revealed:
‘Indeed, they fold their chests.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4681). The second narration was also recorded by al-Bukhārī (4682).

12543 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ‘Spend, and I will spend upon you.’”
He also said:
“The hand of Allah is full and is not diminished by spending. He gives continuously by night and day. Have you considered all that He has spent since He created the heavens and the earth? It has not diminished what is in His hand. His Throne was upon the water, and in His hand is the scale; He lowers and raises.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4684) and Muslim (993).

12544 — ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn narrated:
I entered upon the Prophet ﷺ after tying my she-camel at the door. Some people from Banū Tamīm came to him, and he said:
“Accept the glad tidings, Banū Tamīm.”
They replied twice:
“You have given us glad tidings, so now give us something.”
Then some people from Yemen entered, and he said:
“Accept the glad tidings, people of Yemen, since Banū Tamīm did not accept them.”
They replied:
“We accept them, O Messenger of Allah.”
They said:
“We have come to ask you about the beginning of this matter.”
He replied:
“Allah existed, and nothing besides Him existed. His Throne was upon the water. He wrote everything in the Record and created the heavens and the earth.”
A caller then shouted:
“Your she-camel has gone, Ibn al-Ḥuṣayn!”
I went after it, and there it was, with the mirage appearing between me and it. By Allah, I wished that I had left it and remained to hear the rest.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3191).

12545 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Allah wrote the decrees of all creation fifty thousand years before He created the heavens and the earth.”
He added:
“His Throne was upon the water.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2653).

12546 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No fatigue, illness, anxiety, grief, harm, or distress afflicts a Muslim—even the prick of a thorn—except that Allah expiates some of his sins through it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5641–5642) and Muslim (2573).

12547 — Ṣuhayb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How remarkable is the affair of the believer! All of his affairs are good, and this applies to no one except the believer. If prosperity reaches him, he gives thanks, and that is good for him. If hardship reaches him, he remains patient, and that is good for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2999).

12548 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, no one from this community—whether a Jew or a Christian—hears of me and then dies without believing in that with which I was sent except that he will be among the inhabitants of the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (153).

12549 — Ṣafwān ibn Muḥriz narrated:
While Ibn ʿUmar was performing ṭawāf, a man approached him and asked:
“Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān,” or, “Ibn ʿUmar, what did you hear the Prophet ﷺ say concerning the private conversation with Allah?”
He replied:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“The believer will be brought close to his Lord”—Hishām said, “The believer will draw near”—“until He places His covering over him and makes him acknowledge his sins. He will ask, ‘Do you recognize such-and-such a sin?’
He will reply, ‘I recognize it.’
He will say, ‘My Lord, I recognize it,’ twice.
Allah will say, ‘I concealed it for you in the world, and today I forgive it for you.’
Then the record of his good deeds will be folded up for him.
As for the others—or the disbelievers—it will be proclaimed before all witnesses:
‘These are the ones who lied against their Lord.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4685) and Muslim (2768). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12550 — Ibn Abī Laylā narrated:
Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah met me and said:
“Shall I give you a gift?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came out to us, and we said:
“We have learned how to greet you with peace, but how should we invoke blessings upon you?”
He replied:
“Say: O Allah, bestow Your blessings upon Muḥammad and the family of Muḥammad, just as You bestowed Your blessings upon the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious. O Allah, bless Muḥammad and the family of Muḥammad, just as You blessed the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4797) and Muslim (406). The wording is that of Muslim.
The words:
“He began pleading with Us concerning the people of Lot”
mean that Abraham pleaded with Our messengers who had come to him with the glad tidings and with the announcement that the people of Lot would be destroyed. He said:
“Lot is among them.” They replied, “We know best who is within it. We will certainly save him and his family, except his wife, who will be among those who remain behind.”
 [Sūrat al-ʿAnkabūt 29:32]

12551 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“May Allah have mercy upon Lot. He was indeed taking refuge in a strong support.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3372) and Muslim (151).
The words:
“Or could take refuge in a strong support”
refer ultimately to seeking refuge in Allah, the Exalted. Lot, peace be upon him, had left Iraq with his uncle Abraham, and Allah made him a prophet to the people of Sodom in the region of Syria.
He first said:
“If only I had strength against you or could take refuge in a strong support,”
meaning his clan and people. In this way, he first expressed his inability to protect his guests through worldly means, and then turned to Allah, the Exalted, for He is the strongest, mightiest, and most protective support.
His words:
“I take refuge in a strong support”
therefore describe the true state in which he was, as reassurance to his guests. They do not mean that he had forgotten to place his trust in Allah.

12552 — Abū Mūsā narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, grants respite to the wrongdoer, but when He seizes him, He does not let him escape.”
He then recited:
“Such is the seizure of your Lord when He seizes communities while they are unjust. Indeed, His seizure is painful and severe.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4686) and Muslim (2583).

12553 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said in the lengthy hadith of intercession:
“…No one will speak on that day except the messengers, and the words of the messengers on that day will be: ‘O Allah, grant safety! Grant safety!’…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (806) and Muslim (182).
The expression:
“As long as the heavens and the earth endure”
was used by the Arabs to indicate perpetuity.
Regarding the words:
“Except what your Lord wills,”
if the exception refers to those who are wretched, its meaning is that some believers will be admitted into the Fire because of sins they committed. Allah will then bring them out of it and admit them into Paradise, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12554 — Anas narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A people will be brought out of the Fire after its scorching has touched them. They will then enter Paradise, and the people of Paradise will call them ‘the people who came from Hell.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6559).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated it through another route from Qatādah (7450) with the wording:
“Some people will certainly be scorched by the Fire as punishment for sins they committed. Allah will then admit them into Paradise by the bounty of His mercy. They will be called ‘the people who came from Hell.’”
If the exception refers to those who are blessed, it refers to the period some of them spent in the Fire because of their sins before entering Paradise.

12555 — ʿAbdullāh narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I had improper contact with a woman at the far end of Madinah. I did everything with her short of intercourse. Here I am, so judge me as you wish.”
ʿUmar said to him:
“Allah concealed your sin. You should have concealed it yourself.”
The Prophet ﷺ gave no reply, so the man stood and departed.
The Prophet ﷺ sent someone after him, called him back, and recited to him:
“Establish prayer at the two ends of the day and at the approaches of the night. Indeed, good deeds remove evil deeds. That is a reminder for those who remember.”
A man from among the people asked:
“O Prophet of Allah, is this ruling only for him?”
He replied:
“Rather, it is for all people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2763:42).

12556 — Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
A man kissed a woman and then came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him of what he had done. The following verse was revealed:
“Establish prayer at the two ends of the day and at the approaches of the night. Indeed, good deeds remove evil deeds. That is a reminder for those who remember.”
The man asked:
“Is this only for me?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“It is for everyone from my nation who acts upon it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4687) and Muslim (2763:40).

12557 — Abū al-Yasar narrated:
A woman came to purchase dates from me. I said:
“I have better dates inside the house.”
She entered the house with me, and I approached her, touched her, and kissed her.
I went to Abū Bakr and told him what had happened. He said:
“Conceal your sin and do not tell anyone.”
I could not remain patient, so I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him.
He said:
“Is this how you cared for the family of a man who had gone out on campaign?”
I wished that I had not embraced Islam until that very moment, until I thought that I was among the people of the Fire.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ lowered his head for a long time. Allah then revealed to him:
“Establish prayer at the two ends of the day…”
until the end of the verse.
Abū al-Yasar said:
I came to him, and he recited the verse to me.
His Companions asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, is this only for him, or is it general for all people?”
He replied:
“Rather, it is general for all people.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3115), al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (12/625), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (19/165).

12558 — Anas narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I have committed every kind of sin, major and minor.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked him three times:
“Do you not testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“That testimony overwhelms and removes all of that.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār (6887), Abū Yaʿlā (3433), whose wording is given here, and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (7073) and al-Muʿjam al-Ṣaghīr (1025).

12559 — Ḥumrān narrated:
After ʿUthmān performed ablution, he said:
“By Allah, I will narrate a hadith to you. By Allah, were it not for a verse in the Book of Allah, I would not narrate it to you.
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘No man performs ablution and perfects it, then performs a prayer, except that the sins committed between that prayer and the prayer following it are forgiven.’”
ʿUrwah said:
The verse referred to is:
“Indeed, those who conceal what We revealed of clear proofs and guidance…”
until His words:
“…those who curse.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:159]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (160) and Muslim (227:6).
It was also narrated by Aḥmad (513), al-Bazzār (405), al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (12/615), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (6/2092), all through Abū ʿAqīl—Zuhrah ibn Maʿbad—who heard al-Ḥārith, the freedman of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, say:
One day, ʿUthmān sat down, and we sat with him. The caller to prayer came to him, so he asked for water in a vessel that I believe held approximately one mudd, and he performed ablution.
He then said:
“I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ perform ablution as I have just performed it, and he then said:
‘Whoever performs ablution as I have done and then stands and performs the noon prayer will be forgiven for what occurred between it and the dawn prayer.
Then, when he performs the afternoon prayer, he will be forgiven for what occurred between it and the noon prayer.
Then, when he performs the sunset prayer, he will be forgiven for what occurred between it and the afternoon prayer.
Then, when he performs the night prayer, he will be forgiven for what occurred between it and the sunset prayer.
He may then spend the night turning about, but when he rises, performs ablution, and performs the dawn prayer, he will be forgiven for what occurred between it and the night prayer.
These are the good deeds that remove evil deeds.’”
They asked:
“These are the good deeds, but what are the enduring righteous deeds, ʿUthmān?”
He replied:
“They are: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ ‘Glory be to Allah,’ ‘All praise belongs to Allah,’ ‘Allah is the Greatest,’ and ‘There is no power and no strength except through Allah.’”
Its chain is good.

12560 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
Allah revealed the Qur’an to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he recited it to the people for some time. They then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, could you relate stories to us?”
Allah then revealed:
“Alif-Lām-Rā. These are the verses of the clear Book. We have sent it down as an Arabic Qur’an so that you may understand. We relate to you the best of narratives through what We have revealed to you in this Qur’an, although before it you were among those unaware.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued reciting these verses to them for some time. They then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, could you speak to us with an account?”
Allah then revealed:
“Allah has sent down the finest discourse: a Book consistent in its parts and repeating its lessons, from which the skins of those who fear their Lord shiver; then their skins and hearts soften at the remembrance of Allah. That is the guidance of Allah by which He guides whomever He wills. Whoever Allah allows to stray will have no guide.”
 [Sūrat al-Zumar 39:23]
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in his Musnad (1153) and Abū Yaʿlā in his Musnad (740), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2609) and al-Ḥākim (2/345).
The words:
“The best of narratives”
refer to the story of Joseph. It is called the best of narratives because it contains lessons and wisdom beneficial for both religion and worldly life.

12561 — Abū Salamah narrated:
I used to see dreams that would make me ill. I then met Abū Qatādah, who said:
“I too used to see dreams that would make me ill, until I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘A righteous dream is from Allah. When one of you sees something he likes, he should relate it only to someone he loves. If he sees something he dislikes, he should spit lightly to his left three times, seek refuge in Allah from the evil of Satan and from the evil of the dream, and not tell anyone about it. It will then cause him no harm.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7044) and Muslim (2261:4).

12562 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The noble one, son of the noble one, son of the noble one, son of the noble one: Joseph, son of Jacob, son of Isaac, son of Abraham.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4688).

12563 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
It was asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, who is the noblest of people?”
He replied:
“The one most mindful of Allah.”
They said:
“That is not what we are asking about.”
He said:
“Then Joseph, the prophet of Allah, son of the prophet of Allah, son of the prophet of Allah, son of the intimate friend of Allah.”
They said:
“That is not what we are asking about.”
He said:
“Are you asking me about the noble lineages of the Arabs? The best of them in the pre-Islamic period are the best of them in Islam when they gain understanding of the religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3353) and Muslim (2378). The wording is that of Muslim.

12564 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated regarding the time when the people of the slander said what they said against her.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“If you are innocent, Allah will declare your innocence. If you have committed a sin, then seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him.”
I said:
“By Allah, I find no example for myself except the father of Joseph:
‘So beautiful patience is most fitting, and Allah is the One Whose help is sought against what you describe.’”
Allah then revealed:
“Indeed, those who produced the slander are a group from among you. Do not think it evil for you; rather, it is good for you. Every person among them will bear what he earned of sin, and the one who assumed the greatest share of it will suffer a tremendous punishment.”
Allah revealed the ten verses concerning the incident.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4690) and Muslim (2770).

12565 — Umm Rūmān, the mother of ʿĀʾishah, narrated:
While I was with ʿĀʾishah, she was overtaken by fever. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Perhaps this is because of something being said about you.”
She replied:
“Yes.”
ʿĀʾishah then spoke and said:
“My situation with you is like that of Jacob with his sons:
‘Rather, your souls have enticed you to something. So beautiful patience is most fitting, and Allah is the One Whose help is sought against what you describe.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4691).

12566 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said concerning the words:
“Hayta laka”:
“We recite it only as we were taught it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4692).
This is the recitation of the majority. Nāfiʿ, Ibn Dhakwān, and Abū Jaʿfar recited it as:
“Hiʾtu laka.”
Hishām recited:
“Hiʾta laka.”
Ibn Kathīr recited:
“Haytu laka.”
It is said to be a Coptic expression meaning:
“Come here.”
Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī narrated through his chain from ʿIkrimah, the freedman of Ibn ʿAbbās, concerning the words:
“Hayta laka”:
He said:
“It means: ‘Come here.’ It is in the Ḥawrānī dialect, meaning: ‘Come and draw near.’”

12567 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said in the lengthy hadith of the Night Journey:
“There I saw Joseph, and he had been given half of all beauty…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (162).

12568 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
When the illness of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ became severe, he was asked about leading the prayer. He said:
“Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“Abū Bakr is a tenderhearted man. When he recites, he is overcome by weeping.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Tell him to lead the prayer.”
She repeated her request, and he said:
“Tell him to lead the prayer. You women are like the women associated with Joseph.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (682) and Muslim (418:94). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
ʿĀʾishah’s repeated request arose from consultation within their marital relationship, not from objecting to the judgment of the Prophet ﷺ.
His words:
“You women are like the women associated with Joseph”
refer only to their persistence in seeking what they desired and were inclined toward. The comparison concerns persistence and insistence alone.

12569 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“May Allah have mercy upon Lot. He was indeed taking refuge in a strong support. Had I remained in prison as long as Joseph remained, I would have answered the caller immediately. We have more claim to doubt than Abraham, when Allah said to him, ‘Do you not believe?’ He replied, ‘Yes, but so that my heart may be reassured.’”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:260]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4694) and Muslim (151).
The words:
“I would have answered the caller”
mean the king’s messenger who called Joseph to leave prison.

12570 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said concerning the words:
“Ask him what happened with the women who cut their hands”:
“Had it been me, I would have hurried to answer the summons and would not have first sought to establish my innocence.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (8554), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (13/201), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (7/2155–2156).

12571 — Surāqah ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Were I to command anyone to prostrate before another person, I would command a woman to prostrate before her husband.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (7/152) and Ibn Abī al-Dunyā in al-ʿIyāl (537).

12572 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Were I to command anyone to prostrate before another person, I would command a woman to prostrate before her husband.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1159), al-Bayhaqī (7/291), and Ibn Abī al-Dunyā in al-ʿIyāl (534).
It has been said that Joseph’s mother had died and that Jacob brought Joseph’s maternal aunt instead. However, the Qur’an explicitly mentions both of his parents, and no authentic hadith establishes that Joseph’s mother had died.

12573 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated that he asked ʿĀʾishah about the words of Allah, the Exalted:
“Until the messengers reached the point of despair.”
He said:
“Is it ‘they thought they had been lied to,’ or ‘they had been declared liars’?”
ʿĀʾishah replied:
“They had been declared liars.”
I said:
“But they were certain that their people had rejected them, so this was not merely an assumption.”
She replied:
“Yes, by my life, they were certainly convinced of that.”
I asked her:
“Then what is the meaning of:
‘They thought that they had been lied to’?”
She replied:
“Allah forbid! The messengers would never think such a thing concerning their Lord.”
I asked:
“Then what does the verse mean?”
She replied:
“It refers to the followers of the messengers who believed in their Lord and affirmed the messengers. Their trial became prolonged and divine help was delayed. When the messengers despaired of those among their people who had rejected them, and feared that their own followers might now consider them liars, Allah’s help came to them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4695).

12574 — Ibn Abī Mulaykah narrated:
Ibn ʿAbbās recited the words:
“Until the messengers reached the point of despair and thought that they had been lied to”
 [Sūrat Yūsuf 12:110]
in the lighter form.
He interpreted it in that manner and recited:
“Until the messenger and those who believed with him said, ‘When will the help of Allah come?’ Indeed, the help of Allah is near.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:214]
I met ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr and mentioned this to him. He said:
ʿĀʾishah stated:
“Allah forbid! By Allah, Allah never promised His messenger anything except that the messenger knew it would certainly occur before he died.
“However, trials continued to afflict the messengers until they feared that those who were with them might begin accusing them of falsehood.”
She therefore recited it in the intensified form:
“They feared that they had been declared liars.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4524–4525).

12575 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said concerning the words:
“We make some of them excel others in produce”:
“The inferior dates, the Persian variety, the sweet, and the sour.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (13/431) and al-ʿUqaylī in al-Ḍuʿafāʾ (2/131).
The word al-daqal refers to inferior, dry dates, while al-Fārisī is a variety of dates.

12576 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The keys of the unseen are five, known only to Allah. No one knows what will happen tomorrow except Allah. No one knows what the wombs will lose except Allah. No one knows when rain will come except Allah. No soul knows in what land it will die, and no one knows when the Hour will be established except Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4697).

12577 — Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent one of his Companions to a prominent man from the people of the pre-Islamic period to invite him to Allah, Blessed and Exalted.
The man asked:
“What is this lord of yours to whom you invite me? Is he made of copper? Is he made of iron? Is he made of silver? Is he made of gold?”
The Companion returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him. The Prophet ﷺ sent him back a second time, and the man repeated the same words. He returned and informed the Prophet ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ sent him a third time, and the man again repeated the same statement. The Companion returned and informed the Prophet ﷺ. Allah, Blessed and Exalted, then sent a thunderbolt upon the man and burned him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Blessed and Exalted, has sent a thunderbolt upon your man and burned him.”
Then this verse was revealed:
“He sends the thunderbolts, striking with them whomever He wills, while they dispute concerning Allah, and He is severe in might.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in his Musnad (7007), whose wording is given here, Abū Yaʿlā in his Musnad (3341), al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/283), and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (1701).

12578 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“He sent down water from the sky, and valleys flowed according to their capacity”:
“This is a parable presented by Allah. Hearts receive from revelation according to the measure of their certainty and doubt.”
“As for doubt, deeds accompanied by it bring no benefit. As for certainty, Allah benefits its people through it. This is the meaning of His words:
‘As for the foam, it vanishes as worthless scum,’
which represents doubt, and:
‘As for what benefits people, it remains upon the earth,’
which represents certainty.”
“Just as metal used for ornaments is placed in the fire so that its pure substance is taken while its impurities are left behind, Allah accepts certainty and rejects doubt.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (12/498).

12579 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do you know who among Allah’s creation will be the first to enter Paradise?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“The first of Allah’s creation to enter Paradise will be the poor Emigrants. Through them the frontier posts are defended and dangers are faced. One of them dies while a need remains within his heart that he was unable to fulfill.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say to whichever of His angels He wills: ‘Go to them and greet them.’
The angels will say: ‘We are the inhabitants of Your heavens and Your chosen ones among Your creation. Do You command us to go to these people and greet them?’
He will say: ‘They were servants who worshipped Me and associated nothing with Me. Through them the frontiers were defended and dangers were faced. One of them died while a need remained within his heart that he was unable to fulfill.’
The angels will then come to them, entering upon them from every gate and saying:
‘Peace be upon you for the patience you maintained. How excellent is the final home!’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (6570) and al-Bazzār in his Musnad (2457), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7421).

12580 — Al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād, a member of Banū Fihr, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By Allah, the worldly life compared with the Hereafter is only like one of you placing this finger into the sea. Let him then see what it brings back.”
He pointed with his index finger.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Paradise and the Description of Its Bliss (2858).

12581 — Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed through the marketplace while returning from one of the elevated districts of Madinah, with the people gathered around him. He passed by a dead, small-eared young goat, took hold of it by the ear, and asked:
“Which of you would like to have this for one dirham?”
They replied:
“We would not like to have it for anything. What would we do with it?”
He asked:
“Would you like it to belong to you?”
They replied:
“By Allah, even if it were alive, its small ears would be a defect. What value could it have when it is dead?”
He said:
“By Allah, this world is more insignificant to Allah than this animal is to you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2957).
The word asakk means having small ears.

12582 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān narrated, each confirming the account of the other:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out during the time of al-Ḥudaybiyah. The hadith states that Suhayl ibn ʿAmr came and said:
“Come, write a treaty between us and you.”
The Prophet ﷺ summoned the scribe and said:
“Write: In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.”
Suhayl said:
“As for ‘al-Raḥmān,’ by Allah, I do not know what that is. Instead write: ‘In Your name, O Allah,’ as you used to write.”
The Muslims said:
“By Allah, we will write nothing except: ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Write: ‘In Your name, O Allah.’”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2731–2732).

12583 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to the husband and wife who invoked mutual curses:
“The punishment of this world is lighter than the punishment of the Hereafter…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1493).

12584 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The sun was eclipsed, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the eclipse prayer. The hadith states that the Companions said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we saw you reaching for something while standing, and then we saw you draw back.”
He said:
“Paradise was shown to me, and I reached for a cluster of grapes from it. Had I taken it, you would have continued eating from it for as long as this world remained.”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Eclipse Prayer (445), al-Bukhārī (1052), and Muslim (907).

12585 — Abū Ḥāzim narrated from Sahl ibn Saʿd that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“In Paradise there is a tree beneath whose shade a rider could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Abū Ḥāzim said:
I narrated this to al-Nuʿmān ibn Abī ʿAyyāsh, and he said:
Abū Saʿīd narrated to me that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“In Paradise there is a tree beneath whose shade a rider upon a swift, lean, well-trained horse could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6552–6553) and Muslim (2827–2828).

12586 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever would like his provision to be expanded and his lifespan to be extended should maintain his family ties.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5986) and Muslim (2557).

12587 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have been given five things that were not given to anyone before me: I have been supported through fear cast into the enemy from the distance of a month’s journey; the earth has been made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification, so wherever a man from my nation is overtaken by the time of prayer, let him pray; war spoils have been made lawful for me though they were not lawful for anyone before me; I have been granted intercession; and every prophet was sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to all people.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (335) and Muslim (521).

12588 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Allah favored Muḥammad ﷺ above the prophets, peace be upon them, and above the inhabitants of heaven.
They asked:
“Ibn ʿAbbās, how did He favor him above the inhabitants of heaven?”
He replied:
Allah said concerning the inhabitants of heaven:
“Whoever among them says, ‘I am a deity besides Him,’ We will repay him with Hell. Thus do We repay the wrongdoers.”
 [Sūrat al-Anbiyāʾ 21:29]
But Allah, the Exalted, said to Muḥammad ﷺ:
“Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory.”
 [Sūrat al-Fatḥ 48:1]
They asked:
“How did He favor him above the prophets, peace be upon them?”
He replied:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“We did not send any messenger except in the language of his people so that he might make matters clear to them.”
But Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said to Muḥammad ﷺ:
“We have not sent you except to all humanity as a bearer of glad tidings and a warner, but most people do not know.”
 [Sūrat Sabaʾ 34:28]
Thus, He sent him to both the jinn and humankind.
Good: Narrated by al-Dārimī (48), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/239–240), and al-Ḥākim (2/350).

12589 — Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Moses once stood among his people and reminded them of the days of Allah, and the days of Allah are His blessings.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11196), Aḥmad (21128), and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (13/597–598).

12590 — Abū Dharr narrated from the Prophet ﷺ, concerning what he narrated from Allah, Blessed and Exalted, that He said:
“My servants, you can never attain the ability to harm Me so as to harm Me, nor can you attain the ability to benefit Me so as to benefit Me.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the human and jinn among you, were all as pious as the most pious heart of a single man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the least.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the human and jinn among you, were all as wicked as the most wicked heart of a single man among you, that would not diminish My dominion in the least.
My servants, if the first and last of you, and the human and jinn among you, were all to stand together upon one plain and ask Me, and I were to give every person what he requested, that would diminish what I possess no more than a needle diminishes the sea when dipped into it…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2577).

12591 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said:
“Tell me of a tree that resembles—or is like—the Muslim. Its leaves do not fall, and it does not, and it does not, and it does not, and it gives its fruit at all times.”
Ibn ʿUmar said:
It occurred to me that it was the date palm, but I saw that Abū Bakr and ʿUmar were not speaking, so I disliked speaking before them.
When they did not answer, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“It is the date palm.”
After we rose, I said to ʿUmar:
“Father, by Allah, it had occurred to me that it was the date palm.”
He asked:
“What prevented you from speaking?”
I replied:
“I did not see you speaking, so I disliked speaking or saying anything.”
ʿUmar said:
“Had you said it, that would have been more beloved to me than such-and-such.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4698) and Muslim (2811:64).
The words:
“Its root is firmly fixed”
mean that the tree’s root is firmly established in the earth.
The words:
“Its branches are in the sky”
mean that its branches spread high above.
Likewise, the foundation of faith is firmly rooted in the believer’s heart—namely, the declaration that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah—while his righteous deeds ascend to heaven.
The words:
“It does not, and it does not, and it does not, and it gives its fruit at all times,”
mean that certain things do not afflict it, but the narrator did not preserve the omitted descriptions. The Prophet ﷺ then began a new statement:
“It gives its fruit at all times.”

12592 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said concerning the words:
“Allah makes those who believe firm with the steadfast word”:
“It was revealed concerning the punishment of the grave. The deceased will be asked, ‘Who is your Lord?’ He will reply, ‘My Lord is Allah, and my Prophet is Muḥammad ﷺ.’ This is the meaning of Allah’s words: ‘Allah makes those who believe firm with the steadfast word in the worldly life and in the Hereafter.’”
In another wording:
“When the believer is seated in his grave, he is questioned, and he testifies that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah. This is the meaning of His words: ‘Allah makes those who believe firm with the steadfast word.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1369) and Muslim (2871).

12593 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Those who exchanged Allah’s favor for disbelief”:
“By Allah, they are the disbelievers of Quraysh.”
ʿAmr ibn Dīnār said:
“They are Quraysh, and Muḥammad ﷺ is Allah’s favor.”
Regarding the words:
“They led their people into the home of destruction,”
he said:
“The Fire, through what occurred on the day of Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3977).

12594 — Abū al-Ṭufayl narrated that he heard ʿAlī when Ibn al-Kawwāʾ asked him about the verse:
“Have you not seen those who exchanged Allah’s favor for disbelief and led their people into the home of destruction?”
ʿAlī replied:
“They are the disbelievers of Quraysh on the day of Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11203) and Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (13/672).

12595 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ recited Allah’s words concerning Abraham:
“My Lord, they have led many people astray. Whoever follows me is truly of me, and whoever disobeys me, then You are Forgiving and Merciful.”
He also recited the words of Jesus, peace be upon him:
“If You punish them, they are Your servants; and if You forgive them, You are truly the Almighty, the All-Wise.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:118]
He then raised his hands and said:
“O Allah, my nation, my nation!”
And he wept.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“Gabriel, go to Muḥammad—and your Lord knows best—and ask him what causes him to weep.”
Gabriel, peace be upon him, came and asked him, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ informed him of what he had said, though Allah already knew.
Allah then said:
“Gabriel, go to Muḥammad and say: We will surely please you concerning your nation and will not grieve you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (202).

12596 — Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The people will be gathered on the Day of Resurrection upon a pale white land resembling a loaf of pure flour, containing no landmark belonging to anyone.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6521) and Muslim (2790). The wording is that of Muslim.
The words:
“Containing no landmark belonging to anyone”
mean that there will be no sign of habitation, building, or any other trace.

12597 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The earth on the Day of Resurrection will become one loaf of bread. The Almighty will turn it with His hand just as one of you turns his bread during a journey, as hospitality for the people of Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6520) and Muslim (2792).
The phrase:
“As hospitality for the people of Paradise”
refers to what is prepared for a guest upon his arrival.

12598 — Thawbān, the freedman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, narrated:
I was standing beside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when a Jewish scholar came and said:
“Peace be upon you, Muḥammad.”
I pushed him with such force that he almost fell.
He asked:
“Why did you push me?”
I replied:
“Why did you not say, ‘O Messenger of Allah’?”
The Jewish man said:
“We only call him by the name given to him by his family.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My name is Muḥammad, the name by which my family named me.”
The Jewish man said:
“I have come to ask you questions.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Will what I tell you benefit you?”
He replied:
“I will listen with my ears.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ drew lines upon the ground with a stick he held and said:
“Ask.”
The Jewish man asked:
“Where will the people be on the Day when the earth is changed into another earth and the heavens as well?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“They will be in darkness before the bridge.”
He asked:
“Who will be the first people to cross?”
He replied:
“The poor Emigrants.”
The Jewish man asked:
“What will be their welcome meal when they enter Paradise?”
He replied:
“The extra lobe of the fish’s liver.”
He asked:
“What food will they be given after that?”
He replied:
“The bull of Paradise that used to graze along its edges will be slaughtered for them.”
He asked:
“What will they drink with it?”
He replied:
“From a spring in Paradise called Salsabīl.”
He said:
“You have spoken the truth.”
He continued:
“I have also come to ask you about something known by no one on earth except a prophet or one or two men.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Will it benefit you if I tell you?”
He replied:
“I will listen with my ears.”
He said:
“I have come to ask you about the child.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“The man’s fluid is white, and the woman’s fluid is yellow. When they meet, if the man’s fluid prevails over the woman’s, the child is male by Allah’s permission; and if the woman’s fluid prevails over the man’s, the child is female by Allah’s permission.”
The Jewish man said:
“You have spoken the truth, and you are indeed a prophet.”
He then turned and left.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“He asked me about matters concerning which I had no knowledge until Allah gave me that knowledge.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (315:34).

12599 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“On the Day when the earth will be changed into another earth and the heavens as well.”
I asked:
“Where will the people be on that day, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Upon the bridge.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2791).
The changing of the earth means the transformation of its created form, including its mountains, rivers, and trees, as well as the alteration of its natural conditions, such as heat, cold, air, and gravity. No one knows the true nature of its condition on the Day of Resurrection except Allah, Glorified and Exalted.

12600 — Abū Mālik al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Four practices from the time of ignorance will remain within my nation and will not be abandoned: boasting about ancestry, attacking people’s lineages, seeking rain through the stars, and wailing over the dead.”
He also said:
“If a woman who wails over the dead does not repent before she dies, she will be raised on the Day of Resurrection wearing a garment of pitch and a coat of severe skin disease.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (934).

12601 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah will gather the people on the Day of Resurrection, and they will say, ‘Let us seek someone to intercede with our Lord so that He may relieve us from this place.’
They will go to Adam and say, ‘You are the one whom Allah created with His hand, into whom He breathed of His spirit, and before whom He commanded the angels to prostrate. Intercede for us with your Lord.’
He will reply, ‘I am not fit for that,’ and he will mention his sin. He will say, ‘Go to Noah, the first messenger Allah sent.’
They will go to him, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that,’ and mention his sin. He will say, ‘Go to Abraham, whom Allah took as an intimate friend.’
They will go to him, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that,’ and mention his sin. He will say, ‘Go to Moses, to whom Allah spoke directly.’
They will go to him, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that,’ and mention his sin. He will say, ‘Go to Jesus.’
They will go to him, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that. Go to Muḥammad ﷺ, whose earlier and later sins have been forgiven.’
They will come to me, and I will seek permission to enter before my Lord. When I see Him, I will fall in prostration. He will leave me as long as He wills, and then it will be said to me, ‘Raise your head. Ask and you will be given; speak and you will be heard; intercede and your intercession will be accepted.’
I will raise my head and praise my Lord with words of praise that He will teach me. I will then intercede, and a limit will be set for me. I will bring them out of the Fire and admit them into Paradise.
I will then return and fall in prostration in the same manner a third or fourth time, until no one remains in the Fire except those whom the Qur’an has confined there.”
Qatādah would explain this by saying:
“That is, those for whom eternal residence in the Fire has become binding.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6565) and Muslim (193).

12602 — Ṣāliḥ ibn Abī Ṭarīf narrated:
I asked Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī:
“Did you hear the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say anything concerning the verse:
‘Perhaps those who disbelieve will wish that they had been Muslims’?”
He replied:
“Yes. I heard him say:
“Allah will bring some people out of the Fire after exacting His punishment from them.
When Allah admits them into the Fire with the polytheists, the polytheists will say to them, ‘Did you not claim in the world that you were Allah’s close allies? Why then are you in the Fire with us?’
When Allah hears this from them, He will permit intercession. The angels and prophets will intercede for them until they are brought out by Allah’s permission.
When they have been brought out, the disbelievers will say, ‘If only we had been like them, so that intercession might reach us and we too might be brought out of the Fire.’
This is the meaning of Allah’s words:
‘Perhaps those who disbelieve will wish that they had been Muslims.’
They will be called ‘the people brought out of Hell’ in Paradise because of the blackness upon their faces. They will say, ‘Our Lord, remove this name from us.’
He will command them to wash in a river of Paradise, and that blackness will disappear from them.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7432).
Ibn ʿAbbās and Anas ibn Mālik used to explain the verse:
“Perhaps those who disbelieve will wish that they had been Muslims”
by saying:
“That will occur on the day when Allah gathers the sinful Muslims and the polytheists in the Fire.”
They then related an account similar to the preceding hadith. It was narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr.
Similar explanations were narrated from many Companions and Successors. Some scholars said that when the polytheists say to the Muslims:
“What benefit did that which you used to worship bring you?”
Allah will become angry on behalf of the believers and command the angels and prophets to intercede. They will intercede, and the believers will be brought out of the Fire. Even Iblīs will stretch himself upward, hoping that he too might be among those brought out.
At that point:
“Those who disbelieve will wish that they had been Muslims.”
The matter therefore relates to intercession. No one in whose heart there is the weight of an atom of faith will remain in the Fire, and at that time the disbeliever will wish that he had been a Muslim.
The word:
“Perhaps”
is ordinarily used to indicate something infrequent, but here it conveys frequency and intensity. It also usually precedes a past-tense verb, whereas here it refers to the future because that future event is certain to occur.

12603 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah decrees a matter in heaven, the angels beat their wings in submission to His words, producing a sound like a chain being dragged over a smooth rock.
When fear is removed from their hearts, they ask, ‘What did your Lord say?’ They reply to the one who asked, ‘He spoke the truth, and He is the Most High, the Most Great.’
The one who steals a hearing then hears it, and those who steal hearings are positioned one above another in this manner.”
Sufyān demonstrated this with his hand, tilting it and spreading his fingers apart.
“One of them hears a word and passes it to the one beneath him. The next passes it to the one beneath him, until it is placed upon the tongue of a sorcerer or soothsayer.
A flaming meteor may strike him before he passes it on, or he may pass it on before the meteor reaches him. The soothsayer then mixes one hundred lies with that single word.
People then say, ‘Did he not tell us on such-and-such a day that such-and-such would happen?’ He is believed because of that one word heard from heaven.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4800).

12604 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Some people asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about soothsayers. He replied:
“They are nothing.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, they sometimes tell us something that later proves true.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“That is a word from the jinn which a jinn snatches and repeatedly whispers into the ear of his associate like the clucking of a hen. They then mix more than one hundred lies with it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7561) and Muslim (2228:123). The wording is that of Muslim.
In al-Bukhārī’s wording:
“He repeatedly murmurs it into the ear of his associate like the clucking of a hen.”
The words:
“They are nothing”
mean that the speech of soothsayers contains no genuine or reliable knowledge.
The verb used here refers to producing a repeated sound and repeating words into the listener’s ear until he understands them.
Ibn Qutaybah said in Taʾwīl Mushkil al-Qurʾān:
“The stoning of the devils with meteors existed before the Prophet’s mission ﷺ, but the guarding of the heavens was not as intense as it became after his mission.”

12605 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The angels were created from light, the jinn were created from a smokeless flame of fire, and Adam was created from what has been described to you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2996).

12606 — Samurah ibn Jundab narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Among them are those whom the Fire will reach up to their ankles; among them are those whom it will reach up to their knees; among them are those whom it will reach up to their waist; and among them are those whom it will reach up to their collarbone.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2845:33).
The words:
“For each gate is an assigned portion of them”
mean that every level has its appointed inhabitants from among the criminals, each according to the severity of his wrongdoing.
As for the reported names of these levels and the claims identifying the inhabitants of each one, these are merely conjectures unsupported by the Qur’an or authentic Sunnah. Such matters belong to the unseen, which no one knows except Allah, Glorified and Exalted.
It is therefore better to keep books of Qur’anic commentary free from such unsupported claims than to include them. Allah alone is the One whose help is sought.

12607 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The believers will be saved from the Fire and then detained upon a bridge between Paradise and Hell. Retribution will be taken for the wrongs they committed against one another in the worldly life. When they have been purified and cleansed, they will be granted permission to enter Paradise.
By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, each of them will know the way to his home in Paradise better than he knew the way to his home in the worldly life.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6535).

12608 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Gabriel came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Khadījah is coming to you carrying a vessel containing a condiment, food, or drink. When she comes, convey to her greetings of peace from her Lord and from me, and give her the glad tidings of a house in Paradise made of hollowed pearls, in which there will be neither noise nor weariness.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3820) and Muslim (2432).

12609 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“When Allah created mercy, He created it as one hundred portions. He retained ninety-nine portions with Himself and sent one portion among all His creation.
If the disbeliever knew the full extent of the mercy that is with Allah, he would never despair of Paradise. If the believer knew the full extent of the punishment that is with Allah, he would never feel secure from the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6469).

12610 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not enter the dwellings of these people who were punished unless you are weeping. If you are not weeping, then do not enter them, lest what afflicted them should afflict you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4702) and Muslim (2980).

12611 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
We passed by al-Ḥijr with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said:
“Do not enter the dwellings of those who wronged themselves unless you are weeping, out of fear that what afflicted them might also afflict you.”
He then urged his mount forward and quickly passed beyond the area.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3381) and Muslim (2980:39). The wording is that of Muslim.

12612 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The people camped with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the land of Thamūd at al-Ḥijr. They drew water from its wells and kneaded dough with it.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commanded them to pour out the water they had drawn, feed the dough to the camels, and obtain water only from the well that the she-camel of Ṣāliḥ used to visit.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3379) and Muslim (2981).

12613 — Abū Saʿīd ibn al-Muʿallā narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ passed by me while I was praying and called me. I did not go to him until I had completed my prayer, after which I came to him.
He asked:
“What prevented you from coming?”
I replied:
“I was praying.”
He said:
“Did Allah not say:
‘Believers, respond to Allah and the Messenger when he calls you to that which gives you life, and know that Allah intervenes between a person and his heart and that to Him you will be gathered’?”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:24]
He then said:
“Shall I teach you the greatest sūrah in the Qur’an before I leave the mosque?”
When the Prophet ﷺ was about to leave the mosque, I reminded him. He said:
“‘All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all creation’ is the seven oft-repeated verses and the magnificent Qur’an that I have been given.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4703).

12614 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Mother of the Qur’an is the seven oft-repeated verses and the magnificent Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4704).
This interpretation was held by the Companions ʿUmar, ʿAlī, Ibn Masʿūd, Ibn ʿAbbās, and Abū Hurayrah, as well as a group of the Successors and their followers.
Ibn ʿUmar, Mujāhid, Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, al-Ḍaḥḥāk, and others said that it refers to the seven long sūrahs: al-Baqarah, Āl ʿImrān, al-Nisāʾ, al-Māʾidah, al-Anʿām, al-Aʿrāf, and Yūnus.
This view was also narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās and Ibn Masʿūd.

12615 — Abū Mūsā narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“My example and the example of that with which Allah sent me is like that of a man who came to his people and said, ‘My people, I have seen the army with my own eyes. I am the naked warner, so save yourselves!’
A group from among his people obeyed him. They departed during the early part of the night, traveled calmly, and escaped.
Another group rejected him and remained where they were until morning. The army attacked them at dawn, destroyed them, and completely overwhelmed them.
That is the example of the one who obeys me and follows the truth I have brought, and the example of the one who disobeys me and rejects the truth I have brought.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7283) and Muslim (2283).

12616 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
