“My example and that of the people is like a man who kindled a fire. When it illuminated everything around him, moths and other creatures that fall into fire began falling into it.
He tried to pull them away, but they overpowered him and rushed into it.
I am holding you back by your waistbands from the Fire, while you continue rushing into it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6483) and Muslim (2284).

12617 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My example is like that of a man who kindled a fire. When it illuminated everything around it, moths and other creatures that fall into fire began falling into it. He tried to restrain them, but they overpowered him and rushed into it.”
He then said:
“That is my example and yours. I am holding you back by your waistbands from the Fire, saying, ‘Come away from the Fire! Come away from the Fire!’ But you overpower me and rush into it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2284:18).

12618 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My example and yours is like that of a man who kindled a fire. Grasshoppers and moths began falling into it while he tried to drive them away. I am holding you back by your waistbands from the Fire, while you slip free from my hands.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2285).

12619 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I am holding you back by your waistbands, yet you overpower me and rush into the Fire as moths and grasshoppers rush into it. Soon I will release your waistbands and precede you to the Reservoir, and you will come to me together or in scattered groups.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah in al-Muṣannaf (11/451–452), whose wording is given here, and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (744).

12620 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“As We sent down upon the dividers”:
“They believed in part and disbelieved in part—the Jews and Christians.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4706).

12621 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Those who divided the Qur’an into parts”:
“They are the People of the Book. They divided it into sections, believing in some and disbelieving in others.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4705).
Some scholars said that the word “Qur’an” here means the scripture recited among them, namely the Torah and the Gospel. They believed in parts of their scripture and rejected other parts, such as the passages giving glad tidings of the Prophet ﷺ.
The statement of Ibn ʿAbbās can carry both meanings, while Mujāhid favored the second interpretation.

12622 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“We are sufficient for you against the mockers”:
The mockers were al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah, al-Aswad ibn ʿAbd Yaghūth, al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib Abū Zamʿah from Banū Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, al-Ḥārith ibn Ghayṭal al-Sahmī, and al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī.
Gabriel, peace be upon him, came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and the Messenger of Allah complained to him about these men.
Gabriel showed him Abū ʿAmr al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah and gestured toward the main vein in his arm.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You have done nothing.”
Gabriel replied:
“I have taken care of him for you.”
He then showed him al-Ḥārith ibn Ghayṭal al-Sahmī and gestured toward his abdomen.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“You have done nothing.”
Gabriel replied:
“I have taken care of him for you.”
He then showed him al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī and gestured toward the sole of his foot.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“You have done nothing.”
Gabriel replied:
“I have taken care of him for you.”
As for al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah, he passed by a man from Khuzāʿah who was attaching feathers to his arrows. An arrow struck the main vein in al-Walīd’s arm and severed it.
As for al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib, he became blind. Some reports explain his blindness in one way, while others state that he sat beneath a tree and began crying:
“My sons, will you not defend me? I am being destroyed! Thorns are being driven into my eyes!”
They replied:
“We see nothing.”
He remained in that condition until both of his eyes became blind.
As for al-Aswad ibn ʿAbd Yaghūth, sores appeared upon his head, and he died from them.
As for al-Ḥārith ibn Ghayṭal, yellow fluid accumulated in his abdomen until his excrement came out through his mouth, and he died from it.
As for al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil, one day a thorn from the shabraqah plant entered his foot. His foot became filled with its thorns, and he died.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (5/515–517) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (2/316–318).

12623 — Khārijah ibn Zayd al-Anṣārī narrated that Umm al-ʿAlāʾ, a woman from among the Anṣār who had pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ, told him that ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn was assigned to live with her household when the Anṣār drew lots to host the Emigrants.
Umm al-ʿAlāʾ said:
ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn stayed with us. He became ill, and we cared for him until he died. After we wrapped him in his garments, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered upon us.
I said:
“May Allah have mercy upon you, Abū al-Sāʾib. I testify that Allah has honored you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said to me:
“How do you know that Allah has honored him?”
I replied:
“I do not know. May my father and mother be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“As for ʿUthmān, by Allah, death has come to him, and I hope for good for him. Yet by Allah, although I am the Messenger of Allah, I do not know what will be done with me.”
She said:
“By Allah, after that I never again declared anyone pure before Allah.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2687).

12624 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever calls others to guidance will receive a reward equal to the rewards of those who follow him, without that diminishing their rewards in any way. Whoever calls others to misguidance will bear a sin equal to the sins of those who follow him, without that diminishing their sins in any way.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2674).

12625 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“My brother is suffering from diarrhea.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Give him honey to drink.”
The man gave him honey and returned, saying:
“I gave him honey, but it only increased his diarrhea.”
The Prophet ﷺ repeated the instruction three times. When the man came a fourth time, he again said:
“Give him honey to drink.”
The man replied:
“I have given it to him, but it has only increased his diarrhea.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah has spoken the truth, and your brother’s stomach has lied.”
The man gave him honey again, and he recovered.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5716) and Muslim (2217).
Other hadiths concerning this subject are mentioned in the Book of Medicine.

12626 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to supplicate:
“O Allah, I seek refuge in You from miserliness, laziness, the most feeble stage of life, the punishment of the grave, the trial of the Antichrist, and the trials of life and death.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4707) and Muslim (2706:52).

12627 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting in the courtyard of his home in Makkah, ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn passed by and smiled at him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Will you not sit down?”
He replied:
“Certainly.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat facing him. While speaking with him, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ suddenly raised his gaze toward the sky and looked upward for some time.
He then gradually lowered his gaze until it came to rest upon the ground to his right. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned away from ʿUthmān, who was sitting with him, toward the place where his gaze had settled.
He began moving his head as though carefully trying to understand what was being said to him, while Ibn Maẓʿūn watched.
When the matter had concluded and he had understood what was being said, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his gaze toward the sky as he had done the first time and followed it until it disappeared into the sky.
He then turned back toward ʿUthmān and resumed his original sitting position.
ʿUthmān said:
“Muḥammad, throughout the time I have sat with you and visited you, I have never seen you do what you did today.”
He asked:
“What did you see me do?”
He replied:
“I saw you raise your gaze toward the sky, then lower it to the place on your right. You turned toward it, away from me, and began moving your head as though trying to understand something being said to you.”
He asked:
“Did you notice that?”
ʿUthmān replied:
“Yes.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The messenger of Allah came to me just now while you were sitting here.”
He asked:
“The messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“What did he say to you?”
He replied:
“Indeed, Allah commands justice, excellence, and giving to relatives, and He forbids immorality, wrongdoing, and oppression. He instructs you so that you may take heed.”
ʿUthmān said:
“That was when faith became firmly established in my heart and I came to love Muḥammad ﷺ.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2919), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (893), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (9/27–28).

12628 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are four characteristics which, if all are found in a person, make him a complete hypocrite: when he speaks, he lies; when he promises, he breaks his promise; when he makes a covenant, he betrays it; and when he disputes, he behaves wickedly. Whoever possesses one of these characteristics possesses a characteristic of hypocrisy until he abandons it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3178) and Muslim (58).

12629 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks, he lies; when he promises, he breaks his promise; and when he is entrusted, he betrays the trust.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (33) and Muslim (59).

12630 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Successful indeed is the one who embraces Islam, is provided with what is sufficient, and is made content by Allah with what He has given him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1054).

12631 — Faḍālah ibn ʿUbayd narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Blessed is the one who is guided to Islam, whose livelihood is sufficient, and who remains content.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2349) and Aḥmad (23944), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (705) and al-Ḥākim (1/34–35).
Regarding the words:
“We will surely reward them according to the best of what they used to do”:

12632 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When a disbeliever performs a good deed, he is given some worldly benefit because of it. As for the believer, Allah stores his good deeds for him in the Hereafter and grants him provision in this world because of his obedience.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2808:57).

12633 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Some people in Makkah had embraced Islam but concealed their faith. When the polytheists set out for Badr, they forced these Muslims to accompany them. Some of them were killed at Badr while among the polytheists.
The Muslims said:
“These companions of ours were Muslims who had been brought out by force.”
They therefore sought forgiveness for them.
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Indeed, when the angels take the souls of those who wronged themselves, they will ask, ‘What condition were you in?’ They will reply, ‘We were oppressed in the land.’ The angels will say, ‘Was Allah’s earth not spacious enough for you to emigrate?’ Their refuge will be Hell, and what an evil destination it is.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:97]
The Muslims wrote this verse to those who remained in Makkah. They set out to emigrate, but while they were partway along the road, the polytheists learned of their departure, caught up with them, and forced them to return.
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Among the people are those who say, ‘We believe in Allah,’ but when they are harmed for Allah’s sake, they regard the persecution of people as though it were Allah’s punishment. Yet if victory comes from your Lord, they will surely say, ‘We were with you.’ Does Allah not know best what is within the hearts of all beings?”
 [Sūrat al-ʿAnkabūt 29:10]
The Muslims wrote this verse to them, and they became deeply saddened.
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Then indeed your Lord, toward those who emigrated after being persecuted, then struggled and remained patient—your Lord after that is certainly Forgiving and Merciful.”
The Muslims wrote this verse to them as well.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2204).

12634 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“We are the last nation in this world but the first on the Day of Resurrection. They were given the scripture before us, and this was the day made obligatory upon them, but they differed concerning it. Allah guided us to it, so the people come after us in this regard: the Jews observe the following day and the Christians the day after that.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (876) and Muslim (855). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12635 — Abū Hurayrah and Ḥudhayfah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah caused those before us to miss Friday. The Jews had Saturday, and the Christians had Sunday. Then Allah brought us and guided us to Friday.
Thus came Friday, Saturday, and Sunday in sequence, and in the same way they will follow us on the Day of Resurrection.
We are the last among the people of this world, yet the first on the Day of Resurrection, and judgment will be passed for us before the rest of creation.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (856).

12636 — Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated:
On the day of Uḥud, sixty-four men from the Anṣār and six from the Emigrants were killed, including Ḥamzah. The polytheists mutilated their bodies.
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If we are ever given a day against the polytheists like this one, we will mutilate more of them than they mutilated of us.”
On the day of the conquest of Makkah, an unidentified man said:
“There will be no Quraysh after today.”
A caller appointed by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ proclaimed:
“Grant safety to everyone, black and white, except such-and-such individuals,”
and he named certain people.
Allah, Blessed and Exalted, then revealed:
“If you retaliate, then retaliate only to the extent of the harm done to you. But if you remain patient, that is certainly better for those who are patient.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“We will remain patient and will not retaliate.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3129) and ʿAbdullāh ibn Aḥmad in Zawāʾid al-Musnad (21229–21230). The wording is his.

12637 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ would not go to sleep until he had recited Sūrat Banī Isrāʾīl and Sūrat al-Zumar.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2920), Aḥmad (24388), and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (712).

12638 — Ibn Masʿūd said concerning Sūrat Banī Isrāʾīl, Sūrat al-Kahf, and Sūrat Maryam:
“They are among the earliest and finest sūrahs, and they are among my ancient treasures.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4708).
The word al-ʿitāq is the plural of ʿatīq, meaning ancient. It may also refer to anything that has reached the highest degree of excellence. Ibn Masʿūd intended to express the virtue of these sūrahs because they contain narratives, accounts of the prophets, and histories of earlier nations.
The word tilādī refers to long-held, inherited wealth. He meant that these were among the earliest sūrahs revealed at the beginning of Islam, since they are Makkan, and that they were among the first portions of the Qur’an he recited and memorized. Allah knows best.

12639 — Abū Dharr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The roof of my house was opened while I was in Makkah. Gabriel, peace be upon him, descended, opened my chest, and washed it with Zamzam water. He then brought a golden basin filled with wisdom and faith, poured it into my chest, and closed it.
He then took me by the hand and ascended with me to heaven. When we reached the lowest heaven, Gabriel said to its gatekeeper, ‘Open.’
He asked, ‘Who is this?’ Gabriel replied, ‘Gabriel.’ He asked, ‘Is anyone with you?’ He replied, ‘Yes, Muḥammad ﷺ is with me.’ He asked, ‘Was he sent for?’ Gabriel replied, ‘Yes,’ so he opened the gate.
When we ascended into the lowest heaven, I saw a man with groups of people to his right and groups to his left. Whenever he looked to his right, he laughed, and whenever he looked to his left, he wept.
He said, ‘Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous son.’
I asked, ‘Gabriel, who is this?’ He replied, ‘This is Adam, peace be upon him. The groups to his right and left are the souls of his descendants. Those on the right are the people of Paradise, and those on the left are the people of the Fire. When he looks to his right, he laughs, and when he looks to his left, he weeps.’
Gabriel then ascended with me until he reached the second heaven. He said to its gatekeeper, ‘Open.’ Its gatekeeper asked him the same questions as the gatekeeper of the lowest heaven, and then opened it.”
Anas ibn Mālik said:
The Prophet ﷺ mentioned that he encountered Adam, Enoch, Jesus, Moses, and Abraham—may Allah’s blessings be upon them all—in the heavens. The narrator did not precisely establish the level of each, except that he mentioned Adam, peace be upon him, in the lowest heaven and Abraham in the sixth heaven.
He said:
When Gabriel and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed Enoch, he said:
“Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous brother.”
I asked:
“Who is this?”
Gabriel replied:
“This is Enoch.”
He continued:
“I then passed Moses, peace be upon him, and he said, ‘Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous brother.’
I asked, ‘Who is this?’ Gabriel replied, ‘This is Moses.’
I then passed Jesus, who said, ‘Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous brother.’ I asked, ‘Who is this?’ Gabriel replied, ‘This is Jesus son of Mary.’
I then passed Abraham, peace be upon him, who said, ‘Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous son.’ I asked, ‘Who is this?’ Gabriel replied, ‘This is Abraham.’”
Ibn Shihāb said:
Ibn Ḥazm informed me that Ibn ʿAbbās and Abū Ḥabbah al-Anṣārī used to narrate that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was then raised until I reached a level at which I could hear the scratching of the pens.”
Ibn Ḥazm and Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah then prescribed fifty prayers for my nation.”
“I returned with this command until I passed Moses. Moses, peace be upon him, asked, ‘What has your Lord prescribed for your nation?’
I replied, ‘He has prescribed fifty prayers for them.’
Moses said, ‘Return to your Lord, for your nation will not be able to bear that.’
I returned to my Lord, and He reduced them by half.
I returned to Moses and informed him. He said, ‘Return to your Lord, for your nation will not be able to bear that.’
I returned to my Lord, and He said, ‘They are five, yet they are equal to fifty. My word is not changed.’
I returned to Moses, who said, ‘Return to your Lord.’ I replied, ‘I have become ashamed before my Lord.’
Gabriel then took me until we reached the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary. It was covered by colors whose nature I do not know.
I was then admitted into Paradise, where I saw domes of pearls, and its soil was musk.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (349) and Muslim (163).

12640 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
On the night the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was taken on the Night Journey, two vessels—one containing wine and the other milk—were brought to him in Aelia. He looked at them and chose the milk.
Gabriel said:
“All praise belongs to Allah, Who guided you to the natural disposition. Had you chosen the wine, your nation would have gone astray.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4709) and Muslim (168).

12641 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“When Quraysh accused me of lying, I stood in the Ḥijr, and Allah displayed Jerusalem clearly before me. I began describing its features to them while looking directly at it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3886) and Muslim (170).
Many other hadiths concerning the Night Journey and Ascension are mentioned in the section on the Prophetic biography.

12642 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the expedition of Baṭn Buwāṭ while he was pursuing al-Majdī ibn ʿAmr al-Juhanī.
Five, six, or seven of us would take turns riding a single water-carrying camel. When the turn of an Anṣārī man came, he made his camel kneel, mounted it, and urged it forward, but it hesitated somewhat.
He said to it:
“Move! May Allah curse you!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Who is this person cursing his camel?”
He replied:
“It is I, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Dismount from it, for a cursed animal must not accompany us. Do not supplicate against yourselves, do not supplicate against your children, and do not supplicate against your property, lest your supplication coincide with a time when Allah grants whatever is requested and He answers you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3009).

12643 — Jābir narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Every person’s fate and deeds are fastened to his neck.”
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (14878).

12644 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Four people will present their excuses before Allah, Mighty and Majestic, on the Day of Resurrection: a deaf man who could not hear, a mentally impaired man, an extremely elderly man, and a man who died during an interval between messengers.
The deaf man will say, ‘My Lord, Islam came, but I could not hear anything.’
The mentally impaired man will say, ‘My Lord, Islam came, but children were throwing animal dung at me.’
The elderly man will say, ‘My Lord, Islam came, but I could no longer understand anything.’
The man who died during an interval between messengers will say, ‘My Lord, no messenger came to me.’
Allah will take their covenant that they will obey Him, and He will command them, ‘Enter the Fire.’
By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, had they entered it, it would have become cool and peaceful for them.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in al-Qaḍāʾ wa-al-Qadar (3/910–911).
Imām Aḥmad also narrated it (16302) with an authentic chain, ending with the wording:
“Whoever enters it will find it cool and peaceful, while whoever refuses to enter it will be dragged into it.”
Other hadiths carrying this meaning have also been narrated. Their detailed discussion appears in the Book of Faith.

12645 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“May his nose be rubbed in the dust! May his nose be rubbed in the dust! May his nose be rubbed in the dust!”
It was asked:
“Who, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The one who reaches the time when one or both of his parents are elderly, yet still does not enter Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2551).

12646 — Ṭāriq ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Muḥāribī narrated in a lengthy hadith:
We arrived in Madinah the following day and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ standing upon the pulpit addressing the people. I heard him say:
“The giving hand is the upper hand. Begin with those under your care: your mother, your father, your sister, and your brother, then the nearest relative and the next nearest.”
The hadith continues.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6562) and al-Ḥākim (2/611–612).

12647 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever would like his provision to be expanded and his lifespan to be extended should maintain his family ties.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5986) and Muslim (2557:21).

12648 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“The example of a miser and a charitable person is that of two men wearing two coats of iron extending from their chests to their collarbones. Whenever the charitable person spends, his coat expands—or becomes complete—over his body until it conceals his fingertips and erases his footprints. As for the miser, whenever he intends to spend anything, every ring of the coat clings tightly to its place. He tries to widen it, but it does not widen.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1443) and Muslim (1021).

12649 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no morning on which the servants awaken except that two angels descend. One of them says, ‘O Allah, grant compensation to the one who spends,’ while the other says, ‘O Allah, bring destruction upon the wealth of the one who withholds.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1442) and Muslim (1010).
The word:
“Compensation”
means a replacement for what he spent, whether the spending was obligatory or recommended.
The words:
“Bring destruction upon the wealth of the one who withholds”
mean: destroy what he has withheld. This supplication applies to one who refuses to fulfill obligatory financial duties. It does not include someone who merely refrains from recommended spending, since such a person is not supplicated against.

12650 — ʿAbdullāh narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Which sin is greatest in the sight of Allah?”
He replied:
“That you set up a rival with Allah even though He created you.”
I said:
“That is indeed tremendous. Then which?”
He replied:
“That you kill your child out of fear that he will share your food.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you commit adultery with your neighbor’s wife.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4477) and Muslim (86).

12651 — Abū Umāmah narrated:
A young man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, permit me to commit adultery.”
The people turned toward him, rebuked him, and said:
“Stop! Stop!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Come closer.”
The young man came close and sat down.
He asked:
“Would you like that for your mother?”
The young man replied:
“No, by Allah. May Allah make me your ransom.”
He said:
“Nor do people like it for their mothers.”
He asked:
“Would you like it for your daughter?”
He replied:
“No, by Allah, O Messenger of Allah. May Allah make me your ransom.”
He said:
“Nor do people like it for their daughters.”
He asked:
“Would you like it for your sister?”
He replied:
“No, by Allah. May Allah make me your ransom.”
He said:
“Nor do people like it for their sisters.”
He asked:
“Would you like it for your paternal aunt?”
He replied:
“No, by Allah. May Allah make me your ransom.”
He said:
“Nor do people like it for their paternal aunts.”
He asked:
“Would you like it for your maternal aunt?”
He replied:
“No, by Allah. May Allah make me your ransom.”
He said:
“Nor do people like it for their maternal aunts.”
The Prophet ﷺ then placed his hand upon him and said:
“O Allah, forgive his sin, purify his heart, and guard his chastity.”
After that, the young man no longer turned toward anything unlawful.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (22211) and al-Ṭabarānī (7679).

12652 — ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The blood of a Muslim who testifies that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah is not lawful except in one of three cases: a previously married person who commits adultery, a life in retaliation for a life, and one who abandons his religion and separates from the community.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6878) and Muslim (1676).

12653 — Abū Dharr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Abū Dharr, I consider you weak, and I love for you what I love for myself. Never accept authority over even two people, and never assume responsibility for an orphan’s property.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1826).

12654 — Abū Qatādah and Abū al-Dahmāʾ narrated:
We came upon a Bedouin man who said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ took me by the hand and began teaching me from what Allah had taught him. He said:
‘You will never abandon anything out of fear of Allah except that Allah will grant you something better in its place.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20739).

12655 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When death approached Allah’s Prophet Noah, peace be upon him, he said to his son, ‘I will summarize my final counsel to you: I command you with two things and forbid you from two things.
I command you to hold firmly to “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.” If the seven heavens and seven earths were placed upon one side of a scale and “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah” upon the other, it would outweigh them.
If the seven heavens and seven earths formed one solid ring, the declaration “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah” would break it apart.
I also command you to say, “Glory and praise belong to Allah,” for it is the prayer of every created thing, and through it creation is granted provision.
I forbid you from associating partners with Allah and from arrogance…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (6583).
Its detailed discussion appears in the Book of Faith, in the chapter concerning Noah’s instruction to his son not to associate anything with Allah.

12656 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“We used to hear the food glorifying Allah while it was being eaten.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3579).
The words:
“We used to hear the food glorifying Allah while it was being eaten”
refer to what occurred during the lifetime and in the presence of the Prophet ﷺ. A narration of al-Ismāʿīlī, cited in Fatḥ al-Bārī (6/592), states:
“We used to eat food with the Prophet ﷺ while hearing the food glorifying Allah.”

12657 — ʿAmr ibn ʿAbasah al-Sulamī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whenever the sun rises, nothing from Allah’s creation remains except that it glorifies and praises Allah, Mighty and Majestic, apart from Satan and the most rebellious members of humankind.”
He was asked about the most rebellious members of humankind and replied:
“The worst of creation,”
or:
“The worst of Allah’s creation, Mighty and Majestic.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in Musnad al-Shāmiyyīn (960) and Ibn al-Sunnī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (150). The wording is that of Ibn al-Sunnī.

12658 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Recitation was made easy for David. He would order that his riding animal be saddled, and he would finish reciting before it had been saddled.”
The narrator referred to it as “the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4713).
The words:
“He would finish reciting before it had been saddled”
refer to his scripture. What is meant is the Psalms given to him by Allah, known in the Old Testament as the Book of Psalms, consisting of 150 psalms. It does not refer to the Qur’an revealed to this nation.

12659 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said concerning the verse:
“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of nearness to their Lord”:
“A group of human beings used to worship a group of jinn. The jinn embraced Islam, but the human beings continued worshipping them. Then the verse was revealed:
‘Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of nearness to their Lord.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4714) and Muslim (3030:29).

12660 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said concerning the verse:
“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of nearness to their Lord”:
“It was revealed concerning a group of Arabs who worshipped a group of jinn. The jinn embraced Islam without the human beings who worshipped them realizing it. Then the verse was revealed:
‘Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of nearness to their Lord.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3030:30).

12661 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The people of Makkah asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to turn Mount al-Ṣafā into gold and remove the mountains from around them so that they could cultivate the land.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“If you wish, We will give them what they have requested. But if they then disbelieve, they will be destroyed as those before them were destroyed. Or, if you wish, We will grant them more time, so that descendants may come from them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“No. Rather, I will grant them more time.”
Allah then revealed:
“Nothing prevents Us from sending the signs except that earlier peoples rejected them. We gave Thamūd the she-camel as a clear sign.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2333), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11226), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2225), al-Ḥākim (2/362), and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (10/78–80).

12662 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Quraysh said to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Call upon your Lord to turn Mount al-Ṣafā into gold for us, and we will believe in you.”
He asked:
“Will you truly do so?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He supplicated, and Gabriel came to him and said:
“Your Lord conveys greetings of peace to you and says: If you wish, al-Ṣafā will become gold for them. Whoever among them disbelieves afterward, I will punish with a punishment unlike that inflicted upon anyone else in creation. Or, if you wish, I will open for them the door of repentance and mercy.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Rather, the door of repentance and mercy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2166, 3223), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2224), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (12736), and al-Ḥākim (1/53).
The words:
“We send the signs only as a warning”
mean that Allah sends His signs so that people may become afraid, take heed, and return to Allah, Mighty and Majestic. Among these signs are eclipses of the sun and moon, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12663 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The sun was eclipsed during the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so he led the people in prayer.
He stood for a long time, then bowed for a long time. He stood again for a long time, though shorter than the first standing, then bowed for a long time, though shorter than the first bowing. He then rose and prostrated.
He did the same in the second unit of prayer. He then finished, and the sun had become visible again.
He addressed the people, praised Allah, and said:
“The sun and moon are two signs among the signs of Allah. They are not eclipsed because of anyone’s death or life. When you see this, supplicate to Allah, proclaim His greatness, and give charity.”
He then said:
“Nation of Muḥammad, by Allah, no one has greater protective jealousy than Allah when His male servant or female servant commits adultery. Nation of Muḥammad, by Allah, if you knew what I know, you would laugh little and weep much.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Eclipse Prayer (1), al-Bukhārī (1044), and Muslim (901).

12664 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“We made the vision We showed you only as a test for the people”:
“It was a vision seen with the eyes, shown to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the night he was taken to Jerusalem.”
Concerning the words:
“And the accursed tree in the Qur’an,”
he said:
“It is the tree of Zaqqūm.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3888).

12665 — ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥimār al-Mujāshiʿī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ related that his Lord, Blessed and Exalted, said:
“I created all My servants naturally inclined toward pure monotheism. Then the devils came to them, turned them away from their religion, declared unlawful for them what I had made lawful, and commanded them to associate with Me that for which I had sent down no authority.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2865).

12666 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not turn your homes into graveyards. Satan flees from a house in which Sūrat al-Baqarah is recited.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (780).
2. Reciting Āyat al-Kursī

12667 — Abū Hurayrah narrated, in the account of his guarding the charity collected for Ramadan:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked me:
“What happened to your captive last night?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, he claimed that he would teach me words through which Allah would benefit me, so I released him.”
He asked:
“What were they?”
I replied:
“He told me, ‘When you go to your bed, recite Āyat al-Kursī from its beginning until you complete the verse:
“Allah—there is no deity worthy of worship except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:255]
“He also told me, ‘A guardian from Allah will remain over you, and no devil will approach you until morning.’”
The Companions were the most eager of people to attain goodness.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“He has spoken the truth to you, although he is a persistent liar. Do you know whom you have been speaking to for the past three nights, Abū Hurayrah?”
I replied:
“No.”
He said:
“That was a devil.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2311).
3. Consistently Observing the Prescribed Remembrances, Including:

12668 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever says one hundred times in a day, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over everything,’ will receive the reward of freeing ten slaves. One hundred good deeds will be recorded for him, one hundred sins will be erased from him, and it will serve as protection for him from Satan throughout that day until evening. No one will bring anything better than what he has brought except a person who does more than that.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Book of the Qur’an (486), al-Bukhārī (3293), and Muslim (2691).

12669 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Prayer performed in congregation is twenty-five degrees superior to the prayer of a person praying alone. The angels of the night and the angels of the day gather together at the dawn prayer.”
Abū Hurayrah would then say:
“Recite, if you wish:
‘Indeed, the recitation at dawn is witnessed.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4717) and Muslim (649:246).

12670 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Angels of the night and angels of the day take turns among you. They gather together at the dawn prayer and the afternoon prayer. Then those who spent the night among you ascend, and Allah asks them—although He knows them best—‘In what state did you leave My servants?’ They reply, ‘We left them while they were praying, and we came to them while they were praying.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Shortening of the Prayer (82), al-Bukhārī (555), and Muslim (632).

12671 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“Indeed, the recitation at dawn is witnessed”:
“It is witnessed by the angels of the night and the angels of the day.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3135), Ibn Mājah (670), and Aḥmad (10133).

12672 — Ibn ʿUmar said:
“On the Day of Resurrection, the people will be gathered in groups, every nation following its prophet. They will say, ‘So-and-so, intercede for us,’ until the intercession reaches the Prophet ﷺ. That will be the day when Allah raises him to the praised station.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4718).

12673 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah on the day of its conquest while three hundred and sixty idols surrounded the House. He began striking them with a stick in his hand and reciting:
“Truth has come and falsehood has vanished. Indeed, falsehood is ever bound to vanish.”
And:
“Say, ‘Truth has come, and falsehood can neither originate anything nor bring it back.’”
 [Sūrat Sabaʾ 34:49]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4720) and Muslim (1781).

12674 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
While I was walking with the Prophet ﷺ through a field, and he was leaning upon a palm branch, he passed by a group of Jews.
Some of them said to the others:
“Ask him about the spirit.”
Others said:
“Why do you want to ask him? He may respond with something you dislike.”
They said:
“Ask him.”
One of them approached him and asked him about the spirit.
The Prophet ﷺ remained silent and gave no reply. I knew that revelation was being sent to him.
I remained standing in my place. When the revelation had descended, he recited:
“They ask you about the spirit. Say, ‘The spirit is from the command of my Lord, and you have been given only a little knowledge.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4721) and Muslim (2794).
The word ʿasīb means a palm branch stripped of its leaves.

12675 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Quraysh said to the Jews:
“Give us something about which we may question this man.”
They replied:
“Ask him about the spirit.”
They asked him about the spirit, and Allah revealed:
“They ask you about the spirit. Say, ‘The spirit is from the command of my Lord, and you have been given only a little knowledge.’”
They said:
“We have been given abundant knowledge—the Torah. Whoever has been given the Torah has been given much good.”
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Say, ‘If the sea were ink for the words of my Lord, the sea would be exhausted before the words of my Lord were exhausted, even if We brought another sea like it as additional ink.’”
 [Sūrat al-Kahf 18:109]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3140) and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11252), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (99) and al-Ḥākim (2/531).

12676 — Anas narrated:
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, how will the disbeliever be gathered upon his face on the Day of Resurrection?”
He replied:
“Is not the One Who made him walk upon his two feet in this world able to make him walk upon his face on the Day of Resurrection?”
Qatādah said:
“Indeed, by the might of our Lord.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4760) and Muslim (2806).

12677 — Ḥudhayfah ibn Usayd narrated:
Abū Dharr stood and said:
“Banū Ghifār, speak truthfully and do not disagree, for the truthful one whose truthfulness was confirmed informed me:
‘The people will be gathered in three groups: one group riding, eating, and clothed; another group walking and running; and a third group whom the angels will drag upon their faces and gather toward the Fire.’”
Someone asked:
“We understand the other two groups, but what is the condition of those who walk and run?”
He replied:
“Allah will afflict the riding animals until none remain. A man may possess a splendid garden and offer it in exchange for an old she-camel fitted with a saddle, yet he will be unable to obtain one.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (2086), Aḥmad (21456), al-Bazzār in his Musnad (3891), and al-Ḥākim (4/564).

12678 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was prostrating and supplicating:
“O Most Compassionate, O Most Merciful.”
The polytheists said:
“He claims that he calls upon one god, yet he calls upon two.”
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
“Say, ‘Call upon Allah or call upon al-Raḥmān. Whichever name you call upon, to Him belong the most beautiful names.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (15/123–124).
Regarding the words:
“Do not raise your voice too loudly in prayer, nor make it too quiet, but seek a way between the two”:

12679 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Do not raise your voice too loudly in prayer, nor make it too quiet”:
This verse was revealed while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was concealing his worship in Makkah.
When he prayed with his Companions, he would raise his voice while reciting the Qur’an. When the polytheists heard him, they insulted the Qur’an, the One Who revealed it, and the one who brought it.
Allah, the Exalted, therefore said to His Prophet ﷺ:
“Do not raise your voice too loudly in your prayer,”
meaning: do not recite so loudly that the polytheists hear and insult the Qur’an.
And:
“Do not make it too quiet,”
so that your Companions cannot hear you.
Rather:
“Seek a way between the two.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4722) and Muslim (446).

12680 — ʿĀʾishah said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“Do not raise your voice too loudly in your prayer, nor make it too quiet”:
“This was revealed concerning supplication.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4723) and Muslim (447).

12681 — Darrāj narrated that an elderly man from the Anṣār who was among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ told him that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said concerning the words:
“Do not raise your voice too loudly in your prayer”:
“This refers to supplication. Do not raise your voice while supplicating and mention your sins so that others hear them and shame you because of them.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Tārīkh al-Kabīr (3/256).

12682 — Abū Qatādah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ went out one night and found Abū Bakr praying in a low voice. He then passed by ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, who was praying in a raised voice.
When they later gathered before the Prophet ﷺ, he said:
“Abū Bakr, I passed by you while you were praying in a low voice.”
Abū Bakr replied:
“I made the One with Whom I was privately conversing hear me, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said to ʿUmar:
“I passed by you while you were praying in a raised voice.”
ʿUmar replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, I awaken the drowsy and drive Satan away.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Abū Bakr, raise your voice somewhat.”
And he said to ʿUmar:
“Lower your voice somewhat.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1329) and al-Tirmidhī (447), authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1161), and through him Ibn Ḥibbān (733), and by al-Ḥākim (1/310).

12683 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
A man was reciting Sūrat al-Kahf while beside him was a horse tied with two long ropes. A cloud overshadowed him and continued drawing nearer and nearer, while his horse began to recoil in fear. In the morning, he went to the Prophet ﷺ and told him what had happened. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“That was tranquility descending because of the Qur’an.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5011) and Muslim (795:240).
The word shaṭan means a long rope.

12684 — Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever memorizes ten verses from the beginning of Sūrat al-Kahf will be protected from the Antichrist.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (809).

12685 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān narrated:
One morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke about the Antichrist. In the lengthy hadith, he said:
“There are matters besides the Antichrist that I fear more for you. If he emerges while I am among you, I will contend with him on your behalf. But if he emerges when I am no longer among you, every person must contend with him for himself, and Allah will be my successor in caring for every Muslim.
He is a young man with tightly curled hair and a defective eye. I liken him to ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Qaṭan.
Whoever among you encounters him should recite over him the opening verses of Sūrat al-Kahf…”
The lengthy hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Tribulations and the Signs of the Hour (2937).

12686 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The world is sweet and green, and Allah will place you as successive generations within it and observe how you act. Therefore, guard yourselves against the world and guard yourselves regarding women, for the first trial of the Children of Israel concerned women.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Heart-Softening Reports (2742).

12687 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Solomon said, ‘Tonight I will visit ninety women, and every one of them will give birth to a horseman who will fight in the path of Allah.’
His companion said to him, ‘Say: If Allah wills.’ But he did not say, ‘If Allah wills.’
He visited all of them, but only one woman became pregnant, and she gave birth to half a man.
By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, had he said, ‘If Allah wills,’ they would all have become horsemen fighting in the path of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6639) and Muslim (1654:25).

12688 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated that the Prophet ﷺ came to him and Fāṭimah at night and said:
“Will you not pray?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, our souls are only in Allah’s hand. If He wills to awaken us, He will awaken us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned away when I said that. I then heard him, as he was leaving, striking his thigh and reciting:
“The human being is more argumentative than anything else.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4724) and Muslim (775).

12689 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
I said to Ibn ʿAbbās:
“Nawf al-Bikālī claims that the Moses who accompanied al-Khiḍr was not Moses of the Children of Israel.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“The enemy of Allah has lied. Ubayy ibn Kaʿb informed me that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Moses stood to address the Children of Israel and was asked, ‘Who is the most knowledgeable person?’ He replied, ‘I am.’ Allah reproached him because he did not attribute ultimate knowledge to Him.
Allah revealed to him, ‘I have a servant at the junction of the two seas who possesses knowledge that you do not.’
Moses asked, ‘My Lord, how may I find him?’
He said, ‘Take a fish with you and place it in a basket. Wherever you lose the fish, there he will be.’
Moses took a fish and placed it in a basket. He then set out with his young servant Joshua son of Nun until they reached a rock, where they rested their heads and fell asleep.
The fish stirred within the basket, came out, and fell into the sea:
‘It took its way into the sea like a tunnel.’
Allah held back the flow of water around the fish, leaving a tunnel-like passage over it.
When they awoke, Moses’ companion forgot to tell him about the fish. They continued traveling for the remainder of that day and night.
The following day, Moses said to his servant:
‘Bring us our meal. We have certainly suffered weariness from this journey.’
Moses did not experience fatigue until they had passed the place Allah had appointed.
His servant said:
‘Do you remember when we rested by the rock? I forgot the fish, and none but Satan caused me to forget to mention it. It took its way into the sea in a remarkable manner.’
The fish’s path became a tunnel, while it was a source of amazement to Moses and his servant.
Moses said:
‘That is what we were seeking.’
So they retraced their footsteps.
They followed their tracks back until they reached the rock, where they found a man covered with a garment.
Moses greeted him with peace. Al-Khiḍr replied, ‘How can there be such a greeting in your land?’
Moses said, ‘I am Moses.’
He asked, ‘Moses of the Children of Israel?’
He replied, ‘Yes. I have come so that you may teach me some of the right guidance you have been taught.’
Al-Khiḍr said:
‘You will never be able to remain patient with me.’
‘Moses, I possess some knowledge Allah has taught me that you do not know, while you possess some knowledge Allah has taught you that I do not know.’
Moses said:
‘You will find me patient, if Allah wills, and I will not disobey you in any matter.’
Al-Khiḍr said:
‘If you follow me, do not question me about anything until I myself explain it to you.’
They set out walking along the seashore. A ship passed, and they asked its people to carry them. The sailors recognized al-Khiḍr and took them aboard without payment.
Once they had boarded the ship, al-Khiḍr suddenly removed one of its planks with an axe.
Moses said, ‘These people carried us without payment, yet you deliberately damaged their ship:
“Have you damaged it to drown its people? You have certainly done a terrible thing.”’
Al-Khiḍr replied:
‘Did I not say that you would never be able to remain patient with me?’
Moses said:
‘Do not blame me for what I forgot, and do not impose hardship upon me in this matter.’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The first objection from Moses was due to forgetfulness.”
A sparrow then came and perched upon the edge of the ship. It dipped its beak once into the sea.
Al-Khiḍr said to Moses, ‘My knowledge and your knowledge compared with Allah’s knowledge are no more than what this sparrow has taken from the sea.’
They then left the ship. While walking along the shore, al-Khiḍr saw a boy playing with other children. Al-Khiḍr seized the boy’s head and pulled it off with his hand, killing him.
Moses said:
‘Have you killed an innocent soul who had killed no one? You have certainly done a dreadful thing.’
Al-Khiḍr replied:
‘Did I not tell you that you would never be able to remain patient with me?’
The narrator said:
“This was more severe than the first incident.”
Moses said:
‘If I question you about anything after this, then do not keep me as your companion. You have received a sufficient excuse from me.’
They continued until they reached the people of a town. They asked its inhabitants for food, but the people refused to host them. There they found a wall about to collapse.
The narrator explained:
“It was leaning.”
Al-Khiḍr then stood and straightened it with his hand.
Moses said, ‘We came to people who neither fed us nor hosted us:
“Had you wished, you could have taken payment for it.”’
Al-Khiḍr said:
‘This is the parting between you and me,’
and continued until the words:
‘That is the explanation of what you were unable to bear patiently.’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“We wish that Moses had remained patient so that Allah might have related more of their story to us.”
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr said:
Ibn ʿAbbās used to recite:
“Ahead of them was a king who seized every sound ship by force.”
He also used to recite:
“As for the boy, he was a disbeliever, while his parents were believers.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4725), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (2380).
The young servant mentioned in the words:
“When Moses said to his young servant”
was Joshua son of Nun. He served Moses, peace be upon him. Moses selected him as his deputy and instructed him to lead the Children of Israel after his death.
The word:
“For ages”
means a long period of time. Its plural is aḥqāb.
Regarding the words:
“As for the boy, his parents were believers”:

12690 — Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The boy whom al-Khiḍr killed had been destined to become a disbeliever. Had he lived, he would have burdened his parents with rebellion and disbelief.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2661).
The word:
“Believers”
means that they followed the religion of Abraham, peace be upon him.
The words:
“He had been destined to become a disbeliever”
mean that he would have become a disbeliever upon reaching maturity.

12691 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān narrated:
One morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke about the Antichrist and related a lengthy hadith, in which he said:
“While Jesus is in that condition, Allah will reveal to him: ‘I have brought forth servants of Mine whom no one has the power to fight. Therefore, take My servants to safety upon Mount Ṭūr.’
Allah will send forth Gog and Magog, and they will rush down from every elevation.
The first of them will pass by the Lake of Tiberias and drink all its water. The last of them will pass by and say, ‘There was once water here.’
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will be besieged until the head of an ox will be more valuable to one of them than one hundred dinars are to one of you today.
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will supplicate to Allah. Allah will then send worms into the necks of Gog and Magog, and by morning they will all lie dead as though they were a single soul.
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will then descend to the earth, but they will not find a handspan of land that is not filled with their fat and foul stench.
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will again supplicate to Allah. Allah will send birds whose necks resemble those of Bactrian camels. They will carry the bodies away and cast them wherever Allah wills.
Allah will then send rain from which no house of clay or tent of hair will provide shelter. It will wash the earth until it leaves it like a polished mirror.
The earth will then be commanded: ‘Produce your fruit and restore your blessing.’
On that day, a whole group of people will eat from a single pomegranate…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2937).

12692 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Gog and Magog dig every day. When they are about to see the rays of the sun, their leader says, ‘Return, and you will dig through it tomorrow.’ When they return, the barrier is stronger than it was before.
When their appointed time arrives and Allah intends to send them forth against the people, they will dig until they are about to see the rays of the sun. Their leader will then say, ‘Return, and you will dig through it tomorrow, if Allah, the Exalted, wills,’ thus adding the qualification ‘if Allah wills.’
When they return, they will find it exactly as they left it. They will dig through it and emerge against the people. They will drain the waters, while the people take refuge from them within their fortresses.
They will shoot their arrows toward the sky, and the arrows will return appearing stained with blood. They will say, ‘We have conquered the inhabitants of the earth and overcome the inhabitants of heaven.’
Allah will then send worms into the backs of their necks and kill them by means of those worms.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, the animals of the earth will become fat and well nourished from their flesh and blood.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3153), Ibn Mājah (4080), and Aḥmad (10632), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6829) and al-Ḥākim (4/488).
Many other hadiths concerning Gog and Magog are mentioned in the section on the Signs of the Hour.

12693 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
A Bedouin came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“What is the Trumpet?”
He replied:
“It is a horn into which one will blow.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4742), al-Tirmidhī (2430, 3244), and Aḥmad (6507), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7312) and al-Ḥākim (2/436).

12694 — Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How can I feel at ease when the bearer of the Trumpet has placed the horn to his mouth, lowered his forehead, and is waiting to be commanded to blow?”
It was said:
“We asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, what should we say then?’”
He replied:
“Say: ‘Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs. In Allah we place our trust.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1084).

12695 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Hell will be brought forth on that Day with seventy thousand reins, and with every rein will be seventy thousand angels dragging it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2842).

12696 — Muṣʿab ibn Saʿd narrated:
I asked my father concerning the verse:
“Say, ‘Shall We inform you of those who are the greatest losers in their deeds?’”
“Are they the Ḥarūriyyah?”
He replied:
“No. They are the Jews and Christians.”
“The Jews rejected Muḥammad ﷺ, while the Christians disbelieved in the true nature of Paradise and said that there is neither food nor drink within it.”
“The Ḥarūriyyah are those who break Allah’s covenant after it has been firmly established.”
Saʿd used to call them the rebellious sinners.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4728).
The word al-Ḥarūriyyah, with a fatḥah on the letter ḥāʾ and a ḍammah on the letter rāʾ, is attributed to Ḥarūrāʾ, the village from which the Khawārij first rebelled against ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

12697 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A huge, fat man will come on the Day of Resurrection, yet in Allah’s sight he will not weigh as much as the wing of a mosquito.”
He then said:
“Recite:
‘We will assign no weight to them on the Day of Resurrection.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4729) and Muslim (2785).

12698 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever believes in Allah and His Messenger, establishes prayer, and fasts Ramadan has a right promised by Allah that He will admit him into Paradise, whether he emigrates in Allah’s path or remains in the land where he was born.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should we not give the people this good news?”
He replied:
“Within Paradise are one hundred levels that Allah has prepared for those who strive in His path. The distance between every two levels is like the distance between heaven and earth.
When you ask Allah, ask Him for al-Firdaws. It is the central and highest part of Paradise. Above it is the Throne of the Most Compassionate, and from it the rivers of Paradise spring forth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7423).

12699 — Anas narrated:
Ḥārithah was killed on the day of Badr while still a young boy. His mother came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you know how dear Ḥārithah was to me. If he is in Paradise, I will remain patient and seek Allah’s reward. But if the matter is otherwise, you will see what I will do from grief.”
He replied:
“Woe to you—or may you be bereaved! Do you think there is only one garden? There are many gardens, and he is in the Garden of al-Firdaws.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3982).
The words:
“Ḥārithah was killed on the day of Badr”
refer to Ḥārithah ibn Surāqah ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAdī al-Anṣārī. His father was Surāqah ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAdī ibn Mālik ibn ʿAdī ibn ʿĀmir of Banū al-Najjār. His mother was al-Rubayyiʿ bint al-Naḍr, the paternal aunt of Anas ibn Mālik.

12700 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Al-Rubayyiʿ bint al-Naḍr came to the Prophet ﷺ. Her son, Ḥārithah ibn Surāqah, had been killed at Badr after being struck by a stray arrow. She came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“Tell me about Ḥārithah. If he has attained good, I will seek Allah’s reward and remain patient. But if he has not attained good, I will exert myself in supplication.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Mother of Ḥārithah, there are many gardens within Paradise, and your son has attained the highest al-Firdaws. Al-Firdaws is the elevated part of Paradise, its center, and its finest region.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3174), whose wording is given here, al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (24/262–263), Ibn Ḥibbān (958), Ibn Khuzaymah in Kitāb al-Tawḥīd (781), and al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (15/436).

12701 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Quraysh said to the Jews:
“Give us something about which we may question this man.”
They replied:
“Ask him about the spirit.”
They asked him about the spirit, and Allah revealed:
“They ask you about the spirit. Say, ‘The spirit is from the command of my Lord, and you have been given only a little knowledge.’”
 [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:85]
They said:
“We have been given abundant knowledge—the Torah. Whoever has been given the Torah has been given much good.”
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Say, ‘If the sea were ink for the words of my Lord, the sea would be exhausted before the words of my Lord were exhausted, even if We brought another sea like it as additional ink.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3140), Aḥmad (2309), and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11252), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (99) and al-Ḥākim (2/531).
It appears from this report that this verse and the verse in Sūrat al-Isrāʾ were revealed on two separate occasions, but the Companion combined them within a single narration.
Regarding the words:
“Ink for the words of my Lord,”
Allah’s speech is one of His attributes. It is uncreated and has neither beginning nor end, like His other attributes, including knowledge, power, mercy, and wisdom.
It cannot be confined within any limit. Whatever vastness and majesty hearts may imagine, Allah is greater than that. The same applies to all the attributes of Allah, the Exalted.

12702 — Umm Salamah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard people disputing outside the door of his room. He came out to them and said:
“I am only a human being. Disputing parties come before me, and one of you may be more eloquent in presenting his case than the other. I may therefore think that he is truthful and judge in his favor accordingly.
Whoever receives through my judgment something that rightfully belongs to another Muslim has merely received a piece of the Fire. Let him either take it or leave it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2458) and Muslim (1713).

12703 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I am only a human being like you. I forget just as you forget, so when I forget, remind me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (401) and Muslim (572).
For further discussion, see the Book of Faith.

12704 — Umm Salamah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated concerning the emigration to Abyssinia:
The Negus asked:
“Do you possess anything that he brought from Allah?”
Jaʿfar replied:
“Yes.”
The Negus said:
“Then recite it to me.”
Jaʿfar recited to him the opening portion beginning with “Kāf Hā Yā ʿAyn Ṣād.”
She said:
“By Allah, the Negus wept until his beard became wet, and his bishops wept until their scriptures became wet when they heard what Jaʿfar recited to them.”
The Negus then said:
“Indeed, this and what Moses brought emerge from the same source of light. Depart, for by Allah, I will never surrender them to you.”
The hadith continues.
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (1740, 22498) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (2/301–306).
This statement of the Negus indicates that he may have seen the Gospel of Barnabas, which was later prohibited from being read. It is not unlikely that the Negus had access to it, since he was one of their kings. He therefore recognized that this Prophet was the one whose coming Jesus, peace be upon him, had foretold.
For a detailed discussion, see my book: Judaism, Christianity, and the Religions of India.

12705 — ʿĀʾishah, the Mother of the Believers, narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, his wives intended to send ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān to Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq to ask him for their inheritance from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
ʿĀʾishah said to them:
“Did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ not say:
‘We prophets are not inherited from. Whatever we leave behind is charity’?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Speech, Backbiting, and Piety (27), al-Bukhārī (6730), and Muslim (1758).

12706 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My heirs will not divide any dinars. Whatever I leave after the maintenance of my wives and the expenses of my administrator is charity.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Speech, Backbiting, and Piety (28), al-Bukhārī (6729), and Muslim (1760).

12707 — Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated:
When I arrived in Najrān, they questioned me and said:
“You recite:
‘Sister of Aaron,’
yet Moses lived such-and-such a long time before Jesus.”
When I returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I asked him about that. He replied:
“They used to name their children after the prophets and righteous people who came before them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2135).

12708 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever testifies that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner; that Muḥammad is His servant and Messenger; that Jesus is Allah’s servant and Messenger, His word which He conveyed to Mary, and a spirit from Him; and that Paradise is true and the Fire is true—Allah will admit him into Paradise according to whatever deeds he performed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3435). Muslim also narrated it (28) with the wording:
“Allah will admit him through whichever of the eight gates of Paradise he wishes.”

12709 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No one is more patient in bearing offensive words that he hears than Allah. They attribute a child to Him, yet He continues to grant them well-being and provide for them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7378) and Muslim (2804). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12710 — Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Death will be brought on the Day of Resurrection in the form of a white ram with black markings. It will be made to stand between Paradise and the Fire. It will be said, ‘People of Paradise, do you recognize this?’
They will stretch their necks, look, and say, ‘Yes, this is death.’
It will then be said, ‘People of the Fire, do you recognize this?’
They will stretch their necks, look, and say, ‘Yes, this is death.’
It will then be commanded that death be slaughtered.
Then it will be proclaimed, ‘People of Paradise, everlasting life with no death! People of the Fire, everlasting life with no death!’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then recited:
“Warn them of the Day of Regret, when the matter will have been decided, while they remain heedless and do not believe.”
He gestured with his hand toward the world.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4730) and Muslim (2849).

12711 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the people of Paradise have entered Paradise and the people of the Fire have entered the Fire, death will be brought and placed between Paradise and the Fire, and then it will be slaughtered.
A caller will then proclaim, ‘People of Paradise, there will be no death! People of the Fire, there will be no death!’
The people of Paradise will thereby increase in joy upon their existing joy, while the people of the Fire will increase in sorrow upon their existing sorrow.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6548) and Muslim (2850:43).

12712 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No one will enter Paradise without first being shown the place he would have occupied in the Fire had he done evil, so that he may increase in gratitude. No one will enter the Fire without first being shown the place he would have occupied in Paradise had he done good, so that it may increase his regret.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6569).

12713 — Jābir narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“What stands between a person and polytheism and disbelief is abandoning the prayer.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (82).

12714 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Gabriel:
“What prevents you from visiting us more frequently than you do?”
Then the following verse was revealed:
“We do not descend except by the command of your Lord. To Him belongs what is before us and what is behind us.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4731).

12715 — Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whatever Allah has declared lawful in His Book is lawful, and whatever He has declared unlawful is unlawful. Whatever He remained silent about is a concession and well-being, so accept Allah’s concession, for Allah was never forgetful.”
He then recited:
“Your Lord was never forgetful.”
Good: Narrated by al-Dāraquṭnī (2066), al-Ḥākim (2/375), through him al-Bayhaqī (10/12), and al-Bazzār (4087).

12716 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah said: ‘The son of Adam has denied Me, although he had no right to do so, and he has insulted Me, although he had no right to do so.
As for his denial of Me, it is his statement: “He will not bring me back as He created me the first time.” Yet the first creation is no easier for Me than recreating him.
As for his insult against Me, it is his statement: “Allah has taken a child.”
But I am the One, the Self-Sufficient. I neither beget nor was I begotten, and there is none comparable to Me.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4974).

12717 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
Umm Mubashshir informed me that she heard the Prophet ﷺ say in the presence of Ḥafṣah:
“None of the Companions of the Tree who pledged allegiance beneath it will enter the Fire, Allah willing.”
She said:
“Indeed they will, O Messenger of Allah.”
He rebuked her.
Ḥafṣah recited:
“There is none of you except that he will reach it.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, also said:
‘Then We will save those who were mindful of Allah and leave the wrongdoers therein on their knees.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2496).
Umm Mubashshir was the wife of Zayd ibn Ḥārithah.

12718 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The believers will be saved from the Fire and detained upon a bridge between Paradise and the Fire. Retribution will be taken among them for wrongs they committed against one another in the worldly life.
When they have been purified and cleansed, they will be granted permission to enter Paradise.
By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, each of them will know the way to his home in Paradise better than he knew the way to his home in the worldly life.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6535).

12719 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said concerning the words:
“There is none of you except that he will reach it”:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“All people will reach the Fire, and then they will depart from it according to their deeds.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3159), Aḥmad (4141), whose wording is given here, and al-Ḥākim (2/375).
The Second Meaning: Crossing the Bridge over Hell
The second interpretation is that “reaching it” means crossing the Bridge stretched over Hell. Everyone who enters Paradise must pass over it.
This interpretation was adopted by some scholars, including al-Nawawī. Shaykh al-Islām Ibn Taymiyyah also explained it in this manner, as recorded in Majmūʿ al-Fatāwā (4/279).
He cited as evidence a hadith narrated by Muslim (191:316) from Jābir, indicating that it means passing over the Bridge. He then said:
“The Ṣirāṭ is the bridge, and everyone who enters Paradise must pass over it, whether he was a child in the worldly life or not.”
End of quotation.
The complete hadith of Muslim is cited in its appropriate place.
The View That the Address Refers Only to the Disbelievers
Some scholars held that those intended by entering here are the disbelievers alone, not the believers, based on the context of the passage.
The disbelievers and devils were mentioned immediately beforehand in the words of Allah:
“By your Lord, We will surely gather them together with the devils, then We will bring them around Hell upon their knees. Then We will draw out from every faction those most rebellious against the Most Compassionate. We certainly know best who is most deserving of burning therein. There is none of you except that he will reach it. This is a decree your Lord has made binding. Then We will save those who were mindful of Allah and leave the wrongdoers therein upon their knees.”
 [Sūrat Maryam 19:68–72]
This interpretation was narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās. Ibn Jarīr related in his Tafsīr (15/596), through a man who heard Ibn ʿAbbās recite:
“There is none of them except that he will reach it,”
meaning the disbelievers.
He said:
“No believer will reach it.”
End of report.
I say: First, its chain of transmission is weak because it contains an unidentified man whose name is not given.
Second, the recitation attributed to Ibn ʿAbbās:
“There is none of them,”
instead of:
“There is none of you,”
is an irregular recitation.
Third, it is not supported by the hadiths and reports narrated concerning this subject.
Fourth, Allah says:
“Then We will save those who were mindful of Allah.”
It is well known that the word “then” indicates sequence. Would those who are saved—the God-fearing—be among the disbelievers mentioned before them? This is an objectionable conclusion that no one accepts.
Allah knows best what is correct.

12720 — Khabbāb narrated:
I was a blacksmith, and al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil owed me a debt. I went to him to demand payment.
He said:
“I will not pay you until you disbelieve in Muḥammad.”
I replied:
“I will never disbelieve in him, even after you die and are resurrected.”
He said:
“Will I truly be resurrected after death? Then I will pay you when I return to wealth and children.”
Then the following verses were revealed:
“Have you seen the one who disbelieved in Our signs and said, ‘I will surely be given wealth and children’? Has he looked into the unseen, or has he taken a covenant from the Most Compassionate? No indeed! We will record what he says and increase his punishment extensively. We will inherit from him what he speaks of, and he will come to Us alone.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4735) and Muslim (2795).

12721 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah loves a servant, He calls Gabriel and says, ‘I love so-and-so, so love him.’
Gabriel then loves him and announces throughout heaven, ‘Allah loves so-and-so, so love him.’
The inhabitants of heaven then love him, and acceptance is placed for him upon the earth.
When Allah hates a servant, He calls Gabriel and says, ‘I hate so-and-so, so hate him.’
Gabriel then hates him and announces among the inhabitants of heaven, ‘Allah hates so-and-so, so hate him.’
They then hate him, and hatred is placed for him upon the earth.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7485) and Muslim (2637).

12722 — Abū Umāmah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The supreme name of Allah, by which He answers when He is called upon, is found in three sūrahs of the Qur’an: al-Baqarah, Āl ʿImrān, and Ṭā Hā.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ Mushkil al-Āthār (176), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/282), and al-Ḥākim (1/505–506).
The wording is that of al-Ṭabarānī. Al-Ḥākim added:
Al-Qāsim said:
“I searched for it and found in Sūrat al-Baqarah, in Āyat al-Kursī:
‘Allah—there is no deity worthy of worship except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence.’
In Sūrat Āl ʿImrān:
‘Alif, Lām, Mīm. Allah—there is no deity worthy of worship except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence.’
And in Sūrat Ṭā Hā:
‘All faces will humble themselves before the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all existence, and whoever bears wrongdoing will have failed.’”

12723 — Anas narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever forgets a prayer must perform it when he remembers it. There is no expiation for it except that.”
He then recited:
“Establish the prayer for My remembrance.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (597) and Muslim (684).

12724 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
One day, the Prophet ﷺ was sitting openly before the people when Gabriel came to him and asked:
“What is faith?”
He replied:
“Faith is that you believe in Allah, His angels, meeting Him, His messengers, and resurrection.”
He asked:
“What is Islam?”
He replied:
“Islam is that you worship Allah without associating anything with Him, establish prayer, pay the obligatory zakāh, and fast Ramadan.”
He asked:
“What is excellence?”
He replied:
“It is to worship Allah as though you see Him, and although you do not see Him, He certainly sees you.”
He asked:
“When is the Hour?”
He replied:
“The one being asked knows no more about it than the questioner. I will, however, tell you about its signs: when a slave woman gives birth to her master, and when the shepherds of black camels compete in constructing tall buildings. It is among five matters known only to Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ then recited:
“Indeed, Allah alone has knowledge of the Hour. He sends down the rain and knows what is within the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in which land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.”
 [Sūrat Luqmān 31:34]
Gabriel then departed, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bring him back.”
They could not see anyone, so he said:
“That was Gabriel. He came to teach the people their religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (50) and Muslim (9).

12725 — Sālim ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar said:
“People of Iraq, how readily you ask about minor matters while committing major ones!”
I heard my father, ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar, say:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Tribulation will come from here,”
and he gestured with his hand toward the east:
“From where the two horns of Satan rise.”
Yet you strike one another’s necks, while the man Moses killed from Pharaoh’s people was killed accidentally. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said to him:
“You killed a man, but We saved you from distress and tested you with many trials.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2905:50).
The words:
“We tested you with many trials”
mean that Moses, peace be upon him, encountered one ordeal after another, from each of which Allah delivered him.
