His mother conceived him during the year in which Pharaoh was slaughtering male infants. He was placed in a chest and cast into the river. He later feared for his life after killing the Copt, in addition to the other ordeals he faced. Through Allah’s support and guidance, Moses, peace be upon him, emerged safely from them all.

12726 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Adam and Moses met. Moses said to Adam, ‘Are you the one who brought hardship upon humanity and caused them to be expelled from Paradise?’
Adam replied, ‘Are you the one whom Allah selected with His message, chose for Himself, and to whom He revealed the Torah?’
Moses said, ‘Yes.’
Adam asked, ‘Did you find written therein that this had been decreed for me before He created me?’
He replied, ‘Yes.’
Thus, Adam overcame Moses in the argument.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4736) and Muslim (2652:15). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12727 — Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb narrated in the lengthy account of Heraclius:
Heraclius called for the letter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that Diḥyah had delivered to the governor of Buṣrā, who then forwarded it to Heraclius. He read it, and it contained:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful. From Muḥammad, the servant and Messenger of Allah, to Heraclius, ruler of the Romans. Peace be upon whoever follows guidance.
To proceed: I invite you with the call of Islam. Accept Islam and you will be safe, and Allah will grant you your reward twice…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7) and Muslim (1773:74).

12728 — Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“As for the people of the Fire who are its permanent inhabitants, they will neither die therein nor truly live.
There will, however, be people whom the Fire afflicts because of their sins—or he said, because of their misdeeds—and they will be caused to die until they become charcoal. Then permission for intercession will be granted.
They will be brought out in groups and spread along the rivers of Paradise.
It will then be said, ‘People of Paradise, pour water over them.’
They will grow like a seed growing in the silt carried by a flood.”
A man among the people remarked:
“It is as though the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had lived in the desert.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (185).

12729 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The people of Paradise will see those in the lofty chambers above them just as they see a brilliant star remaining on the distant horizon in the east or west, because of the difference in rank between them.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are those the dwellings of the prophets, which no one else can reach?”
He replied:
“No! By the One in Whose hand is my soul, they are men who believed in Allah and affirmed the truthfulness of the messengers.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3256) and Muslim (2831).

12730 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Paradise contains one hundred levels. The distance between every two levels is like the distance between heaven and earth, and al-Firdaws is the highest of them.
From it spring the four rivers of Paradise, and above it is the Throne.
When you ask Allah, ask Him for al-Firdaws.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2531).
Imām Aḥmad also narrated it (22695), with the wording:
“Between every two levels is a journey of one hundred years.”

12731 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Madinah, the Jews were fasting on ʿĀshūrāʾ. He asked them about it, and they replied:
“This is the day on which Moses prevailed over Pharaoh.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“We have a greater right to Moses than they do, so fast this day.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4837) and Muslim (1130). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12732 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The believers will gather on the Day of Resurrection and say, ‘Let us seek someone to intercede for us before our Lord.’
They will go to Adam and say, ‘You are the father of humanity. Allah created you with His hand, commanded His angels to prostrate to you, and taught you the names of all things. Intercede for us before your Lord so that He may relieve us from this place.’
He will say, ‘I am not fit for that,’ and will mention his sin and feel ashamed.
He will say, ‘Go to Noah, for he was the first messenger Allah sent to the people of the earth.’
They will go to him, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that.’ He will mention that he asked his Lord about something of which he had no knowledge and will feel ashamed.
He will say, ‘Go to the intimate friend of the Most Compassionate.’
They will go to Abraham, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that. Go to Moses, a servant to whom Allah spoke and whom He gave the Torah.’
They will go to Moses, but he will say, ‘I am not fit for that.’ He will mention killing a person who had not killed anyone and will feel ashamed before his Lord.
He will say, ‘Go to Jesus, Allah’s servant and messenger, His word and His spirit.’
Jesus will say, ‘I am not fit for that. Go to Muḥammad ﷺ, a servant whose former and latter sins Allah has forgiven.’
They will come to me, and I will proceed until I seek permission to enter before my Lord, and permission will be granted.
When I see my Lord, I will fall in prostration, and He will leave me for as long as He wills.
Then it will be said, ‘Raise your head. Ask and you will be given. Speak and you will be heard. Intercede and your intercession will be accepted.’
I will raise my head and praise Him with words of praise that He will teach me. Then I will intercede.
A specific number will be assigned to me, and I will admit them into Paradise. Then I will return to Him, and when I see my Lord, I will do the same. I will intercede again, and a specific number will be assigned to me, and I will admit them into Paradise.
Then I will return a fourth time and say, ‘None remains in the Fire except those whom the Qur’an has confined there and for whom everlasting punishment has become obligatory.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4476) and Muslim (193).

12733 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Wrongdoing will become layers of darkness on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2447) and Muslim (2579). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12734 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Beware of wrongdoing, for wrongdoing will become layers of darkness on the Day of Resurrection.
Beware of avarice, for avarice destroyed those before you. It drove them to shed one another’s blood and treat sacred prohibitions as lawful.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2578).

12735 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Do you know who the bankrupt person is?”
They replied:
“The bankrupt person among us is one who possesses neither money nor goods.”
He said:
“The truly bankrupt person from my nation will come on the Day of Resurrection with prayer, fasting, and zakāh.
But he will also have insulted one person, falsely accused another, consumed another’s wealth, shed another’s blood, and struck another.
This person will be given some of his good deeds, and that person will be given some of his good deeds.
If his good deeds are exhausted before all his liabilities are settled, some of their sins will be taken and cast upon him, and then he will be cast into the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2581).

12736 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Adam and Moses, peace be upon them, disputed before their Lord, and Adam overcame Moses in the argument.
Moses said, ‘You are Adam, whom Allah created with His hand, into whom He breathed of His spirit, before whom He commanded His angels to prostrate, and whom He settled in His Paradise. Then through your sin you caused humanity to descend to the earth.’
Adam replied, ‘You are Moses, whom Allah selected with His message and speech, to whom He gave the tablets containing an explanation of all things, and whom He brought near for private conversation.’
‘How long before my creation did you find that Allah had written the Torah?’
Moses replied, ‘Forty years.’
Adam asked, ‘Did you find written therein:
“Adam disobeyed his Lord and went astray”?’
He replied, ‘Yes.’
Adam said, ‘Do you blame me for performing an act that Allah had written for me to perform forty years before He created me?’
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Thus, Adam overcame Moses in the argument.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2652:15).

12737 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The believer will be in a green garden within his grave. His grave will be expanded for him to seventy cubits, and it will be illuminated for him like the full moon.
Do you know concerning what this verse was revealed:
‘He will have a constricted life, and We will gather him blind on the Day of Resurrection’?”
“Do you know what the constricted life is?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It is the punishment of the disbeliever in his grave.
By the One in Whose hand is my soul, ninety-nine dragons will be set upon him.
Do you know what the dragons are? They are seventy serpents, each serpent having seven heads. They will bite and tear at him until the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (6644), Ibn Ḥibbān (3122), whose wording is given here, and al-Bayhaqī in ʿAdhāb al-Qabr (80).

12738 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said concerning the words:
“A constricted life”:
“It is the punishment of the grave.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/381), and through him by al-Bayhaqī in Ithbāt ʿAdhāb al-Qabr (71).

12739 — Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
We were sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he looked at the full moon and said:
“You will certainly see your Lord just as you see this moon, without crowding or difficulty in seeing Him.
Therefore, if you are able not to be overcome and miss a prayer before sunrise and a prayer before sunset, then do so.”
He meant the afternoon and dawn prayers.
Jarīr then recited:
“Glorify your Lord with praise before sunrise and before sunset.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (554) and Muslim (633).

12740 — ʿUmārah ibn Ruʾaybah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No one who performs the prayer before sunrise and the prayer before sunset will enter the Fire.”
He meant the dawn and afternoon prayers.
A man from Baṣrah asked him:
“Did you personally hear this from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man said:
“I also testify that I heard it from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My ears heard it, and my heart retained it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (634).

12741 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that he entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the upper room where he had secluded himself from his wives after swearing to remain apart from them.
He was lying upon a woven mat with nothing between his body and the mat. Beneath his head was a leather cushion stuffed with palm fiber. A pile of acacia leaves lay near his feet, and untanned hides were hanging near his head.
I saw the marks of the mat upon his side and began to weep.
He asked:
“What makes you weep?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, Chosroes and Caesar enjoy what they enjoy, while you are the Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Are you not satisfied that they should have this world while we have the Hereafter?”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4913) and Muslim (1479:31).

12742 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“What I fear most for you is what Allah will bring forth for you of the splendor of this world.”
They asked:
“What is the splendor of this world, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The blessings and produce of the earth.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, can good bring evil?”
He replied:
“Good brings only good. Good brings only good. Good brings only good.
Everything produced by spring may kill through overeating or nearly kill, except the animal that grazes moderately upon green plants.
It eats until its sides become full, then faces the sun, regurgitates and chews its cud, urinates, and passes dung. It then returns and eats again.
This wealth is green and sweet.
Whoever takes it rightfully and places it where it rightfully belongs will find it an excellent aid.
Whoever takes it without right is like one who eats but is never satisfied.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6427) and Muslim (1052:122).

12743 — Abān ibn ʿUthmān narrated:
Zayd ibn Thābit came out from the presence of Marwān at midday. I thought:
“He would not have summoned him at this hour except to ask him about something.”
I asked him, and he replied:
“He questioned us about matters we had heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever makes this world his primary concern, Allah will scatter his affairs, place poverty before his eyes, and nothing of the world will come to him except what has been decreed for him.
Whoever makes the Hereafter his intention, Allah will gather his affairs, place richness within his heart, and the world will come to him humbled.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4105) and Aḥmad (21590).

12744 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“May Allah have mercy upon a man who rises during the night and prays, then awakens his wife and she prays. If she refuses, he sprinkles water upon her face.
May Allah have mercy upon a woman who rises during the night and prays, then awakens her husband and he prays. If he refuses, she sprinkles water upon his face.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1308), al-Nasāʾī (1610), and Ibn Mājah (1336).

12745 — Abū Saʿīd and Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When a man awakens his wife during the night and they both pray—or they perform two units of prayer together—they will be recorded among the men and women who remember Allah frequently.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1309) and Ibn Mājah (1335).

12746 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“One night I saw in a dream that we were in the house of ʿUqbah ibn Rāfiʿ, and fresh dates from the dates of Ibn Ṭāb were brought to us.
I interpreted this as elevation for us in this world, a good end in the Hereafter, and that our religion had become wholesome and firmly established.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2270).

12747 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every prophet was given signs through which people believed in him. What I have been given is revelation that Allah revealed to me, so I hope to have the greatest number of followers on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Virtues of the Qur’an (4981) and Muslim in The Book of Faith (152), both through al-Layth, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Saʿīd, from his father, from Abū Hurayrah. The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

12748 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“Sūrat Banī Isrāʾīl, al-Kahf, Maryam, Ṭā Hā, and al-Anbiyāʾ are among the earliest and finest sūrahs, and they are among my ancient treasures.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4739).
The expression:
“Among the earliest and finest”
uses the plural of ʿatīq, meaning something that has reached the highest degree of quality.
The word al-tilād refers to something ancient and long possessed. Their being described as early refers to their order of revelation, since they are Makkan sūrahs, and they were among the first portions of the Qur’an Ibn Masʿūd memorized.

12749 — Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām narrated:
While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was among his Companions, he asked them:
“Do you hear what I hear?”
They replied:
“We do not hear anything.”
He said:
“I can hear the creaking of heaven, and it is not to be blamed for creaking. There is not a handspan of space within it except that an angel is standing or prostrating upon it.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Naṣr al-Marwazī in Taʿẓīm Qadr al-Ṣalāh (1/258), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (3/224–225), and Abū al-Shaykh in al-ʿAẓamah (3/986).

12750 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, whenever I see you, my soul becomes content and my eyes are comforted. Tell me about the origin of all things.”
He replied:
“Everything was created from water.”
I said:
“Tell me of an action which, if I adhere to it, will admit me into Paradise.”
He said:
“Spread greetings of peace, feed others, maintain family ties, and stand in prayer during the night while people are asleep. Then enter Paradise in peace.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (7932), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (508) and al-Ḥākim (4/160).

12751 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell me from what creation was made.”
He replied:
“From water.”
Good: Narrated by Isḥāq ibn Rāhawayh (301).

12752 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There are two phrases beloved to the Most Compassionate, light upon the tongue, and heavy upon the scale: ‘Glory and praise belong to Allah,’ and ‘Glory belongs to Allah, the Magnificent.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7563) and Muslim (2694). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12753 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah will single out a man from my nation before all creation on the Day of Resurrection. Ninety-nine records will be unfolded before him, each record extending as far as the eye can see.
Allah will ask, ‘Do you deny anything contained in these records? Have My recording angels wronged you?’
He will reply, ‘No, my Lord.’
Allah will ask, ‘Do you possess any excuse?’
He will reply, ‘No, my Lord.’
Allah will say, ‘Indeed, you possess one good deed with Us, and no injustice will befall you today.’
A card will then be produced upon which is written:
‘I testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, and I testify that Muḥammad is His servant and Messenger.’
Allah will say, ‘Attend the weighing of your deeds.’
The man will say, ‘My Lord, what weight can this card have compared with all these records?’
He will be told, ‘You will not be wronged.’
The records will be placed upon one side of the scale and the card upon the other. The records will become light, while the card will outweigh them, for nothing can outweigh the name of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2639), whose wording is given here, Ibn Mājah (4300), Aḥmad (6994), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (225) and al-Ḥākim (1/6).

12754 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Prophet Abraham, peace be upon him, never made a statement contrary to the apparent reality except on three occasions. Two of them were for the sake of Allah: his statement,
‘Indeed, I am ill,’
 [Sūrat al-Ṣāffāt 37:89]
and his statement,
‘Rather, this largest one of them did it.’
The third concerned Sarah.
He entered the land of a tyrannical ruler while accompanied by Sarah, who was among the most beautiful of people.
He said to her, ‘If this tyrant learns that you are my wife, he will overpower me and take you. If he asks you, tell him that you are my sister, for you are my sister in Islam. I know of no Muslim upon the earth other than you and me.’
When Abraham entered the ruler’s land, one of the ruler’s men saw Sarah and went to him, saying, ‘A woman has entered your land who is worthy of belonging to no one but you.’
The ruler sent for her, and she was brought to him. Abraham, peace be upon him, stood in prayer.
When she entered before the ruler, he could not restrain himself and reached his hand toward her, but his hand was seized with a severe paralysis.
He said to her, ‘Supplicate to Allah to release my hand, and I will not harm you.’
She supplicated, and his hand was released. He attempted to touch her again, and his hand was seized even more severely than the first time.
He made the same request, and she supplicated for him. He then attempted a third time, and his hand was seized more severely than on the previous two occasions.
He said, ‘Supplicate to Allah to release my hand, and you have Allah’s guarantee that I will not harm you.’
She supplicated, and his hand was released.
He summoned the man who had brought her and said, ‘You brought me a devil, not a human being. Remove her from my land.’ He then gave her Hājar as a servant.
She returned walking. When Abraham, peace be upon him, saw her, he ended his prayer and asked, ‘What happened?’
She replied, ‘All is well. Allah restrained the hand of the wicked man and granted me a servant.’”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“She is your mother, descendants of the water of heaven.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3357, 3358) and Muslim (2371:154). This is Muslim’s wording.

12755 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“Those to whom the people said, ‘The people have gathered against you, so fear them,’ but it only increased them in faith, and they said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.’”
 [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:173]
“Abraham, peace be upon him, said these words when he was cast into the fire. Muḥammad ﷺ also said them when people told him:
‘The people have gathered against you, so fear them,’ but it only increased them in faith, and they said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4563).

12756 — Umm Sharīk narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ commanded that house geckos be killed and said:
“It used to blow upon the fire of Abraham, peace be upon him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3359).

12757 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The frog used to extinguish the fire from Abraham, while the gecko used to blow upon it. Therefore, killing the former was prohibited and killing the latter was commanded.”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (8392). Its chain is authentic.

12758 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Two women were together with their two sons when a wolf came and carried away the son of one of them.
One woman said to the other, ‘The wolf took your son.’
The other replied, ‘Rather, it took your son.’
They brought their dispute before David, and he awarded the surviving child to the older woman.
They then went to Solomon son of David, peace be upon them both, and informed him of the case.
He said, ‘Bring me a knife so that I may divide the child between you.’
The younger woman cried, ‘No! May Allah have mercy upon you. He is her son.’
Solomon then awarded the child to the younger woman.”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“By Allah, I had never heard the word sikkīn for a knife until that day. We used to call it only mudyah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3427) and Muslim (1720:20). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

12759 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
I passed by ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān in the mosque and greeted him with peace. He looked directly at me but did not return my greeting.
I went to the Commander of the Believers, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, and asked him twice:
“Commander of the Believers, has something new occurred in Islam?”
He replied:
“No. Why do you ask?”
I said:
“Nothing, except that I passed by ʿUthmān in the mosque a short while ago. I greeted him, and he looked directly at me but did not return my greeting.”
ʿUmar sent for ʿUthmān and asked him:
“What prevented you from returning your brother’s greeting?”
ʿUthmān replied:
“I did not do that.”
Saʿd said:
“Indeed, you did.”
They both swore to the truth of their statements.
Then ʿUthmān remembered and said:
“Yes. I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him. You passed by me while I was reflecting upon some words I had heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. By Allah, whenever I remember them, a veil overtakes my sight and heart.”
Saʿd said:
“I will tell you those words. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ once began telling us about the foremost supplication, but a Bedouin came and occupied his attention until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood and departed. I followed him.”
When I feared he would reach his home before I could ask him, I stamped my feet upon the ground. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned toward me and asked:
“Who is this? Is it Abū Isḥāq?”
I replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He asked:
“What is the matter?”
I said:
“Nothing, by Allah, except that you began telling us about the foremost supplication, but this Bedouin came and distracted you.”
He replied:
“Yes. It is the supplication of the man of the fish while he was inside the fish:
‘There is no deity worthy of worship except You. Glory be to You. Indeed, I have been among the wrongdoers.’
No Muslim ever supplicates to his Lord with it concerning anything except that He answers him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3505), Aḥmad (1462), whose wording is given here, and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (656).

12760 — Al-Nawwās ibn Samʿān narrated:
One morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke about the Antichrist and related a lengthy hadith, in which he said:
“While Jesus is in that condition, Allah will reveal to him: ‘I have brought forth servants of Mine whom no one has the power to fight. Therefore, take My servants to safety upon Mount Ṭūr.’
Allah will send forth Gog and Magog, and they will rush down from every elevation.
The first of them will pass by the Lake of Tiberias and drink all of its water. The last of them will pass by and say, ‘There was once water here.’
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will be besieged until the head of an ox will be more valuable to one of them than one hundred dinars are to one of you today.
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will supplicate to Allah. Allah will send worms into their necks, and by morning Gog and Magog will all lie dead as though they were a single soul.
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will then descend to the earth, but they will not find a handspan of land that is not filled with their fat and foul stench.
Allah’s Prophet Jesus and his companions will supplicate to Allah. Allah will send birds whose necks resemble those of Bactrian camels. They will carry the bodies away and cast them wherever Allah wills.
Allah will then send rain from which no house of clay or tent of hair will provide shelter. It will wash the earth until it leaves it like a polished mirror.
The earth will then be commanded: ‘Produce your fruit and restore your blessing.’
On that day, an entire group of people will eat from a single pomegranate…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2937).
The hadith clearly establishes that the emergence of Gog and Magog is one of the signs of the Hour. For further details, see the Book of Tribulations.

12761 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“There is a verse in the Book of Allah about which people do not question me. I do not know whether they understand it and therefore do not ask, or whether they are unaware of it and therefore do not ask.”
He was asked:
“Which verse is it?”
He replied:
When the verse was revealed:
“Indeed, you and whatever you worship besides Allah are fuel for Hell. You will enter it,”
this was distressing to the people of Makkah, and they said:
“Muḥammad has insulted our gods.”
Ibn al-Zibaʿrā stood and asked:
“What is troubling you?”
They replied:
“Muḥammad has insulted our gods.”
He asked:
“What did he say?”
They replied:
“He said:
‘Indeed, you and whatever you worship besides Allah are fuel for Hell. You will enter it.’”
He said:
“Call him for me.”
Muḥammad ﷺ was called, and Ibn al-Zibaʿrā asked:
“Muḥammad, does this apply specifically to our gods, or to everyone worshipped besides Allah?”
He replied:
“Rather, it applies to everyone worshipped besides Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Ibn al-Zibaʿrā said:
“By the Lord of this House, we have defeated him in argument.”
He said:
“Muḥammad, do you not claim that Jesus is a righteous servant, Ezra is a righteous servant, and the angels are righteous servants?”
He replied:
“Indeed.”
Ibn al-Zibaʿrā said:
“The Christians worship Jesus, the Jews worship Ezra, and Banū Malīḥ worship the angels.”
The people of Makkah erupted in celebration.
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Indeed, those for whom the best reward has already been decreed from Us”—
meaning Jesus, Ezra, and the angels—
“They will be kept far away from it.”
He said that the following verse was also revealed:
“When the son of Mary was presented as an example, your people immediately raised an outcry over it.”
 [Sūrat al-Zukhruf 43:57]
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2918), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (12/153), and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ Mushkil al-Āthār (986).

12762 — Muḥammad ibn Ḥāṭib said:
I heard ʿAlī delivering a sermon. He recited the verse:
“Indeed, those for whom the best reward has already been decreed from Us…”
He then said:
“It was revealed concerning ʿUthmān and his companions.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah in his Muṣannaf (32715), Aḥmad in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (771), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (16/415), and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ al-Mushkil (3/21).
The word:
“Fuel”
literally means something cast or thrown. Small stones are called ḥaṣbāʾ because they are thrown. Thus, they will be cast into Hell, as Allah says:
“Its fuel is people and stones.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:24]
That is, the disbelievers and their stone idols.
It has also been said that ḥaṣab means firewood in the dialect of the people of Yemen. Some recited it as:
“The firewood of Hell,”
but this is an irregular recitation. Its meaning is the fuel of Hell.
The words:
“You will enter it”
mean that they will go into it.
The words:
“All will remain therein forever”
refer to both the worshippers and those inanimate objects they worshipped, such as stones and trees.

12763 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Emigrants will have pulpits of gold upon which they will sit on the Day of Resurrection, secure from terror.”
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī said:
“Were I to favor anyone with this narration, I would have favored my own people with it.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān in his Ṣaḥīḥ (7262).

12764 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will fold the heavens on the Day of Resurrection, then take them in His right hand and say, ‘I am the King. Where are the tyrants? Where are the arrogant?’
He will then fold the earths in His left hand and say, ‘I am the King. Where are the tyrants? Where are the arrogant?’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7412) and Muslim (2788).
The words:
“As We began the first creation, We will repeat it”
mean that Allah, the Exalted, will recreate creation on the Day of Resurrection just as He created them the first time, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12765 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ delivered a sermon and said:
“You will be gathered before Allah barefoot, naked, and uncircumcised:
‘As We began the first creation, We will repeat it. This is a promise binding upon Us. We will certainly do it.’
The first person to be clothed on the Day of Resurrection will be Abraham.
Men from my nation will be brought and taken toward the left. I will say, ‘My Lord, my Companions!’
It will be said, ‘You do not know what they introduced after you.’
I will say as the righteous servant said:
‘I was a witness over them while I remained among them,’
until the words:
‘You are Witness over all things.’
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:117]
It will be said, ‘They continued turning back upon their heels from the time you left them.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4740) and Muslim (2860:58). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12766 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
It was said:
“O Messenger of Allah, supplicate against the polytheists.”
He replied:
“I was not sent as one who curses. I was sent only as a mercy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2599).

12767 — ʿAmr ibn Abī Qurrah narrated:
Ḥudhayfah was in al-Madāʾin and would mention statements that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had made concerning some of his Companions while angry.
Some of those who heard this from Ḥudhayfah would go to Salmān and report Ḥudhayfah’s statements to him.
Salmān would say:
“Ḥudhayfah knows best what he is saying.”
They would return to Ḥudhayfah and say:
“We mentioned your statement to Salmān, but he neither confirmed nor denied what you said.”
Ḥudhayfah went to Salmān while he was in a vegetable garden and asked:
“Salmān, what prevents you from confirming what I heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
Salmān replied:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would sometimes become angry and say things concerning some of his Companions while angry. He would also be pleased and say things concerning some of his Companions while pleased.”
“Will you not stop until you cause some people to inherit love for certain men and others to inherit hatred for certain men, and until you cause disagreement and division?”
“You know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ delivered a sermon and said:
‘Any man from my nation whom I insult or curse while angry—indeed, I am a descendant of Adam and become angry as human beings become angry, while I was sent only as a mercy to all worlds—then make that statement a blessing for him on the Day of Resurrection.’”
Salmān then said:
“By Allah, you must stop, or I will write to ʿUmar about you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4659), Aḥmad (23706), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (6/318–319).

12768 — ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir said:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, has Sūrat al-Ḥajj been distinguished by containing two prostrations?”
He replied:
“Yes. Whoever does not perform both prostrations should not recite them.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1402), al-Tirmidhī (578), Aḥmad (17412), and al-Ḥākim (2/390).

12769 — Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say, ‘Adam!’
Adam will reply, ‘Here I am at Your service, and all goodness is in Your hands.’
Allah will say, ‘Bring forth the contingent destined for the Fire.’
Adam will ask, ‘What is the contingent destined for the Fire?’
Allah will say, ‘From every thousand, nine hundred and ninety-nine.’
That will be when the young child turns gray-haired:
‘Every pregnant woman will deliver her burden, and you will see the people appearing intoxicated, although they are not intoxicated; rather, the punishment of Allah is severe.’”
This weighed heavily upon the Companions.
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, which one of us will be that single person?”
He replied:
“Receive glad tidings. From Gog and Magog will come one thousand, while from you will come one.”
He then said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, I hope that you will constitute one quarter of the people of Paradise.”
We praised Allah and proclaimed His greatness.
He then said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, I hope that you will constitute one third of the people of Paradise.”
We praised Allah and proclaimed His greatness.
He then said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, I hope that you will constitute half of the people of Paradise.
Your likeness among all nations is that of a single white hair upon the hide of a black bull, or a small mark upon the foreleg of a donkey.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4741) and Muslim (222). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.
The words:
“That will be when the young child turns gray-haired”
correspond to the words of Allah:
“How, then, will you protect yourselves, if you disbelieve, from a Day that will turn children gray-haired?”
 [Sūrat al-Muzzammil 73:17]

12770 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The verses beginning:
“People, be mindful of your Lord. Indeed, the convulsion of the Hour is a tremendous thing,”
until the words:
“But the punishment of Allah is severe,”
were revealed.
They were revealed to the Prophet ﷺ while he was on a journey. He raised his voice while reciting them until his Companions gathered around him.
He said:
“Do you know what Day this is? It is the Day when Allah will say to Adam, ‘Adam, stand and send forth the contingent of the Fire: from every thousand, nine hundred and ninety-nine to the Fire and one to Paradise.’”
This was extremely distressing to the Muslims.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Remain upright, strive to come close to perfection, and receive glad tidings.
By the One in Whose hand is my soul, compared with all humanity, you are only like a small mark upon the side of a camel or a spot upon the foreleg of an animal.
There are two immense groups with you which, whenever added to anything, greatly outnumber it: Gog and Magog, and those who perished among the disbelieving humans and jinn.”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq in his Tafsīr (2/31), whose wording is given here, ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (1185), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7354) and al-Ḥākim (1/29, 4/566).

12771 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You will be gathered barefoot, naked, and uncircumcised.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will the men and women be looking at one another?”
He replied:
“The matter will be far too severe for them to be concerned with that.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6527) and Muslim (2859).

12772 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, appoints an angel over the womb. The angel says, ‘My Lord, a drop. My Lord, a clot. My Lord, a lump of flesh.’
When Allah wills to complete its creation, the angel asks, ‘Male or female? Wretched or blessed? What will be the provision and lifespan?’ All of this is then written while the child is in its mother’s womb.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6595) and Muslim (2646).

12773 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ—the truthful one whose truthfulness is confirmed—told us:
“The creation of each one of you is gathered in his mother’s womb for forty days. He then becomes a clot for a similar period, and then a lump of flesh for a similar period.
Allah then sends an angel who is commanded to write four matters: his deeds, his provision, his lifespan, and whether he will be wretched or blessed.
The soul is then breathed into him…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6594) and Muslim (2643). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12774 — Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“Among the people is one who worships Allah upon an edge”:
“A man would come to Madinah. If his wife gave birth to a son and his horses produced offspring, he would say, ‘This is a good religion.’ But if his wife did not give birth and his horses did not produce offspring, he would say, ‘This is a bad religion.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4742).

12775 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some Bedouins would come to the Prophet ﷺ and embrace Islam.
When they returned to their lands, if they experienced a year of rain, fertility, and healthy births among their livestock, they would say:
“This religion of ours is good,”
and they would hold firmly to it.
But if they experienced a year of drought, poor births, and famine, they would say:
“There is no good in this religion of ours.”
Allah then revealed:
“Among the people is one who worships Allah upon an edge.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr, as cited in Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr (5/400), and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (10/118–119).

12776 — Abū Dharr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ asked one day:
“Do you know where this sun goes?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It travels until it reaches its resting place beneath the Throne, where it falls in prostration. It remains in that state until it is told, ‘Rise and return from where you came.’
It then returns and rises from its usual place of rising.
It continues traveling until it reaches its resting place beneath the Throne, where it falls in prostration. It remains in that state until it is told, ‘Rise and return from where you came.’
It then returns and rises from its usual place of rising.
It will continue traveling without people noticing anything unusual until it reaches that resting place beneath the Throne. It will then be told, ‘Rise and appear from your place of setting.’
It will then rise from the west.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Do you know when that will occur? It will occur when:
‘No soul will benefit from believing at that time if it had not believed before or earned goodness through its faith. Say, “Wait; we too are waiting.”’”
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:158]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3199) and Muslim (159:250). The wording is that of Muslim.
In another wording narrated by Muslim:
“It goes and seeks permission to prostrate, and permission is granted to it. Then it will be as though it is told, ‘Return from where you came,’ and it will rise from the west.”
He then recited according to the reading of ʿAbdullāh:
“That is its appointed resting place.”
 [Sūrat Yā Sīn 36:38]
This verse is one of the verses of prostration. It is stated in the authentic hadith:

12777 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the son of Adam recites a verse of prostration and then prostrates, Satan withdraws, weeping and saying, ‘Woe to me! The son of Adam was commanded to prostrate and he prostrated, so Paradise is his. I was commanded to prostrate but refused, so the Fire is mine.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (81).

12778 — Qays ibn ʿUbād narrated:
I heard Abū Dharr swear emphatically that the verse:
“These are two opposing parties who disputed concerning their Lord”
was revealed concerning those who came forward for single combat on the day of Badr: Ḥamzah, ʿAlī, and ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith, against ʿUtbah and Shaybah, the two sons of Rabīʿah, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4743) and Muslim (3033). The wording is that of Muslim.

12779 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said:
“I will be the first person to kneel before the Most Compassionate for judgment in a dispute on the Day of Resurrection.”
Qays ibn ʿUbād said:
“It was concerning them that the verse was revealed:
‘These are two opposing parties who disputed concerning their Lord.’”
He said:
“They were those who engaged in single combat on the day of Badr: Ḥamzah, ʿAlī, and ʿUbaydah—or Abū ʿUbaydah—ibn al-Ḥārith, against Shaybah ibn Rabīʿah, ʿUtbah, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah.”
In another narration, ʿAlī said:
“This verse was revealed concerning us:
‘These are two opposing parties.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3965). The second narration was also narrated by al-Bukhārī (3967).
The word:
“He kneels”
means that he sits upon his knees while presenting his dispute.
The intended meaning of his being first is restricted to the fighters of this nation, because the mentioned duel was the first single combat to occur in Islam.
Regarding the words:
“Boiling water will be poured over their heads, melting whatever is within their bellies as well as their skins”:

12780 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Boiling water will be poured over their heads. It will penetrate until it reaches the interior of their bodies, causing everything within them to melt and flow out through their feet. This is the melting described in the verse. They will then be restored to their former state.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2582), Aḥmad (8864), and al-Ḥākim (2/387).

12781 — Abū Ḥāzim narrated:
I was standing behind Abū Hurayrah while he was performing ablution for prayer. He would wash his arm until the water reached his armpit. I asked him:
“Abū Hurayrah, what kind of ablution is this?”
He replied:
“Descendants of Farrūkh, are you here? Had I known that you were present, I would not have performed ablution in this manner.”
“I heard the Prophet ﷺ, my beloved friend, say:
‘The adornment of the believer will reach as far as the water of ablution reaches.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (250).
The words:
“Their clothing therein will be silk”
mean that the garments of the people of Paradise will be made of silk, including brocade, fine silk, and similar materials, as Allah says:
“They will wear green garments of fine silk and brocade and will be adorned with bracelets of silver. Their Lord will give them a purifying drink.”
 [Sūrat al-Insān 76:21]
This is reserved for the believers in the Hereafter, and they are not permitted to wear such garments in this world. As for the disbelievers, they wear silk and brocade in this world but will be deprived of them in the Hereafter.

12782 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā narrated:
They were with Ḥudhayfah when he asked for a drink, and a Magian served him. When the man placed the cup in his hand, Ḥudhayfah threw it at him and said:
“Were it not that I had prohibited him from doing this more than once or twice, I would not have acted in this manner.”
He then said:
“But I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
‘Do not wear silk or brocade. Do not drink from vessels of gold and silver, and do not eat from their plates, for they are for them in this world and for us in the Hereafter.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5426) and Muslim (2067).

12783 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The three people most hated by Allah are: one who commits deviation within the Sacred Precinct, one who seeks to revive a practice of the pre-Islamic period within Islam, and one who seeks to shed a person’s blood without right.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6882).

12784 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The first woman to wear a waist sash was the mother of Ishmael. She wore it to conceal her tracks from Sarah.
Abraham brought her and her infant son Ishmael, whom she was nursing, and left them near the House beside a large tree above Zamzam, at the upper part of the Sacred Mosque. At that time, no one lived in Makkah, and there was no water there.
He left them there with a leather bag containing dates and a waterskin containing water. Abraham then turned and departed.
The mother of Ishmael followed him and said:
“Abraham, where are you going, leaving us in this valley where there are neither people nor anything else?”
She repeated this several times, but he did not turn toward her.
She asked:
“Has Allah commanded you to do this?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
She said:
“Then He will not abandon us.”
She then returned.
Abraham continued until he reached the mountain pass where they could no longer see him. He turned toward the House, raised his hands, and supplicated with these words:
“Our Lord, I have settled some of my descendants in an uncultivated valley…”
until the words:
“…so that they may be grateful.”
 [Sūrat Ibrāhīm 14:37]
The mother of Ishmael continued nursing Ishmael and drinking from the water until the waterskin became empty. She grew thirsty, and her son also became thirsty.
She watched him twisting in distress—or rolling upon the ground—but then moved away because she could not bear to look at him.
She found al-Ṣafā to be the nearest hill. She climbed it and looked across the valley to see whether she could find anyone, but she saw no one.
She descended from al-Ṣafā. When she reached the valley, she lifted the edge of her garment and ran like a person in extreme distress until she crossed it.
She then reached al-Marwah, stood upon it, and looked to see whether she could find anyone, but she saw no one.
She did this seven times.
Ibn ʿAbbās said that the Prophet ﷺ stated:
“That is the origin of the people’s walking between them.”
When she climbed al-Marwah, she heard a sound. She said to herself:
“Be quiet,”
and listened carefully. She heard the sound again.
She said:
“You have made yourself heard. Help us if you possess any aid.”
She then saw the angel at the site of Zamzam. He dug with his heel—or with his wing—until water appeared.
She began forming a basin around it with her hands and filling her waterskin, while the water continued gushing after she scooped from it.
Ibn ʿAbbās said that the Prophet ﷺ stated:
“May Allah have mercy upon the mother of Ishmael. Had she left Zamzam alone—or had she not scooped water from it—Zamzam would have become a flowing spring.”
She drank and nursed her child.
The angel said to her:
“Do not fear being abandoned. Here stands the House of Allah, which this boy and his father will build. Allah never abandons those devoted to Him.”
The site of the House was raised above the surrounding ground like a hillock, and floodwater would pass to its right and left.
She remained there until a group from Jurhum—or a household from Jurhum—passed by while approaching from the direction of Kadāʾ.
They camped in the lower part of Makkah and saw a bird circling. They said:
“This bird must be circling over water, although we have long known this valley to contain none.”
They sent one or two scouts, who discovered the water and returned to inform them.
They came to her while she was beside the water and asked:
“Will you permit us to settle near you?”
She replied:
“Yes, but you will have no ownership over the water.”
They agreed.
Ibn ʿAbbās said that the Prophet ﷺ stated:
“This pleased the mother of Ishmael, for she loved human company.”
They settled there and sent for their families, who joined them, until several households from among them were established there.
The boy grew up and learned Arabic from them. As he matured, they admired him and valued his company.
When he reached maturity, they married him to a woman from among them. The mother of Ishmael later died.
After Ishmael had married, Abraham came to inspect those he had left behind. He did not find Ishmael, so he asked his wife about him.
She replied:
“He has gone out to seek provision for us.”
He asked her about their condition and way of life. She replied:
“We are in misery, difficulty, and hardship,”
and complained to him.
He said:
“When your husband returns, convey my greeting of peace to him and tell him to change the threshold of his door.”
When Ishmael returned, he sensed that someone had visited and asked:
“Did anyone come to you?”
She replied:
“Yes. An old man of such-and-such a description came. He asked about you, and I informed him. He asked about our living conditions, and I told him that we live in hardship and distress.”
He asked:
“Did he give you any instruction?”
She replied:
“Yes. He told me to convey his greeting of peace to you and to say: ‘Change the threshold of your door.’”
Ishmael said:
“That was my father. He has instructed me to separate from you, so return to your family.”
He divorced her and married another woman from among them.
Abraham remained away for as long as Allah willed and later returned. He did not find Ishmael, so he entered upon his wife and asked about him.
She replied:
“He has gone out to seek provision for us.”
He asked:
“How are you?”
and questioned her about their living conditions.
She replied:
“We are well and live in abundance,”
and praised Allah.
He asked:
“What food do you eat?”
She replied:
“Meat.”
He asked:
“What do you drink?”
She replied:
“Water.”
He supplicated:
“O Allah, bless their meat and water.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“At that time they possessed no grain. Had they possessed it, Abraham would also have prayed for blessing in it.”
The narrator said:
“No one outside Makkah depends exclusively upon meat and water without suffering from them.”
Abraham said:
“When your husband returns, convey my greeting of peace to him and instruct him to keep the threshold of his door.”
When Ishmael returned, he asked:
“Did anyone visit you?”
She replied:
“Yes. A dignified old man visited us.”
She praised him and said:
“He asked about you, and I informed him. He asked about our living conditions, and I told him that we were well.”
He asked:
“Did he give you any instruction?”
She replied:
“Yes. He conveys his greeting of peace to you and commands you to keep the threshold of your door.”
Ishmael said:
“That was my father, and you are the threshold. He has instructed me to keep you.”
Abraham remained away for as long as Allah willed. He later returned and found Ishmael sharpening arrows beneath a large tree near Zamzam.
When Ishmael saw him, he stood to greet him, and they embraced as a father and son do.
Abraham said:
“Ishmael, Allah has commanded me to do something.”
He replied:
“Then do what your Lord has commanded you.”
Abraham asked:
“Will you help me?”
He replied:
“I will help you.”
Abraham said:
“Allah has commanded me to build a House here,”
and pointed toward a mound elevated above its surroundings.
They then raised the foundations of the House. Ishmael brought the stones while Abraham built.
When the structure became high, Ishmael brought the stone and placed it for him. Abraham stood upon it while building, and Ishmael continued handing him stones.
They both said:
“Our Lord, accept this from us. Indeed, You are the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:127]
They continued building while moving around the House and repeating:
“Our Lord, accept this from us. Indeed, You are the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3364).

12785 — Abū Dharr narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, which mosque was established first upon the earth?”
He replied:
“The Sacred Mosque.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“Al-Masjid al-Aqṣā.”
I asked:
“How much time was there between them?”
He replied:
“Forty years. Thereafter, wherever prayer reaches you, perform it there, for there is virtue in doing so.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3366) and Muslim (520).
The words:
“Purify My House”
mean: cleanse it from polytheism, sins, physical impurities, and all defilement, and reserve it for those mentioned in the verse: those who circumambulate, stand in prayer, bow, and prostrate.
When Quraysh introduced the worship of beings besides Allah into this House and erected idols within it—including images of Abraham and Ishmael, peace be upon them—the Prophet ﷺ purified the House from the worship of anything besides Allah and restored it to the exclusive worship of Allah for those who circumambulate, stand, bow, and prostrate.
Through this, Allah completed the covenant He had taken from Abraham, peace be upon him.

12786 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
“I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ mount his riding animal at Dhū al-Ḥulayfah. He then pronounced the talbiyah after it stood upright carrying him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1514) and Muslim (1187:29). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12787 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated in the lengthy hadith describing the Prophet’s pilgrimage:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed in the mosque and then mounted al-Qaṣwāʾ. When his she-camel reached the open plain of al-Baydāʾ and stood upright beneath him, I looked as far as my eyes could see. In front of him were riders and people walking; the same was true to his right, to his left, and behind him…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).

12788 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No righteous deeds performed during any days are superior to deeds performed during these ten days.”
They asked:
“Not even striving in Allah’s path?”
He replied:
“Not even striving in Allah’s path, except for a man who goes out risking both his life and wealth and returns with neither.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (969).

12789 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are no days greater in the sight of Allah, nor days during which righteous deeds are more beloved to Him, than these days. Therefore, frequently proclaim Allah’s oneness and praise Him during them.”
He meant the first ten days of Dhū al-Ḥijjah.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū ʿAwānah in his Musnad (3024).
The words:
“Eat from them”
mean: eat from the sacrificial animals offered during pilgrimage and from the festival sacrifices.
It is authentically established that after the Prophet ﷺ slaughtered his sacrificial animals, he ordered that a piece be taken from each camel and cooked. He then ate from the meat and drank from its broth.

12790 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ then went to the place of sacrifice and slaughtered sixty-three camels with his own hand. He then gave the remainder to ʿAlī, who slaughtered them, and he included ʿAlī in his sacrificial offering.
He then ordered that a piece be taken from every camel. The pieces were placed in a pot and cooked, and the two of them ate from the meat and drank from its broth…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).

12791 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ initially prohibited eating the meat of sacrificial animals after three days. He later said:
“Eat from it, give some of it in charity, take some as provisions, and store some.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (1046) and Muslim (1972).

12792 — ʿAlī narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ sent me to supervise the sacrificial camels. He commanded me to distribute their meat, and then commanded me to distribute their coverings and skins.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1716) and Muslim (1317).
The words:
“The miserable poor person”
refer to a destitute person who possesses nothing. Al-Buʾs means extreme poverty and hardship.

12793 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“O Messenger of Allah, Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy has begun menstruating.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Perhaps she will delay our departure. Did she not perform ṭawāf with you?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Then depart.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (226), al-Bukhārī (328, 1561), and Muslim (1211:128).

12794 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“The Ḥijr is part of the House, because the Messenger of Allah ﷺ circumambulated the House from beyond it, and Allah said:
‘Let them circumambulate the Ancient House.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Khuzaymah (2740), al-Ḥākim (1/460), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (5/90).

12795 — Abū Bakrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ asked three times:
“Shall I inform you of the gravest major sins?”
They replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Associating partners with Allah and disobedience toward one’s parents.”
He had been reclining, but then sat upright and said:
“And beware of false speech.”
He continued repeating it until we thought:
“If only he would stop.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2654) and Muslim (87).

12796 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever does not abandon false speech, acting upon it, and ignorant behavior, Allah has no need for him to abandon his food and drink.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6057).

12797 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
We went out with the Prophet ﷺ for the funeral of a man from the Anṣār. He related a lengthy hadith in which he described the soul of the disbeliever:
“The angels ascend with it. Whenever they pass by a gathering of angels, they ask, ‘What is this foul soul?’
They answer, ‘So-and-so son of so-and-so,’ calling him by the ugliest names by which he was known in the world.
They continue until they reach the lowest heaven and request that its gate be opened for him, but it is not opened.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then recited:
“Indeed, those who denied Our signs and arrogantly rejected them—the gates of heaven will not be opened for them, nor will they enter Paradise until a camel passes through the eye of a needle. Thus do We recompense the criminals.”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:40]
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say, ‘Record his book in Sijjīn in the lowest earth,’ and his soul will be cast down violently.”
He then recited:
“Whoever associates partners with Allah is as though he had fallen from the sky and been snatched away by birds, or swept by the wind into a distant place.”
“His soul is then returned to his body. Two angels come to him, make him sit, and ask him, ‘Who is your Lord?’…”
The hadith continues.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4753), Imām Aḥmad (18534), whose wording is given here, and Hannād ibn al-Sarī in al-Zuhd (339).

12798 — Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ saw a man leading a sacrificial camel and said:
“Ride it.”
The man replied:
“It is a sacrificial camel.”
He said:
“Ride it.”
The man repeated:
“It is a sacrificial camel.”
He said three times:
“Ride it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1690) and Muslim (1323:373).

12799 — Abū al-Zubayr narrated:
I heard Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh being asked about riding a sacrificial animal. He replied:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Ride it in a reasonable manner when necessity compels you, until you find another mount.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1324).
The words:
“Until an appointed time”
mean until the predetermined time when the animals reach Makkah and are slaughtered in Minā or another legally designated place.

12800 — Anas narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ sacrificed two black-and-white rams. I saw him place his foot upon the sides of their necks, pronounce Allah’s name and glorify Him, and slaughter them with his own hand.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5558) and Muslim (1966:18). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12801 — Anas narrated:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed four units of the noon prayer with us in Madinah and two units of the afternoon prayer at Dhū al-Ḥulayfah. He spent the night there until morning and then mounted his riding animal until it stood with him upon al-Baydāʾ…”
The narration also states:
“The Prophet ﷺ slaughtered sacrificial camels with his own hand while they were standing.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1551) and Muslim (690).

12802 — Ziyād ibn Jubayr narrated:
Ibn ʿUmar passed by a man who was slaughtering his sacrificial camel while it was kneeling. He said:
“Make it stand while one of its legs is tied. That is the Sunnah of your Prophet ﷺ.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1713) and Muslim (1320).

12803 — Jābir narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ and his Companions used to slaughter sacrificial camels while their left foreleg was tied and they stood upon their remaining legs.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1767).
The words:
“When they fall upon their sides, eat from them”
mean after they have fallen to the ground, which is an expression indicating that the soul has left the body.
It is not permissible to eat from a sacrificial camel immediately after it has been slaughtered until it has died and all movement has ceased. If life remains within it, nothing may be cut from it or eaten, because of the following hadith:

12804 — Abū Wāqid al-Laythī narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Madinah, the people used to cut off the humps of living camels and the fat tails of living sheep. He said:
“Whatever is cut from an animal while it is alive is carrion.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2858), al-Tirmidhī (1480), whose wording is given here, Aḥmad (21903), and Ibn al-Jārūd (876).
Regarding the words of Allah:
“Eat from them and feed the needy who asks and the one who does not ask”:
The word al-qāniʿ comes from the verb qanaʿa, with a fatḥah on the letter nūn, meaning to ask humbly and submissively.
The verb may also mean to be content and satisfied. Some poets said:
The slave is free when he is content, and the free man is enslaved when he begs. Be content, but do not beg, for nothing disgraces a person except greed.
The first interpretation is stronger because al-muʿtarr is mentioned alongside it. Al-Muʿtarr is one who presents himself where charity is distributed without explicitly asking. He indicates his need indirectly and attends the place of giving, but does not request anything verbally.
The earlier verse states:
“Eat from them and feed the miserable poor person.”
The command in the words:
“Feed the needy who asks and the one who does not ask”
apparently indicates obligation. This is also suggested by the Prophet’s practice when he sacrificed one hundred camels in Minā, ate from them, and instructed his Companions to eat. This was the view of al-Shāfiʿī.
The majority of scholars held that feeding others from the sacrifice is recommended rather than obligatory. However, they considered it disliked for a person to consume all of it without giving any of it in charity.
Some scholars recommended dividing the meat of the sacrifice into three portions: one for the person offering the sacrifice and his family, one to be given as gifts to relatives and friends, and one to be distributed among the poor and needy.
In the two authentic collections, Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Eat, feed others, and store some.”
In al-Bayhaqī (9/297), the wording states:
“And distribute it among the people.”
The stronger view is that the matter is broad and flexible. The greater the amount given in charity, the better.
See the Book of Sacrifices.

12805 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah does not look at your appearances or your wealth. Rather, He looks at your hearts and your deeds.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2564:34).

12806 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ was expelled from Makkah, Abū Bakr said:
“They have expelled their Prophet. Indeed, we belong to Allah and to Him we shall return. They will surely be destroyed.”
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Permission to fight has been granted to those who are being fought because they were wronged, and Allah is certainly able to grant them victory.”
Abū Bakr said:
“I then knew that fighting would take place.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“This was the first verse revealed permitting fighting.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3085), whose wording is given here, al-Tirmidhī (3171), Aḥmad (1865), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4710).

12807 — Al-Zuhrī said:
This was the first verse revealed concerning fighting, as ʿUrwah informed me from ʿĀʾishah:
“Permission to fight has been granted to those who are being fought because they were wronged, and Allah is certainly able to grant them victory,”
until the words:
“Indeed, Allah is All-Strong, Almighty.”
Thereafter, permission to fight was granted in many other verses of the Qur’an.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11283).
The word:
“Monasteries”
is the plural of ṣawmaʿah. It refers to a raised structure reached by stairs, which monks used as a place of worship while living in seclusion from people, whether they belonged to the People of the Book or the Magians.
Today, the word is also used for structures in which grain is stored and processed, such as grain silos.
The word:
“Churches”
is the plural of bīʿah and refers to Christian churches.
The word:
“Synagogues”
is the plural of ṣalāh and is said to derive from the Syriac word ṣalūthā, referring to Jewish houses of worship.
The word:
“Mosques”
refers specifically to the places of worship of Muslims.
Regarding the words:
“In which the name of Allah is mentioned frequently”:
The pronoun in:
“Within them”
refers to all of these places of worship, because the mention of Allah, Glorified and Exalted, was shared among all of these communities, although some of them associated partners with Him.
For this reason, Allah specifically reserved mosques for His worship alone, saying:
“The mosques belong to Allah, so do not invoke anyone along with Allah.”
 [Sūrat al-Jinn 72:18]

12808 — ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“The first thing Allah created was the Pen. He said to it, ‘Write.’
It asked, ‘My Lord, what shall I write?’
He said, ‘Write the decrees of everything until the Hour is established.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4700).

12809 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ‘Who is more unjust than one who attempts to create like My creation? Let them create an ant, a grain, or a kernel of barley.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5953) and Muslim (2111). The wording is that of Muslim.

12810 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said on that day:
“Let the Jews know that there is ease and broadness in our religion. I was sent with a tolerant and upright religion.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (24855).

12811 — Anas narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Make matters easy and do not make them difficult. Give glad tidings and do not drive people away.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (69) and Muslim (1734). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12812 — Abū Mūsā narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent one of his Companions on a mission, he would say:
“Give glad tidings and do not drive people away. Make matters easy and do not make them difficult.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1732).

12813 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ sent Muʿādh and Abū Mūsā to Yemen and instructed them:
“Make matters easy and do not make them difficult. Give glad tidings and do not drive people away. Cooperate with one another and do not disagree.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3038) and Muslim (1733).
Regarding the words:
“He named you Muslims before and in this revelation”:

12814 — Zayd ibn Sallām narrated that al-Ḥārith al-Ashʿarī informed him that the Prophet ﷺ said in a lengthy hadith:
“I command you to observe five matters that Allah commanded me to observe: hearing and obeying, striving in Allah’s cause, emigration, and remaining with the united Muslim community.
Whoever separates from the united community by even a handspan has removed the bond of Islam from his neck unless he returns.
Whoever calls with the slogans and loyalties of the pre-Islamic period will be among those who kneel in Hell.”
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, even if he prays and fasts?”
He replied:
“Even if he prays and fasts. Therefore, call yourselves by the designation Allah has given you: Muslims, believers, and servants of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2863) and Aḥmad (17170), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1895) and Ibn Ḥibbān (6233).

12815 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Sāʾib narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ led us in the dawn prayer in Makkah and began reciting Sūrat al-Muʾminūn. When he reached the mention of Moses and Aaron—or the mention of Jesus; Muḥammad ibn ʿAbbād was uncertain, or the narrators differed—the Prophet ﷺ was overcome by a cough, so he bowed.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (455).

12816 — Yazīd ibn Bābnūs narrated:
We asked ʿĀʾishah:
“Mother of the Believers, what was the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ like?”
She replied:
“The character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was the Qur’an.”
She then recited:
“Successful indeed are the believers,”
until she reached:
“And those who carefully maintain their prayers.”
She said:
“Such was the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11287) and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (308).
The words:
“They carefully maintain their prayers”
mean that they consistently perform them at their appointed times. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says:
“Indeed, prayer has been prescribed for the believers at appointed times.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:103]
Performing prayer at its proper time is among the deeds most beloved to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12817 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Which deed is most beloved to Allah?”
He replied:
“Prayer performed at its proper time.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“Then dutifulness toward one’s parents.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“Striving in the path of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (527), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (85).

12818 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every one of you has two dwellings: one in Paradise and one in the Fire. If a person dies and enters the Fire, the people of Paradise inherit his dwelling. This is the meaning of Allah’s words:
‘Those are the inheritors.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4341) and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (373).
Al-Firdaws is the highest, central, and finest part of Paradise, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12819 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever believes in Allah and His Messenger, establishes prayer, and fasts Ramadan has a promise from Allah that He will admit him into Paradise, whether he strives in the path of Allah or remains in the land where he was born.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should we not give the people this glad news?”
He replied:
“Paradise contains one hundred levels that Allah has prepared for those who strive in His path. The distance between every two levels is like the distance between heaven and earth. When you ask Allah, ask Him for al-Firdaws, for it is the central and highest part of Paradise. I believe that above it is the Throne of the Most Compassionate, and from it the rivers of Paradise spring forth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2790).

12820 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated in a lengthy hadith:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ consulted Abū Bakr and ʿUmar concerning the prisoners.
Abū Bakr said:
“O Messenger of Allah, spare your people and accept ransom from them, so that you may use it for assistance.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“Execute them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Had the two of you agreed, we would not have opposed you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ adopted Abū Bakr’s opinion, so Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“It is not fitting for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land. You desire the temporary gains of this world, while Allah desires the Hereafter. Allah is Almighty, All-Wise.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:67]
Ibn ʿAbbās continued:
Then the verses beginning:
“We certainly created the human being from an extract of clay,”
were revealed until their conclusion.
ʿUmar said:
“Blessed is Allah, the best of creators.”
Then the words were revealed:
“Blessed is Allah, the best of creators.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/438–439) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/288).
The words:
“We certainly created the human being from an extract of clay”
refer to the father of humanity, Adam, peace be upon him, who was created from clay, as stated in the hadith:

12821 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah created Adam from a handful taken from all parts of the earth. The children of Adam therefore came according to the nature of the earth: among them are the red, white, black, and those of intermediate colors; the gentle and harsh; and the wicked and good.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4693), al-Tirmidhī (2958), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (9/3).

12822 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“People must stop boasting about their deceased forefathers, who are merely charcoal in Hell, or else they will become more insignificant before Allah than the beetle that rolls dung with its nose. Allah has removed from you the arrogance of the pre-Islamic period and its boasting about ancestry. A person is only either a pious believer or a wretched sinner. All people are the children of Adam, and Adam was created from dust.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3955) and Aḥmad (10781).
Regarding the words:
“Then We placed him as a drop in a secure resting place. Then We made the drop into a clinging clot, and made the clot into a lump of flesh, and made the lump into bones, and clothed the bones with flesh. Then We developed him into another creation. Blessed is Allah, the best of creators”:

12823 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ—the truthful one whose truthfulness is confirmed—told us:
“The creation of each one of you is gathered in his mother’s womb for forty days. He then becomes a clinging clot for a similar period and then a lump of flesh for a similar period. Allah then sends an angel who is commanded to write four matters: his deeds, provision, lifespan, and whether he will be wretched or blessed. The soul is then breathed into him.
A person among you may continue performing deeds until only a cubit remains between him and Paradise, but what has been written overtakes him, and he performs the deeds of the people of the Fire. Another may continue performing deeds until only a cubit remains between him and the Fire, but what has been written overtakes him, and he performs the deeds of the people of Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3208) and Muslim (2643).

12824 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Between the two trumpet blasts will be forty.”
They asked Abū Hurayrah:
“Forty days?”
He replied:
“I refuse to specify.”
They asked:
“Forty years?”
He replied:
“I refuse to specify.”
They asked:
“Forty months?”
He replied:
“I refuse to specify.”
The Prophet ﷺ continued:
“Every part of the human being will decay except the tailbone, from which creation will be reconstructed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4814) and Muslim (2955). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Muslim added at the beginning of the hadith:
“Allah will then send water down from the sky, and they will grow as vegetation grows. Every part of the human being will decay except one bone: the tailbone.”
In another narration recorded by Muslim:
“There is a bone in the human being that the earth will never consume. From it, he will be reconstructed on the Day of Resurrection.”
They asked:
“Which bone is it, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The tailbone.”
The word al-ʿajb, with a sukūn on the letter jīm, refers to the bone at the lower end of the spine near the base of the back.

12825 — ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Eat olive oil and apply it to your bodies, for it comes from a blessed tree.”
In another wording:
“Use olive oil as a condiment.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1851), Ibn Mājah (3319), al-Ḥākim (4/123), and al-Ḍiyāʾ al-Maqdisī in al-Mukhtārah (82, 83).

12826 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“People, Allah is pure and accepts only what is pure. Allah has commanded the believers with the same command He gave the messengers. He said:
‘Messengers, eat from the wholesome things and perform righteous deeds. I am fully aware of what you do.’
He also said:
‘Believers, eat from the wholesome things We have provided for you and give thanks to Allah, if it is truly Him that you worship.’”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:172]
He then mentioned:
“A man who travels for a long time, disheveled and covered in dust, raising his hands toward the sky and saying, ‘My Lord! My Lord!’ Yet his food is unlawful, his drink is unlawful, his clothing is unlawful, and he has been nourished with what is unlawful. How, then, can his supplication be answered?”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1015).

12827 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Muḥammad, I appeal to you by Allah and by the bonds of kinship. We have been reduced to eating ʿilhiz”—meaning camel hair mixed with blood.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“We had already seized them with punishment, but they neither humbled themselves before their Lord nor pleaded submissively.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11289), Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (17/93), Ibn Ḥibbān (967), and al-Ḥākim (2/394).

12828 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I believe Allah, the Exalted, said: ‘The son of Adam insults Me, although he has no right to insult Me, and he denies Me, although he has no right to deny Me. His insult is his statement that I have a child, and his denial is his statement that I will not recreate him as I created him the first time.’”
In another narration:
“The first creation is no easier for Me than recreating it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3193). The second narration is also recorded by him (4974).

12829 — Muʿādh ibn Jabal narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said in a lengthy hadith:
“O Allah, I ask You for the performance of good deeds, the abandonment of evil deeds, love for the poor, and that You forgive me and have mercy upon me. If You intend a trial for a people, then cause me to die without being put to trial. I ask You for Your love, the love of those who love You, and the love of every deed that brings me closer to Your love.”
