Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3235), whose wording is given here, Aḥmad (22109), and Ibn Khuzaymah in Kitāb al-Tawḥīd (441).

12830 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that when the Prophet ﷺ entered the mosque, he would say:
“I seek refuge in Allah the Magnificent, in His noble Face, and in His eternal authority from Satan the accursed.”
The narrator asked:
“Is that all?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“When a person says that, Satan says, ‘He has been protected from me for the remainder of the day.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (466).

12831 — Abū al-Yusr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to supplicate:
“O Allah, I seek refuge in You from being crushed beneath a collapsing structure. I seek refuge in You from falling from a height. I seek refuge in You from drowning, burning, and extreme old age. I seek refuge in You from Satan overwhelming me at the time of death. I seek refuge in You from dying while retreating from Your path, and I seek refuge in You from dying from a venomous bite.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1552–1553), al-Nasāʾī (5531–5532), and Aḥmad (15523).

12832 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the following verse was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Warn your closest relatives,”
 [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:214]
he said:
“People of Quraysh, save yourselves from Allah’s punishment, for I cannot benefit you at all before Allah. Children of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, I cannot benefit you at all before Allah. ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, I cannot benefit you at all before Allah. Ṣafiyyah, aunt of the Messenger of Allah, I cannot benefit you at all before Allah. Fāṭimah, daughter of the Messenger of Allah, ask me for whatever you wish from my wealth, but I cannot benefit you at all before Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2753) and Muslim (206).

12833 — Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the verse:
“Warn your closest relatives,”
 [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:214]
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned Quraysh. They gathered, and he addressed them both generally and specifically, saying:
“Children of Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy, save yourselves from the Fire. Children of Murrah ibn Kaʿb, save yourselves from the Fire. Children of ʿAbd Shams, save yourselves from the Fire. Children of ʿAbd Manāf, save yourselves from the Fire. Fāṭimah, save yourself from the Fire, for I possess no power to protect you from Allah. I only have ties of kinship with you, which I will continue to maintain properly.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (204).
The phrase:
“I will moisten it with its proper moisture”
is an idiomatic expression meaning that he would maintain the ties of kinship in this world. However, he could not protect them from Allah’s judgment. This explanation was given by al-Suyūṭī.

12834 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever is held back by his deeds will not be advanced by his lineage.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2699).

12835 — Ḥanash ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
A man suffering from an affliction was brought to Ibn Masʿūd. He recited into the man’s ear:
“Did you think that We created you without purpose and that you would not be returned to Us? Exalted is Allah, the True King. There is no deity worthy of worship except Him, Lord of the Noble Throne,”
and continued until the end of the sūrah. The man recovered.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What did you recite into his ear?”
He informed him, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if a man possessing certainty recited these verses over a mountain, it would move from its place.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2513).

12836 — Ibrāhīm ibn al-Ḥārith al-Taymī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dispatched us on a military expedition and instructed us to recite in the evening and morning:
“Did you think that We created you without purpose?”
We recited it, gained spoils, and returned safely.
Good: Narrated by Abū Nuʿaym in Maʿrifat al-Ṣaḥābah (728).

12837 — Abū Hurayrah and Zayd ibn Khālid al-Juhanī narrated that two men brought a dispute before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. One of them said:
“O Messenger of Allah, judge between us according to the Book of Allah.”
The other, who was the more knowledgeable of the two, said:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah. Judge between us according to the Book of Allah, and permit me to speak.”
He said:
“Speak.”
The man said:
“My son was employed as a laborer by this man and committed adultery with his wife. I was told that my son was liable to be stoned, so I ransomed him with one hundred sheep and a slave girl of mine.
I then asked the people of knowledge, and they informed me that my son was liable to one hundred lashes and exile for one year, while the man’s wife was liable to stoning.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, I will certainly judge between you according to the Book of Allah. Your sheep and slave girl must be returned to you.”
He ordered that the man’s son be given one hundred lashes and exiled for one year. He then commanded Unays al-Aslamī to go to the other man’s wife, saying:
“If she confesses, stone her.”
She confessed, so he stoned her.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (6), al-Bukhārī (6842, 6843), and Muslim (1697, 1698).

12838 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated:
There was a man named Marthad ibn Abī Marthad who used to carry Muslim captives out of Makkah and bring them to Madinah.
There was also a prostitute in Makkah named ʿAnāq who had previously been his intimate companion. Marthad had promised one of the Muslim captives in Makkah that he would carry him to safety.
He said:
“I arrived beneath the shadow of a wall in Makkah on a moonlit night. ʿAnāq came and noticed the dark outline of my shadow beside the wall. When she approached, she recognized me and asked, ‘Marthad?’
I replied, ‘Marthad.’
She said, ‘Welcome! Come and spend the night with us.’
I replied, ‘Allah has forbidden fornication.’
She cried out, ‘People of the tents! This man is carrying away your captives.’
Eight men pursued me. I took the route through al-Khandamah and reached a cave or hollow, which I entered.
They came and stood directly above me. They urinated, and their urine fell upon my head, but Allah blinded them to my presence.
They then returned. I went back to my companion and carried him. He was a heavy man. When I reached the area of the idhkhir grass, I removed his chains. I continued carrying him while he assisted me until I reached Madinah.
I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, may I marry ʿAnāq?’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained silent and gave him no answer until the following verse was revealed:
“The fornicator does not marry except a female fornicator or a polytheist, and the female fornicator is not married except by a fornicator or a polytheist. That has been forbidden to the believers.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Marthad, the fornicator does not marry except a female fornicator or a polytheist, and the female fornicator is not married except by a fornicator or a polytheist. Therefore, do not marry her.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2051), al-Tirmidhī (3177), whose wording is given here, al-Nasāʾī (3228), and al-Ḥākim (2/166).

12839 — Shuʿbah, the freedman of Ibn ʿAbbās, narrated:
I heard a man ask Ibn ʿAbbās:
“I used to have relations with a woman and committed with her what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, had forbidden me. Allah then granted me repentance from that, and I wished to marry her.
Some people said to me, ‘The fornicator does not marry except a female fornicator.’”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“That verse does not refer to this situation. Marry her, and if there is any sin in that, let it be upon me.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah in al-Muṣannaf (17200), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (17/153), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2521).

12840 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A fornicator who has received the prescribed punishment should not marry anyone except someone like him.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2052), Aḥmad (8300), al-Ḥākim (2/166), and through him al-Bayhaqī (7/156).
Imām Aḥmad said:
“The marriage contract of a chaste man to an unrepentant prostitute is invalid for as long as she remains in that condition. She must be called to repentance. If she repents, the marriage contract becomes valid; otherwise, it does not.
Likewise, a free and chaste woman should not be married to an openly immoral fornicator until he makes sincere repentance. This is because Allah says:
‘That has been forbidden to the believers.’”
Ibn Kathīr mentioned this in his commentary.

12841 — Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
ʿUwaymir came to ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAdī, the chief of Banū ʿAjlān, and asked:
“What do you say about a man who finds another man with his wife? Should he kill him and then be executed for killing him, or what should he do? Ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about this for me.”
ʿĀṣim went to the Prophet ﷺ and began asking him about it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ disliked and censured such questions.
ʿUwaymir said:
“By Allah, I will not stop until I personally ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about it.”
He came and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, if a man finds another man with his wife, should he kill him and then be executed, or what should he do?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Allah has revealed Qur’an concerning you and your wife.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commanded them to perform the mutual imprecation prescribed by Allah in His Book, and he performed mutual imprecation with her.
He then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, if I retain her as my wife after this, I will have wronged her.”
He therefore divorced her. This became the established practice for all spouses who subsequently performed mutual imprecation.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Observe the child. If she gives birth to a dark-skinned child with intensely black, wide eyes, large buttocks, and full calves, then I believe that ʿUwaymir spoke truthfully concerning her. But if she gives birth to a reddish child resembling a small lizard, then I believe that ʿUwaymir lied concerning her.”
She gave birth to a child matching the description which indicated that ʿUwaymir had spoken truthfully. Thereafter, the child was attributed to his mother.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4745) and Muslim (1492). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Al-Bukhārī added in another narration (4746):
“She was pregnant, but the husband denied that the pregnancy was his. Her son was therefore attributed to her. The established rule of inheritance was that he would inherit from her, and she would inherit from him the share Allah had prescribed for her.”

12842 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Hilāl ibn Umayyah accused his wife before the Prophet ﷺ of adultery with Sharīk ibn Saḥmāʾ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Produce evidence, or the prescribed punishment will be applied to your back.”
Hilāl asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, when one of us sees a man with his wife, must he leave to search for evidence?”
The Prophet ﷺ continued saying:
“Produce evidence, or the prescribed punishment will be applied to your back.”
Hilāl said:
“By the One Who sent you with the truth, I am certainly truthful. Allah will surely reveal something that will protect my back from the punishment.”
Gabriel descended and revealed:
“Those who accuse their wives…”
The Prophet ﷺ recited the verses until he reached:
“If he is among the truthful.”
The Prophet ﷺ then left and summoned the woman. Hilāl came and gave his testimony while the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah knows that one of you is lying. Will either of you repent?”
The woman then stood and gave her testimony. When she reached the fifth oath, the people stopped her and said:
“This oath will make the punishment inevitable.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“She hesitated and stepped backward until we thought that she would turn back from completing the oath.
She then said, ‘I will not disgrace my people for the rest of time,’ and proceeded with the oath.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Observe her child. If she gives birth to one with darkened eyes, full buttocks, and thick calves, then he belongs to Sharīk ibn Saḥmāʾ.”
She gave birth to a child matching that description.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Were it not for what has already been revealed in the Book of Allah, I would have dealt with her severely.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4747).
